# module_tests — the scenario harnesses, what each drives, and what none of them covers

> The document `.agents/conventions/common/scenario-tests.md` asks every repository for: one
> harness that drives the product the way its users drive it, written in the product's own language,
> kept in git, and **described here — including the flows it does not cover.**
>
> This repository had nine harnesses and no document. The rule's second half is the one that bites:
> *owning a harness is not enough — it has to be named, catalogued and run.* Four of these were run
> by whoever remembered them, and the day this file was written one of them had been broken for long
> enough that nobody could say when.
>
> Unit tests are governed by [testing.md](../.agents/conventions/common/testing.md) and are not
> catalogued here. What is here is the tier that starts a real process.

## The tiers

| Tier | Where | Count | What it proves |
|---|---|---|---|
| Unit, extension | `src_vs_code/src/test/*.test.ts`, node:test | 7,746 (37 skipped, 7,709 passing; 2026-11-11, on the branch of E2 of `PLAN_agent_refusal_names_unlisted_entry.md`, rule B; 7,644 on the branch of E1 before it) | logic, in-process, `vscode` stubbed; the busy-terminal guard (`terminalActivity`, `busyTerminalGuard`, `launcherBusyTerminal` suites) is under *The green arrow never types into a busy terminal* |
| Unit, .NET | `src_minimalapi_server/tests`, `src_cli/tests`, `src_mcp/tests`, `src_broker_client/tests`, `src_service_defaults/tests` — xUnit | 1,933 (2026-11-07, after E2.3 S5 and its fix round; 1,351 on 2026-10-14, after E6; totals with skips, platform-specific process tests skipped off their platform) | server 830, cli 156, mcp 177, broker 164, service defaults 606 (81 on 2026-10-14; 569 after E2.3 S1, 596 after S2; server 825 before S4, 828 after it). Two `ParentWatchProcessTests` of the mcp suite fail on a WSL machine, before E2.3 as well (the host looks like WSL and `creds-mcp.exe` cannot start). The cli and mcp suites include PROCESS tests since E1 of PLAN_wsl_bridge_outlives_its_client: the built binary run with a marker in argv, environment and protocol bodies, its log file grepped for it after positive controls (`ServingLogTests`, `RelayLogTests`; helper `src_service_defaults/tests/Support/HostProcess.cs`, linked into both). E2 added the lifetime ones: the built `creds-mcp` given a captured Claude Code 2.1.289 session start (`src_mcp/tests/fixtures/`) must exit within 10 s of stdin EOF with a `subscriptions/listen` open (`ServerEndsWithClientTests`, positive control the 2025-06-18 handshake); a stub broker holding a POST must see the connection close before `drain + deadline` with no forced exit (`ToolCancellationTests`, `Support/StubBroker.cs`); the server started through `cmd /c` / `sh -c` must exit when that intermediary is killed, and must not when relayed or switched off (`ParentWatchProcessTests`). Each has an in-process twin (`ServeOnTests`, `ServerLifetimeTests`, `ParentWatchTests`) for the coverage tool. E3 added the child holders' tier: the built `creds-mcp` on the pump path against a script standing in for the Windows half — SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP each end it with 128 + n and the child dead, after a `SigIgn` check from `/proc` (`WslPumpHostTests`); the built `creds relay` serving twenty connections leaves no `relay-pipe` child alive, and SIGTERM or SIGHUP stops a held connection's stubborn child and removes the socket (`AgentRelayHostTests`); both Linux and macOS only, where those halves run. Their in-process twins: `WslPumpTests` and `WslPumpRunTests`, `AgentRelayConnectionTests` (`CarryConnectionAsync` over fakes and the whole relay over a real socket), `ChildLifetimeTests`; the shared helpers (`FakeChild`, `Posix`, `TestStreams`) sit beside `HostProcess` and are linked, not copied. E6 (defect D, 2026-10-14) added, in `WslInteropTests`: the pure `WindowsBridge.WithParentDeath` argv shaping, the pure `ResolveSetpriv`/`GuardStatus` (kill switch, trusted-dir order, the real off-reason) and the pure `BinaryResolves` (path vs bare-name-on-PATH vs missing); and two `src_mcp` process tests on Linux (skipped without `setpriv`): `WslPumpHostTests.A_child_left_STOPPED_and_orphaned_still_dies_with_the_wrapper` (the pump's stubborn child SIGSTOPped, the wrapper SIGKILLed, the child must still die through `setpriv --pdeathsig KILL`) and `ParentDeathLauncherTests` (the thread-scope backstop — a guarded child forked through `StartPiped` on a thread that then exits must outlive it, else the kernel's thread-scoped pdeathsig would kill the Windows half when a pool worker retires; self-calibrating, skipped on a process-scoped kernel), plus the pure `Program.GuardLogLine` naming the real off-reason (`ProgramGuardLogTests`); `Posix` gained `Stop`. The 2026-10-14 pass counts are in this row's head E2.3 (S1, 2026-11-07) added the test-leftovers helper to all five executables: an adapted copy of `dew_flow_mcp`'s `tests/Mcp.Tests/Leftovers` at `197cc76`, in `src_service_defaults/tests/Support/Leftovers/` (compiled by `CredsForDevs.ServiceDefaults.Tests` and linked into the other four by one wildcard each) and its tests in `src_service_defaults/tests/Leftovers/` (that project only). `template-manifest.json` in the first folder lists every copied file, its upstream blob and each change; `TheAdaptedCopyTests` undoes the changes on the raw bytes and fails on one unexplained byte, so a copied file is never edited by hand. Each executable now sweeps the temp root before its first test (`LeftoversSweepFixture`, an assembly fixture; `SweepRanTests` proves it ran) and fails the run at its end if the run left a folder it claimed (the audit). `CREDS_LEFTOVERS_SELFTEST=1` (or `failed-dispose`) with `--filter-class "*LeftoversSelfTest"` leaks a folder on purpose: that run must FAIL with the audit's first line, and CI checks it on three systems. The copied tests need the shared-rules mount (`git submodule update --init .agents/conventions`) and `node` 20.19 or 22.12 and later. **Two limits are copied with the helper.** F29: on Windows two creds test executables started at once can each sweep the same root; then one can report a removal it did not do, or restore a folder without its claim. Run them one at a time. F31: what a run in an ELEVATED Windows process leaves is owned by `BUILTIN\Administrators`; no sweep removes it and the audit's claim scan does not see it, so on `windows-latest` (elevated) the audit works through its registry half only, and what a killed run leaves there waits for a person. **E2.3 S2 (2026-11-07)** made `HostProcess` registry-correct and added the second root. `TempDirectory(what, warn)` returns an owned, claimed, registered folder (`OwnedDir`); `Remove(path, warn)` takes it out of a static map with one atomic `TryRemove` and only the winner disposes it, so a removal that gives up is one warning, keeps folder and claim, stays in the helper's registry and fails the run at the audit (`disposed but not removed`); a path `TempDirectory` did not give is one warning and untouched. `Start` ties a child to each registered folder its arguments or environment name; `KillOnDispose` kills the tree, then the rest of the child's group, and waits (bounded, not for the pipes) for the confirmed exit and an empty group, and a folder with an open tie is kept (the tie check and the take-out are one step under the folder's gate; the `WaitForExit` after the kill is not pinned by a test on Linux, where .NET's `Kill` returns after the child is reaped - measured, 0 of 300 kills left `HasExited` false); a child whose end cannot be confirmed fails its test with one exception naming the pid and the folder. A child that names none of `TMPDIR`, `TEMP`, `TMP` gets an owned folder under the short socket root as all three, removed once the direct child has exited and, on Linux and macOS, no live process of its group is left (the child starts through `perl`, which makes it a session leader, so its pid is the group's id; on Windows there is no group: the folder goes with the direct child's confirmed exit, and the Reaper's tree kill and the OS refusing to delete an open file are what hold there; a descendant that left its parent's tree and holds no open file is not seen) (a killed .NET host leaves `dotnet-diagnostic-*` and `clr-debug-pipe-*` in its temp folder; measured: six entries per suite before, none in `CredsCli.Tests` after, three in `CredsMcp.Tests` from `ParentWatchProcessTests`, which start the host by their own `Process.Start`). `OwnedCreds` (creds' own, beside the copy) holds `ShortSocketRoot` (`/tmp` on Linux and macOS, the temp root on Windows) and `Canonical`, which follows every link (realpath(3); the final path of the open entry on Windows) and decides whether two roots are one. `LeftoversSocketRootSweepFixture` (a second assembly fixture) sweeps and audits the short root where the canonical roots differ (macOS always; Linux with `TMPDIR` set) and does nothing where they are one. `SocketRootSweepTests`, `HostProcessAdapterTests` and `SweepRanTests` prove it; `WindowsHandoffTests` (Windows only) reads a folder made by the Node wrapper under both keys; its natural-output case MEASURES who owns a new folder and asserts what that implies (collected when the caller's, `foreign-uid` when another SID's, F31), and says which outcome the runner gave. Measured: on `windows-latest` (run 42388598855) a Node-made folder of the test process was the caller's own, not `BUILTIN\Administrators`'. **E2.3 S3 (2026-11-07)** moved the client, process and socket sites to the helper. Every folder a client test makes is an `OwnedScope` or `HostProcess.TempDirectory` folder under a SHORT `what` (`mcp-pump`, not `creds-mcp-pump`); the paths that create nothing take `Owned.TempRoot()` under an exemption marker. A socket binds inside an owned folder under the short root, through `SocketSites.Path` in `CredsCli.Tests`; `SocketSitesTests` shows the folder is owned and the path fits in UTF-8 bytes on Linux (107) and macOS (103), for the longest owner token and for `/tmp` and `/private/tmp`, and that macOS's own temp root would leave under ten bytes. `ParentWatchProcessTests`, which start their host by their own `Process.Start`, hand it an owned `TMPDIR`, `TEMP` and `TMP`: `CredsMcp.Tests` left 3 CoreCLR entries in a private `TMPDIR` before and leaves 0 now (`CredsCli.Tests` 0). `ClientSitesScanTests` runs the copied guard's `raw-temp` reading over the four client trees (a scratch copy; helper folders left out): the findings outside a marker are exactly the three `/tmp` literals that create nothing (`AgentRelayTests`: the default socket path twice and the refused-path literal), left for E2.5's guard; it also fails on a caller that passes a `creds-` name. **E2.3 S4 (2026-11-07)** moved the server's tests. `VaultServer`'s data folder is an `OwnedDir` (`vault`) in the temp root itself, removed after `base.Dispose` (the host and its container end first, then the environment is restored, then the folder goes); the shared `cred-vault-tests` parent is gone. The store, maintenance, logging-sink, startup-guard and registration-hook tests make their folders through an `OwnedScope` (one short `what` per class) and have no hand-written delete; `StartupRefusalProcessTests` asks `HostProcess.TempDirectory` for `vault-refusal`. `VaultServerFolderTests` shows the folder is owned, claimed, registered while the server lives and gone with it, has no shared parent, and is still there at every step of ending the host. `ClientSitesScanTests` reads the server's tests as a fifth tree; the seven backup sites of E2.3 S5 are the only server findings left, in a second expected list S5 empties. With a private `TMPDIR` the server executable left 7 top-level entries (`cred-vault-tests` with 31 children, six backup parents) before and leaves 6 (the backup parents) after S4. **E2.3 S5 (2026-11-07)** moved the backup sites: `BackupArchiveCommandTests`, `BackupArchiveTests`, `BackupKeyFileTests`, `BackupRunnerTests` and `BackupStoreTests` make their folders through an `OwnedScope` (`backup-cli`, `backup`, `key-file`, `backup-run`, `backup-store`), and the AOT kit is a per-run owned folder (`aot-kit`) that the test prints with the two commands to run against it; to keep a kit for a check by hand, set `CREDS_AOT_KIT_DIR` to a folder of your own (it is overwritten and never removed; never set in CI). `AotKitFolderTests` (Linux and WSL only: the server executable runs nowhere else and two executables at once on Windows meet F29) starts this executable twice at once on one private `TMPDIR` and checks the two kit folders differ, are owned names and are gone, with nothing of a kit left behind. `ClientSitesScanTests` now expects exactly the three `/tmp` literals of `AgentRelayTests` over the five test trees. With a private `TMPDIR` each of the five executables leaves 0 entries; on `main` they left 26 top-level entries (956 below them). Counted in the real temp root, the 42 old-name entries this machine held were removed by hand and a full pass of the five leaves none. The template's repository guard, run by hand and not committed, found 18: nine `mktemp` in shell (E2.4), six docker lines in `docker.yml` (E2.4) and the three `/tmp` literals (E2.5). `ParentWatchProcessTests` end the host they started before they remove its folders and keep the folders when the end is not confirmed (`ParentWatchDisposeTests` pins it with a fixture child). The server's test hosts still write a per-run log under `tests/bin/.../logs/`; an owned log folder is a todo. |
| HTTP contract | `http/http-run.mjs` over `http/*.http` | 82 requests, 9 files | the real server over HTTP, with a coverage report that refuses an unlisted route |
| Scenario | `src_vs_code/scripts/*-itest.cjs` | 9 harnesses | a real process, a real socket, a real binary |

## The ten harnesses

Every one is a plain `.cjs` file run by node — no framework — because each starts a real process and
what it needs is control over teardown, not a runner. Nine live in `src_vs_code/scripts/`; the tenth
drives the SERVER binary and lives in the server's tree, so that a server-only change is what re-runs
it. Two of the nine already drive .NET products this way (`creds`, `creds-mcp`) — the harness language
is node throughout because what these need is process control, not a test runner. `npm run itest:<name>` compiles first.

| Harness | Drives | In CI | Verified 2026-09-06 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `src_vs_code/scripts/agent-broker-itest.cjs` | the broker over its own loopback HTTP surface, `vscode` stubbed | **yes** — *Integration test (agent broker)* | pass |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/git-transport-itest.cjs` | the encrypted vault in a real git repository: commit, clone, delete | **yes** — *Integration test (git transport)* | pass |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/creds-cli-itest.cjs` | the real `creds` binary against a live broker | **yes** — *Integration test (creds CLI against the broker)* | pass |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/creds-mcp-itest.cjs` | `creds-mcp` over stdio, the full tool surface, both switch ladders and the quiet path (#95), and — since 2026-09-24 — the caller's TAB TITLE from the C# sender to the TypeScript modal (#136: a fixture home with a registry entry and a transcript; the modal must quote the title and — since 2026-09-24, the owner reversing D4 — the audit line must carry it the same way; skipped on Windows, where `HOME` does not decide the profile folder) | **yes, added 2026-09-06** | pass — **95** checks on Linux (WSL, 2026-09-24), 93 on Windows where the title check is skipped; **111** on Windows (2026-09-30), with the kind-catalogue legs; **116** on Windows (2026-10-09), with level 8 — the real binary killed while its consent modal is open, then Allow: nothing runs, one `ABANDONED` line (E4.S1 of `PLAN_wsl_bridge_outlives_its_client.md`); **137** on Linux (WSL, 2026-11-11), with H1 and H2 in the folder level and the two-windows leg of `scripts/lib/mcpTwoWindowsLeg.cjs` (H3 to H6, rule A: a hidden entry or folder reads as a missing one, with one window and with two; section *A refusal names only what the agent may view*) |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/masked-run-itest.cjs` | a masked run through a real pty, asserting no whole secret appears | **yes, added 2026-09-06** | pass |
| `src_minimalapi_server/scripts/backup-archive-itest.cjs` | the REAL server binary sealing, verifying and opening a backup archive | **yes, added 2026-09-07** — in `ci · server`, on the server's own path filter | pass |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/creds-mcp-wsl-itest.cjs` | the same MCP surface, bridged from inside a WSL distribution; since 2026-10-11 (E5.S1 of `PLAN_wsl_bridge_outlives_its_client.md`) a Claude-Code-shaped client INSIDE the distribution (`wsl-mcp-client.cjs`, the wrapper's real parent) opens the captured session with its held `subscriptions/listen` and ends it six ways — SIGINT, SIGTERM, SIGHUP, a closed stdin, SIGKILL of the wrapper, and (E6/defect D, 2026-10-14) the `/init` interop proxy SIGSTOPped then the wrapper SIGKILLed — and the Windows `creds-mcp.exe` of the run must be alive before each ending (the positive control) and gone within 5 s after it | no — see below | pass — **42** checks (2026-10-11, the final harness; 40 on its first version); 4 of them RED against the pre-E2/E3 build, 10 of 40 on the first version (see *The bridge, proved on the real machine*). E6 added the sixth, `stopped`, ending; its real-bridge run is the manual E6.S2 step, the mechanism measured directly in RESULTS, *Defect D*, and the CI-runnable proof is `WslPumpHostTests.A_child_left_STOPPED_and_orphaned_still_dies_with_the_wrapper` |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/ssh-agent-itest.cjs` | the SSH agent on a named pipe, and which ssh client can reach it; since 2026-09-24 the REAL `SshAgentManager` too, with a signing prompt nobody answers — the real `ssh-keygen -Y sign` must fail within the bound, not hang; since 2026-10-20 (#197 E1.1, section 5, POSIX only) a LIVE login — a real `ssh` against a user-mode `sshd -d` on loopback with a throwaway host key, through the real manager — whose dialog must name *an SSH login* and carry, as a line of its own, *This login is for the server whose host key is ED25519* followed by the fingerprint `ssh-keygen -l -E sha256` prints for that host key (the host-bound request OpenSSH 8.9+ sends). **It ran on the CI runner** (PR #262, run 39580477985, `ubuntu-latest`, `OpenSSH_9.6p1 Ubuntu-3ubuntu13.20`): *ok the real ssh logged in to sshd through the agent · ok the dialog names an SSH login · ok the dialog names the server's host key on a line of its own*; and, before that, in an Alpine container as a non-root user with a locked account (OpenSSH 10.3p1) — so a non-root `sshd` does not trip on the account lock. On a failed login the check's detail carries the last 20 lines of sshd's `-d -e` output. It does **not** prove a login from Windows: that is M1, a real Windows client against a real server, owed by hand. Without `/usr/sbin/sshd` it is a `FAIL` when `CI=true` and a printed `skip` locally; on Windows it prints `skip  live OpenSSH login — POSIX only; M1 covers Windows`. Since 2026-10-21 (#197 E2.1, **section 6**, every platform, no `sshd`) a REAL `VaultKeys` (never mocked; a Map `SecretStorage`) is joined to a real manager by the production `connectAgentToLock`, and three real `ssh-keygen -Y sign` run against it: the first, answered *Allow for 10 minutes*, signs with one dialog; the second rides the window with no new dialog; then `vaultKeys.lock()`, and the third **asks again** (a second dialog, the log line `every signing window ended: the vaults were locked`) and, answered once, **exits 0** — the key was not revoked or unloaded. The section's vscode stub gained a working `EventEmitter` and `showInformationMessage`, and `__AGENT_ANSWER__` may be an array answered in order. Since 2026-10-22 (#197 E3.4, **section 7**, POSIX only, one `sshd -d` run per login — six runs) the **login window** is proven with real OpenSSH: a real `VaultKeys`, `connectAgentToLock` AND `connectAgentToSettings` (auto-lock at its default, 60), a manager per leg. *Ed25519 leg:* login 1 raises one dialog with four buttons and one list (1, 4, 8, 12 hours), answered *Allow longer…* → *1 hour*, and logs in; login 2 raises **no dialog** and the log names *login window of "itest Ed25519 key"*; a real `ssh-keygen -Y sign` (a Git signature) still asks, without *Allow longer…*, and signs; `vaultKeys.lock()`, then login 3 asks again, without *Allow longer…*, saying *Longer windows need the vault unlocked*, and logs in. *RSA leg* (sshd adds `PubkeyAcceptedAlgorithms +ssh-rsa`): login 1 (SHA-2, the client's default) → *1 hour*; login 2 (SHA-2) no dialog; login 3 with `-o PubkeyAcceptedAlgorithms=ssh-rsa` (SHA-1, flags 0) **asks**, without *Allow longer…*, and says *Longer windows need a SHA-2 signature*. The stub gained `showQuickPick` (reads the label to "click" from `__AGENT_PICK__`; none named is Escape), `CancellationTokenSource` and a `workspace` (`getConfiguration` from `__AGENT_SETTINGS__`, `onDidChangeConfiguration`). The sshd helpers (`launch`, `startSshd` — now taking extra `sshd_config` lines —, `endChild`, `lastLines`) and section 7's `login` live in `scripts/itestSshd.cjs`, so the script stays at 759 lines. Its scenario RED was shown **by revert** (the fix landed in E3.3): with the gate's scopes reverted to `scopes(false)`, *login 2 raised no dialog* fails with `2 dialogs` (and the Git-signature and lock checks that count dialogs after it). It does **not** prove a login from Windows — that is the Extension Development Host check, by hand. Skipped on Windows with `skip  live login window — POSIX only; the Extension Development Host check covers Windows`; with `CI=true` a missing sshd FAILS. **Cost:** the section prints `live login window: six sshd runs, N ms` — 1960 ms in the Alpine container (OpenSSH 10.3p1, non-root user) | **yes, added 2026-09-11** — the POSIX branch only, see below; the CI job installs `sshd` first (apt with `Acquire::Retries=3`, so a mirror hiccup does not turn the job red) | pass (all seven sections; sections 5, 6 and 7 in an Alpine container as a non-root user, 2026-10-22; section 5 in CI on `ubuntu-latest`, PR #262) |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/bridged-ssh-itest.cjs` (with `itestBridgedRig.cjs`, `itestBridgedTrust.cjs`, `itestBridgedSecurityKey.cjs`, `itestBridgedRefusals.cjs` and `itestBridgedClient.cjs`) | **E1.6, `npm run itest:bridged-ssh`, POSIX only.** What a REAL `ssh` and a REAL `sshd -d` do under the per-call file of a bridged `ssh:exec` (the E1.6 spec §S6.5, legs (a)-(k) and (n)-(q)). Every production leg is the product's own `writeBridgedCall` and `bridgedSpawn` from `out/`, then a spawn of `ssh` with that argv, environment and `cwd`; every control is written by hand under `-F <rig home>/.ssh/config` and shows that this rig can make the bad thing happen. The rig is a hop on 127.0.0.1 and a destination on 127.0.0.2, a storage folder and a home with a SPACE in their names, keys from `ssh-keygen`; `sshd -d` serves one connection, so each login has its own run, and a second `sshd` with another host key on the same address and port is how a changed host key is made. Leg 0 (`ssh -G` under the file: the lock-down, the hop's own identity and the sentinel, no other `identityfile`, no `sendenv`) needs no `sshd`. It covers: that a hop is never asked for the destination's password and a password destination is asked once at most, by the destination's name (a); that the environment of the child is the allow-list (b); that no local program starts from the person's configuration (c); that an agent's keys are not offered (d); that each leg is offered its own key and the hop refusals spend no spawn (e); first contact, its record outliving the keys folder and a changed key refused, for a destination and a hop (f), (h), (k); that `${VAR}` in a path is replaced by `ssh` and the writer refuses it, and a hop user that reaches a shell is refused (j); on CI only, a hostile `/etc/ssh/ssh_config` is never read (i) and no default identity is offered (n). **Added by the fix-forward (§S6.14).** The harness prints `ssh -V` as its first line, and runs whole as the job `bridged-ssh-images` on `ubuntu-22.04` and `ubuntu-24.04` beside `ubuntu-latest` (probe PV1: OpenSSH 8.9p1 and 9.6p1, green, 2026-11-16). Leg (b) 3 puts `BRIDGE_TEST_SECRET` back into the child under the PRODUCTION file, against an `sshd` that accepts it: the remote `printenv` still exits 1 with nothing printed, so the file's missing `SendEnv` is proven on its own. Leg (b) 4 reads the environment a child RECEIVES: `/usr/bin/env` started through the production spawner `runBounded`, with the environment and `cwd` of `bridgedSpawn`, prints exactly the asked names and neither `BRIDGE_TEST_SECRET` nor `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` (control: the same child under the whole environment prints both; it needs no `sshd`). Leg (o), in `itestBridgedSecurityKey.cjs` (Y4, probe PF1), has two halves. The CONTROL: a synthetic `ed25519-sk` key file with no token, a hand-written config with no `PubkeyAcceptedAlgorithms` line, and the askpass log read for a prompt that begins `Enter PIN for` (measured on OpenSSH 8.9; 9.6 and 10.2 ask only to confirm user presence, so there it prints *NOT MEASURED* and does not fail). The PRODUCTION half: the same key as the hop's stored key under the product's own per-call file, a pinned password destination and the same askpass; where the control showed the prompt, the askpass log is EMPTY and the hop leg ends `Permission denied` (the hop's `sshd` saw the connection); where it did not, the same facts are printed as a record, nothing is asserted, and `F11` alone pins the `PubkeyAcceptedAlgorithms -sk-*` line (the user-presence prompt is not the spec's leg, so the assertion is not widened to it). Leg (p), in `itestBridgedRefusals.cjs` (Y5), needs no `sshd`: a chain whose one hop has the host `none`, no user and port 22 is refused (R6) with the harness's spawn counter unchanged and the storage folder, the pin file and the person's `known_hosts` as they were; a RECORD prints `ssh -G -J none` and `ssh -G -J u@none` (the first with no `proxyjump` line on 10.2p1); a key destination whose pin does not parse (`garbage`) is refused (R11) the same way. Leg (q), in `itestBridgedClient.cjs` (Y3), needs no `sshd` either: the product's own `bridgedSshClientCheck` from `out/` is asked with the program named to it: the real `ssh -V` through an `ssh` of the rig's `bin` folder that logs its start and runs the real client (the verdict is ok, the version is printed, the log holds exactly `-V`: the control), a stand-in script that prints `OpenSSH_8.6p1` (R13, naming `OpenSSH_8.6` and nothing else of the output) and a stand-in that exits 255 and prints nothing (R12, `exit 255`); each stand-in logs one start, the real client's log still holds its one line (no other spawn), and the check's folders are gone. **What it does not prove:** a Windows run (M8, M9); a password login that SUCCEEDS (the CI `sshd` is not root and cannot check a password: a leg shows who was ASKED); anything the record legs print (they assert nothing about the build, and the lines go to the spec's As-built). With `CI=true` a missing `sshd` fails; off CI the legs that need it print a skip. | **yes** — *Integration test (bridged ssh)*, after the SSH agent step | **not run on the machine that wrote it (no `sshd`): legs 0, (b) 4, (p) and (q) ran there; the legs that need an `sshd`, (o) production among them, are CI's first run** |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/bridged-mysql-itest.cjs` | **E1.6, `npm run itest:bridged-mysql`, Linux.** Parent §4.11, leg (m): a fake server completes the MySQL handshake and answers the query with a `LOAD DATA LOCAL INFILE` request for a marker file; Oracle's `mysql` and MariaDB's client, each named by an absolute path (the harness prints each `--version`; with `CI=true` a missing one fails). Control: without `--local-infile=0` the server receives the marker's bytes on each client, and with an option file that sets `load-data-local-dir` and `--local-infile=0` but no `--no-defaults` Oracle's client still sends them. Production: `buildDbQueryLaunch('mysql', …, { bridged: true })`'s arguments with the same option file in the rig home: the server receives none of the bytes, on each client. **Added by the fix-forward (§S6.14).** PD1, a record leg: `mysql_config_editor` writes a login file with a marker host under a rig home, and `--no-defaults --verbose --help` of each client runs with and without `MYSQL_TEST_LOGIN_FILE` at a path that does not exist (Oracle's client reads the file and the variable closes it; MariaDB's reads none; 2026-11-16). Leg (m) added: that case as an ASSERTION on the production launch's environment (`absentFile` from `materializedKeyPath`), with Oracle's control (the file is read without the variable); MariaDB has no control, since it reads no login file. `received()` reads its result after the client's pipes closed and the server read each connection to its end, not after a fixed delay. **Added by §S6.15 (epoch 11).** PM1, a record leg on each client present: against the same fake on TCP (listening on `127.0.0.1` and, where the host has it, `::1`), `--no-defaults --protocol=TCP -h localhost` reaches it (PM1-a: a STOP if not, since that client rejects the flag or still takes the socket); the same without the flag (PM1-b: a record, and where the client reaches the fake by TCP anyway the socket control is *not shown*, which is no STOP); a login with no `-u` (PM1-c: the user name the fake reads); and `MYSQL_PWD` set and unset (PM1-d: the length of the authentication answer, never the answer). Leg (t), assertions on the builder's own launch (`buildDbQueryLaunch('mysql', <an entry whose host is localhost, with a stored user and password>, …, { bridged: true, absentFile })` and `bridgedSshEnv`): the fake is reached on TCP, the login names the entry's user, the authentication answer is not empty; the control without `--protocol=TCP` does not reach the fake on Oracle's client and is recorded as *not shown* on MariaDB's (it reached the fake on the runner of 2026-11-17). The harness checks the answer's length and does not recompute it from the password. **Not proven:** other client versions; a login a real server checked (the fake accepts any login); any platform but Linux. | **yes** — *Integration test (bridged mysql client)* | **not run on the machine that wrote it (no `mysql` client): CI's first run** |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/bridged-psql-itest.cjs` | **E1.6 fix-forward (§S6.14 Y0 and Y6), `npm run itest:bridged-psql`, Linux.** A fake PostgreSQL server and a real `psql` (named by `BRIDGED_PSQL`, default `/usr/bin/psql`; the harness prints its `--version`; with `CI=true` a missing one fails). PD2: the server answers the start-up message with a request for a clear-text password and records the password message; `psql -w` under a rig home whose `.pgpass` holds a marker for that host and port, with no `PGPASSWORD`, with no `PGPASSFILE` and with it at a path that does not exist. PD3: the server answers `SSLRequest` with `S`, completes TLS with a throwaway certificate (made by `openssl` at run time), REQUESTS a client certificate and records the one offered; `psql` with `sslmode=require` under a rig home whose `.postgresql` holds a throwaway certificate and key (the marker), with no `PGSSLCERT`/`PGSSLKEY` and with both at paths that do not exist. PD2 and PD3 are RECORD legs (the lines `record (PD2)` and `record (PD3)`; both answered as expected on 2026-11-16: the person's file is used and the variables close it). Leg (r) is the same two cases as ASSERTIONS, **reshaped by §S6.15 Z2 (epoch 11)**: its two production launches named an entry with no stored password, which the builder now refuses (Q16), and `psql` reads `PGPASSWORD` before any password file. They launch an entry that stores a password: PD2's server receives the stored password and not the marker; PD3's offers no certificate. Because the stored password now settles PD2 by itself, a second case takes `PGPASSWORD` and `PGPASSFILE` (and for PD3 `PGSSLCERT` and `PGSSLKEY`) out of the environment and shows that the built string's own `passfile` (`sslcert`, `sslkey`) keywords alone keep the person's files closed, beside a control with the three keywords cut out of the string, which sends the marker (offers the certificate). **Added by §S6.15 (epoch 11), in `lib/psqlProbeLegs.cjs`:** PQ2, record legs that complete PQ1 on this client (its version is printed first): the stored URI handed whole (hand-written, as the builder made it until Z2) with a certificate named in it, with `service=s3`, with `?passfile=`, `?service=s1` and `?service=s2`, and with the key spelled `%70assfile`, `PASSFILE` and doubled; a hand-written keyword string of the shape the builder writes against an environment that names the person's marker files, against an unnamed `.pg_service.conf`, with `passfile` written twice, with `require_auth` and `sslcertmode` against an environment that says the opposite; `sslmode=verify-full` against a server certificate a CA made at run time (the CA in the rig home's `root.crt`, without it, with a revoking `root.crl`), through the builder with an entry that stores a password; the shape with a database name `service=s1` and one that begins `postgresql://`. A line that starts `STOP (PQ2-` says an answer went against the parent's row. Leg (s), assertions: the stored URI handed whole is the control (the rig home's markers and the URI-named certificate arrive; the certificate is asserted when PQ2-a showed it); `buildDbQueryLaunch` answers `undefined` for each of those entries once a stored password is put in, and builds the same entry without the setting, and no process is started while it runs (observed on `child_process`, whose seven starters are counted for the duration of the builder calls, after a check that the observer sees a start of node itself); an entry inside the profile, launched from the builder's answer under a rig home that holds every marker file, sends the stored password and no marker to PD2's server and offers no certificate to PD3's. The harness is three files: `bridged-psql-itest.cjs` (PD2, PD3, (r)), `lib/psqlProbeLegs.cjs` (PQ2, (s)) and `lib/psqlRig.cjs` (the fake servers, the run of one `psql`, the certificates; `lib/psqlCheck.cjs` is the pass/fail line). A check fails only for a fault of the rig (no `psql`, a client that never reached the server, a path that exists) or, in (r) and (s), for an assertion. **Not proven:** other `psql` versions; a database that asks for a certificate and refuses a connection without one; any platform but Linux; a login a real server checked (the fake servers record what arrives and refuse it); Kerberos or any authentication the server asks for besides a clear-text password and a client certificate. | **yes** — *Integration test (bridged psql client, probes PD2, PD3, PQ2)*, after the mysql step | **run on the machine that wrote the legs of §S6.15 with `psql` 18.6 (75 checks); CI's run with the runner's client** |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/wsl-agent-relay-itest.cjs` | `ssh-keygen -Y sign` inside Linux reaching an agent in a Windows process; since 2026-10-11 (E5.S1) also that the relay's Windows children end with their connections — ten `ssh-add -l` leave 0 `creds.exe relay-pipe` of the run and the relay's fd count at its baseline; the relay is stopped by its own pid; the manager's `dispose()` takes down a relay holding a LIVE connection, its relay-pipe on Windows included | no — see below | pass **after repair — see below**; **40** checks (2026-10-11, the final harness; 36 on its first version), 9 of them RED against the pre-E3 build |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/server-transport-itest.cjs` | `ServerTransport` against a RUNNING Cred Vault Server | no — see below | **not run** — needs a server on `127.0.0.1:5113` |

**Why each of the remaining three is not in CI.** *"Not in CI" with a reason is a decision; "not in CI" alone is
a harness rotting* — so each carries one.

- **`creds-mcp-wsl-itest.cjs` and `wsl-agent-relay-itest.cjs`** need a WSL distribution with the .NET
  SDK, node and python3 inside it. The extension job runs on `ubuntu-latest`, where WSL does not exist
  and the thing under test — the Windows↔Linux bridge — has no meaning; the `windows-latest` leg of
  `ci-clients.yml` (E5.S2, 2026-10-11) runs the .NET process tests of the Windows half, not these — a
  hosted Windows runner has no distribution either.
- **`ssh-agent-itest.cjs`** was left out for a real reason, and the reason turned out to argue for
  reading its result narrowly rather than for running nothing. The argument was: on Ubuntu it
  exercises the POSIX branch, while its subject is the Windows named-pipe path and which of the
  several `ssh-add` binaries on a Windows PATH can reach it — so a green there is a pass about the
  branch it does not exist to protect. All true. What it misses is that the script also drives the
  agent PROTOCOL against a REAL `ssh-add` — a key parsed, loaded, listed and signed for — and
  nothing else in CI does that on any platform. **Added 2026-09-11**, with the step's comment saying
  in as many words that the Windows half stays uncovered by it.
- **`server-transport-itest.cjs`** needs a Cred Vault Server running with the Local auth scheme. CI
  states this reason in the workflow itself: adding it would mean building and running the server
  inside the extension's job.

Two were added on the day this file was written, because neither had a reason — only an absence.

**And the leak that made two of them unrunnable by hand is fixed** (`scripts/wslStrays.cjs`, new).
Both WSL harnesses asserted *"nothing outlives the client"* as a GLOBAL question — `ps -eo args |
grep '[c]reds-mcp'` over the whole machine. Measured 2026-09-11: both failed that one check
identically on `main` and on a feature branch.

**And then the processes were looked at, which is the part worth keeping.** They were not debris.
Thirteen of them: six `creds-mcp` servers, each the child of a LIVE Claude Code session running
inside WSL, plus a `creds relay` holding `/run/user/1000/creds-agent.sock` — the runtime path the
extension publishes, not the `/tmp/creds-relay-itest-<pid>.sock` the harness uses. Every one was
somebody's working tool. So the global question was not merely fragile after a bad run: it is red on
a working machine **always**, and the harness was unrunnable by hand for anyone who actually uses
this product. A harness that cannot be run is a harness that rots.

Its own leftovers are a real second failure, and the sweep is for those: a run that failed partway
stopped without taking its processes down, so the next run inherited them.

The question is a DIFFERENCE now — what was alive before this run, against what is alive after —
and a sweep in a `finally` takes down whatever this run started and left, whichever way it ended. A
stray from somebody else's session is neither asserted on nor killed: this run did not start it. The
first check in the relay harness got stronger by the same change, since *"a real relay is running"*
now means ours rather than any.

**And since 2026-10-11 the question is asked by EXECUTABLE PATH, on both sides** (E5.S1 of
[PLAN_wsl_bridge_outlives_its_client.md](PLAN_wsl_bridge_outlives_its_client.md)). Each run
copies the binaries it starts into a directory named for the run — `%LOCALAPPDATA%\creds-itest\creds-itest-<run>\` on Windows (the person's own local application data, never the shared temp directory),
`${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-~/.cache}/creds-itest/<run>/` inside the distribution (`scripts/wslRun.cjs`; the person's own
cache directory rather than `/tmp`, where a predictable name could be pre-created by another local user and the
harness would run their binary — SonarCloud S5443 on the first version) — and a watch is "processes of this
image whose executable lies under this run's directory": `watchWindowsPids` through `Get-CimInstance
Win32_Process`, `watchLinuxPids` through `/proc/<pid>/exe` (`scripts/wslProcs.py`). The Windows side is
watched at all because that is the side the leak was on — every `creds-mcp.exe` and `creds.exe relay-pipe`
the bridge starts is a Windows process, parented to the WSL service and never to the test's `wsl.exe`. A
stop goes by pid through a HELD handle (a `Process` object on Windows, a pidfd inside the distribution),
the path re-read through it immediately before the kill, so a pid reused between the check and the stop
cannot be hit; the pids and the directory reach PowerShell and Python through their environment or argv,
never through a command line. And **nothing is deleted inside the distribution** (a machine rule since an
agent's `wsl.exe` cleanup removed parts of a home directory, 2026-10-07): the Linux build lands in a
directory named by a hash of its inputs, so a stale build is never reused and never removed; sockets and
scratch files are run-unique; each run ends by printing what the cache directory now holds — a few MB per run,
for the person to clear when they like. **And every step inside the distribution is exe +
argv** (`security.md`; E5's code round): `wsl.exe -e bash -l scripts/wslSteps.sh NAME=value... -- <verb> [args]`,
one constant checked-in script whose exports are applied after the login profile and whose every value — a path, a
socket, a file list — is an argv entry; nothing composes shell text from a value, and a harness that cannot run
here throws `skip(...)` so the sweep and the finish still run before it exits 0.

**`masked-run-itest.cjs` on Linux was verified, not assumed.** A reviewer called adding it to an
`ubuntu-latest` runner blocking, on the grounds that a pty harness written and run only on Windows
usually breaks on Linux. Fair, and checkable: it was run inside a real WSL Ubuntu with node 20 and
every check passed. There is no native pty module here — `node-pty` is not a dependency of this
extension at all; what the harness stubs is VS Code's pseudoterminal *interface*, and the shell it
spawns is the platform's own.

**`server-transport-itest.cjs` is documented from its source, not from a run** — it is the one
harness on this page nobody has executed while writing about it, and that is stated rather than
implied. Its assertions and CI status are read off the file; whether they still hold is unverified
until someone starts a server and runs it. That is the honest state, and it is why it sits last in
`itest:all` with its prerequisite spelled out.

## Test leftovers (2026-11-10, E2.5 of [PLAN_test_leftovers_E2.md](../todo/PLAN_test_leftovers_E2.md))

The map of what a test, a harness or a CI script of this repository makes outside its own process, and who removes it. The detail of each
helper stays in the paragraph that built it: E2.1 (the unit suite, next section), E2.2 (the harnesses, *Prerequisites, timeouts and cleanup*),
E2.3 (the C# executables), E2.4 (`owned.sh`, the shell scripts, the image smoke and the acme harness; all in that same section).

1. **The rule and the name.** The contract is `.agents/conventions/common/test-leftovers.md` and its detail
   (`test-leftovers-detail.md`). The token of this repository is `creds`. A thing a test makes is named
   `testsToDelete-<owner>-creds-<what>-<id>`; a folder is claimed first, by the file `<folder>.owner` beside it (never inside).
2. **The three helpers** (one per language; none is a second implementation of the rule's decisions):

   | Helper | What it is | Who makes the folder | Collected at the start of a run | Audit at the end | Windows |
   |---|---|---|---|---|---|
   | Node: `src_vs_code/scripts/lib/leftovers.cjs` (with `harnessScope.cjs`) | a wrapper over the mounted reference `tools/lib/owned.mjs`; nothing of it is copied | `ownedDir(t, what)` or a `cleanupScope()` | `npm test` and `npm run itest:all`, in both roots (the temp root and the short socket root) | `scripts/run-tests.mjs` removes what this run's record claimed and left, and only once the process tree is PROVEN ended (Linux). On macOS it removes nothing (it cannot look for a descendant that left the process group): it lists the folders this run claimed and left, and fails the run; a run that claimed none keeps its own exit code | the Node sweep only lists `unverified:` (F16); the audit removes nothing (the supervisor cannot confirm its job is empty): it lists a folder this run claimed and fails the run; claims that carry this record but are older than the runner are listed `unproven:` and left alone |
   | C#: `src_service_defaults/tests/Support/Leftovers/` | an adapted copy of `dew_flow_mcp` at `197cc76`, changed only by the operations of `template-manifest.json` (checked by `TheAdaptedCopyTests`), linked into the five xUnit executables | `OwnedScope`, `OwnedDir` | each executable's sweep (`LeftoversSweepFixture`, shown by `SweepRanTests`) | the audit after the run | the C# sweep collects, under both keys; two executables at once can race (F29); what an elevated run leaves waits for a person (F31) |
   | bash: `.github/scripts/lib/owned.sh` | written here, with a self-test in real child processes | `owned_dir <var> <what>`; its root is `TMPDIR` (a CI step hands it `RUNNER_TEMP`: the helper reads no other variable) | no sweep of its own: the next `npm test` or xUnit run, or the janitor | the exit trap removes the folder by its exact name | needs Git Bash, Node and the mount; the janitor is a dry run there until F9 |

3. **Containers.** The image smoke of `docker.yml` is a carve-out of the contract: owner-form names with the runner's uid as a literal, one
   always-run remover, a check step that lists what is left, and a smoke that stays failed. `deploy/acme-test/run.sh` owns its compose project,
   labels and state and calls the janitor first. See the paragraphs *The image smoke is a carve-out of the contract* and *The acme harness owns its run*.
4. **Who collects what a killed run left** (`kill -9` runs no trap; the claim and the folder stay):

   | OS | Collected by |
   |---|---|
   | Linux, macOS | the next `npm test`, `npm run itest:all` or xUnit executable; or the janitor (below) |
   | Windows | the C# sweep of the xUnit executables, under both keys; the Node sweep only lists (F16); the janitor is a dry run until F9; new files of `BUILTIN\Administrators` wait for a person (F31: the review at a persistent Windows machine, or 2026-12-05) |

5. **The two guards.** Both read the files of the repository text by text, are green on the head, and are checked on every pull request.

   | | The shipped Node guard | The copied C# guard |
   |---|---|---|
   | Reads | `*.test.*`, `.sh` and `.ps1` everywhere, `.github/scripts/*.{mjs,js,sh}`, workflows, the `scripts` of every `package.json` (`--profile node`) | `.cs` of the six test roots and six source roots (`tests/`, `src/` and the five projects' own); `.sh`, `.ps1`, `.py` everywhere; JavaScript in the script folders; the `src_vs_code/src/test/` helpers that are not `*.test.ts` (read as scripts); workflows; `owner-blind` in scripts and workflows through the mounted classifier (started through `node`) |
   | Runs | `docs.yml` (the required job `plan lifecycle · submodule pin`), after the step that installs the mount's modules (`npm ci --ignore-scripts --prefix .agents/conventions`) | `LeftoversRepositoryGuardTests` in `CredsForDevs.ServiceDefaults.Tests`: `ci-clients.yml` (Linux, macOS, Windows), `sonarcloud.yml`, and the release jobs `cli-binaries` and `mcp-binaries` on every runner they use |
   | By hand | `npm ci --ignore-scripts --prefix .agents/conventions` once (a fresh checkout's mount has no `node_modules`, and the guard cannot load `yaml`), then `node .agents/conventions/tools/leftovers-check.mjs --repo . --profile node --token creds --identity "the developer-credentials server and extension"` | `./src_service_defaults/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredsForDevs.ServiceDefaults.Tests --filter-class "*LeftoversRepositoryGuardTests"` (`.exe` on Windows); the plants are `LeftoversGuardCredsTests` |
   | A scan of nothing | exit 2 and `scanned nothing under <dir> — discovery broke; the repository is not clean`; a `docs.yml` step runs it on an empty repository | `FilesScanned == 0` and the same sentence (the copied test) |

   What a clean Node run means: zero for the guard's listed spellings (table D2 of `dew_flow_conventions` `todo/PLAN_F30_folding_reader.md` at
   the pin `37b9aa5`), in the files its `node` profile reads. It reads `'a' + 'b'`, `.concat`, `['a', 'b'].join('')` and unquoted-piece joins in a
   `.cjs`, a `.ts` or a `.sh`; it does not read a name built through a variable, across lines or at run time. The step's comment in `docs.yml` has the
   whole sentence. The C# guard excludes the two docker classes (`docker-run`, `docker-name`) for exactly one file, `.github/workflows/docker.yml`
   (`LeftoversGuard.DockerJudgedByNodeGuard`, pinned by a test): the carve-out is read by the Node guard, which is green on it; the same text under
   another workflow name keeps both findings. `leftoversGuardFixtures.test.ts` reads that list from `LeftoversGuard.cs` as text and proves, through the mounted guard, that each path is one the `node` profile scans and that a container start planted there is a `docker-run` finding: a later pin whose discovery dropped the file would turn it red, instead of leaving the file to neither guard. The wrapper `leftovers.cjs` is the fifth entry of `FoldingAllowances`; it changes no answer (the copy
   folds `.cs` only), and a case in `LeftoversGuardCredsTests` pins that.

   The tests that read the shipped Node guard (`dockerSmokeLeftovers`, `acmeRunLeftovers`, `leftoversGuardFixtures`) get its path and its load from
   `src_vs_code/src/test/mountedGuard.ts`: ONE constant for the mount path. The guard loads `yaml` from the mount's own `node_modules`, so a job that
   runs the whole unit suite installs it (`npm ci --ignore-scripts --prefix .agents/conventions`) and sets `CREDS_GUARD_TESTS_REQUIRED=1` on the step:
   there a guard that cannot load FAILS the test with the reason; a person's `npm test` without the modules still skips with the reason.
   `workflowMount.test.ts` pins both for the three jobs (`ci-extension.yml`, `sonarcloud.yml`, the `release.yml` extension job).
6. **What neither guard reads** (measured at E2.5): a split spelling in `src_vs_code/scripts/*.cjs` or `src_minimalapi_server/scripts/*.cjs` (the `node`
   profile does not discover those folders and the C# copy folds `.cs` only); `${TMPDIR…}` in a `.sh` (it is a root, not a temp call); a name built
   through a variable or across lines; compose files; a `/tmp` inside a raw string that also holds a quote (the template's limit); and, for the Node
   guard, `mktemp` in a workflow `run:` block (the C# copy reads it).
7. **The exemptions.** `testsToDelete-exempt: <class> — <reason>` on the line or the line above; a class of the rule (never `owner-blind`, never
   `ryuk-off`), a reason of at least ten characters and three words. List them with `git grep -n testsToDelete-exempt -- . ':!todo' ':!research'`. A new
   one is read by a reviewer; this page keeps no copy of the list. The product scripts `install.sh` (two), `deploy/restore.sh` and `deploy/restore-archive.sh`
   (one each) carry a marker for their own `mktemp`: their reasons do not say they clean up, because `install.sh` and `deploy/restore.sh` do not on every path.
8. **The janitor.** `JANITOR_MIN_AGE_MINUTES=10 bash .agents/conventions/tools/janitor.sh --token creds --dry-run` lists what a killed run left, for this token
   only (without `--dry-run` it removes). On Windows run it from Git for Windows' bash: it removes nothing there until F9. On macOS it needs bash 4 or newer (N20).
9. **Old names** (`creds-*`, `cvbk-*`, `arrival-lease-*`, `wsl-mcp-check-*`, the legacy prefix spelling) are not swept; a person removes them by hand once per machine.
10. **What stays open.** `kill -9` runs no trap (the next run or the janitor collects); F16 (the Node sweep on Windows), F9 (the janitor on Windows), F31, N6
    (`${TMPDIR…}` is no finding), N20 (bash 3.2), N21 (a flaky Windows dry run), and the Windows clients leg is not a required check.

## What the unit suite leaves behind (E2.1, 2026-11-02)

`npm test` makes only folders that are **named, claimed, removed and collected** under
`.agents/conventions/common/test-leftovers.md`: `testsToDelete-<owner>-creds-<what>-<id>`, with the claim in a
sibling file `<folder>.owner` (never inside, because tests assert the exact content of their folders).

- **The helper.** `src_vs_code/scripts/lib/leftovers.cjs` (types in `leftovers.d.cts`) is a WRAPPER over the mounted
  reference `.agents/conventions/tools/lib/owned.mjs`. It binds the token `creds` once with `ownedFor` and re-exports
  the reference's functions as they are (`ownedDir`, `cleanupScope`, `tempRoot`, `removeQuietly`, `readOwner`, …).
  It adds three things: `shortSocketRoot()`, `ownedFile()` and `writeStub()`. Nothing of the reference is copied.
  No mount, no helper: it refuses and names `git submodule update --init .agents/conventions`. CI fetches the mount in
  every job that runs these tests: the extension job, its Windows runner job, the extension release job and the SonarCloud job
  (its coverage run is `node --test` over the compiled folder). `workflowMount.test.ts` reads the workflows job by job and fails
  a job that runs the unit tests without the step.
- **A test makes its folder** with `ownedDir(t, '<what>')` (`t` is the test context) or, for a helper that many tests
  call, with one module-level `const scope = cleanupScope(); after(() => scope.close());` in the file. A failing
  assertion still removes the folder. Never at module load: a run that filters the file's tests away skips its hooks.
- **Sockets** live in folders made under `shortSocketRoot()` (`/tmp` on Linux and macOS, the temp root on Windows),
  with a `<what>` of at most five characters: macOS allows 103 bytes in a socket path and its temp root is about 49
  characters long. `leftoversSites.test.ts` computes the longest path (owner `w` + 10 digits, ten-digit pid) through
  the product's own `socketPathFor` and `agentSocketPath` and asserts it fits, on every OS.
- **The runner** `scripts/run-tests.mjs`, in this order: loads the helper; sweeps both roots (see below); exports its
  record once as `TESTSTODELETE_RUN=<record>`; starts `node --test` in a process tree of its own (a group on POSIX, the
  reference's job-object supervisor on Windows); when the coordinator has exited, ends the WHOLE tree and confirms no
  descendant is alive; then audits. The audit finds every owner-form folder and orphan claim of both roots whose claim
  is EXACTLY the record the runner exported (a string, never recomputed), removes it, and turns a passing suite into
  exit 1 with `this run left <n> folder(s) it claimed`. A folder claimed by any other record is never touched and never
  counted; an unmarked folder is listed. If the tree cannot be confirmed ended, the audit removes nothing, fails the run
  and says `(not removed: <n> descendant(s) still alive)` — or `could not be confirmed` when the check itself failed. A root
  the audit cannot list (anything but "not there") is named (`unlisted: <root> (<code>)`) and fails a passing run; a claim it
  cannot read is named (`unreadable:`) and left alone. The group probe is one `kill(-pgid, 0)`; only on Linux, and only when it
  says "alive", `/proc/*/stat` is read once, and a group of zombies only is ended (a container with no init never reaps them). After the
  group is killed the runner also looks for what LEFT the group (a grandchild that called `setsid`): on Linux every process
  carrying the run's record as a whole environment entry, everywhere the parent-pid chain; the scan runs once after the group is
  gone, survivors are ended and only those pids are polled. A claim file another user owns is listed and never read.
  The wait is bounded: 60 minutes, `RUN_TESTS_TIMEOUT_MS` overrides it, and a timeout that fires is reported before the tree
  is ended. One run at a time per process. **On Windows the audit removes nothing for now**: the reference's job supervisor
  gives no acknowledgment that its job is empty, so the audit lists and fails a run that left a claimed folder (a passing run
  with nothing claimed stays passing); **on macOS the survivor scan has no environment to read, so the audit removes nothing there: a run that claimed a folder lists it and fails, a run that claimed none leaves with the suite's own code** (and a chain-walk survivor there is not killed either: a survivor is signalled only with a start time bound, which is Linux's); where a record has no start time (macOS, Windows) only claims modified since the runner started, less two seconds of clock slack, count as this run's, and older ones are listed as `unproven:`; that choice removes nothing on those systems, where the audit does not remove at all.
- **Where the collection guarantee holds.** `npm test` (and, with E2.2, `npm run itest:all`) sweep; a direct
  `node --test out/test/<file>.test.js` makes and removes its own scratch but does not sweep old leftovers — the next
  runner run, or the janitor, does. **On Windows the Node sweep collects nothing** until F16 closes: the reference lists
  `unverified:` there. The collector on Windows is the C# sweep of the xUnit executables (E2.3) or the janitor.
- **The start-of-run sweep (wired, F22, release `cc24f9e`).** The runner's default sweep is the MOUNTED reference's
  `sweepLeftovers` from `.agents/conventions/tools/lib/leftovers-sweep.mjs`, called with this repository's token AFTER the
  caller's options (a `token` in them cannot override it), over both roots. It is loaded fail-closed on its own: if the module
  cannot be loaded the runner writes ONE line (`leftovers sweep not run (…)`), sweeps nothing and keeps the suite's exit code. A
  sweep never changes the exit code. It collects a dead-owner, old, owner-form `creds` folder under the two keys; a live or
  unknown owner, another token's folder and an unmarked folder survive (listed); on Windows it lists `unverified:` and removes
  nothing (F16). Before F22 the mounted sweep ignored `creds` folders (it served the reference's own token only).
- **Proofs.** `leftoversRunnerSweep.test.ts` (the real default sweep in private roots), `leftoversWrapper.test.ts` (binding, names, claim beside the folder, unwind, export list, refusal without
  the mount), `leftoversRunner.test.ts` (the audit by record, the other record's folder untouched, the kill path with a
  real `node --test` and a surviving descendant, the timeout, the refusal; `ci-extension.yml` job
  `runner lifecycle · windows` runs the wrapper, runner, gate, hardening and sweep files on Windows (the sweep file's F16 `unverified:` scenario and the hardening file's win32 pid case run only there [E2.1 win jobs]); `leftoversRunnerGate.test.ts` and
  `leftoversRunnerHardening.test.ts` carry the process-tree, macOS and bound scenarios; `leftoversRunnerAudit.test.ts` the root it cannot list and the claim it cannot read) and `leftoversSites.test.ts` (a syntax-tree scan for raw
  temp calls in the migrated files, a call through a local alias of a named import included, with companions that keep the scan alive).
- **Old names** (`creds-*`, `cvbk-*`, `arrival-lease-*`, `wsl-mcp-check-*`, `creds-life-*.sock`, the legacy
  `testsToDelete-agent-*`) are not swept: `creds-` is not a registered token and other software uses such names. A
  person removes the ones already on a machine by hand.

## Prerequisites, timeouts and cleanup

Since E2.2 every harness makes only **owned** folders and stubs: `testsToDelete-<owner>-creds-<what>-<id>` with the claim
in a sibling `<folder>.owner`, made through `scripts/lib/leftovers.cjs`, and removed by `scripts/lib/harnessScope.cjs` on
every exit path the process can handle: a return, a failure count, a throw (also at load), `.catch`, an uncaught error from a
callback, and SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP (exit 128 + the signal; a second signal during the close is ignored). A harness never calls `process.exit` in its
body; it returns its code. A child that may make temp paths (the `creds-mcp` and CLI binaries, `git`, the server in the backup
harness, the shell of the masked run, `ssh`, `sshd`) gets an owned folder as `TMPDIR`, `TEMP` and `TMP`. A folder that holds
a Unix socket is made under the short socket root (`/tmp` on Linux and macOS) with a short name. A process a harness starts
through `itestSshd.cjs` (`ssh`, `sshd`) belongs to the scope it was launched in: `launch(scope, …)` and `startSshd(scope, …)`
register its end in that scope right after the spawn, so closing a section ends its own processes and waits for their exit
before it removes the section's folder (registered earlier), and never ends a process of another scope.

What stays after a kill is therefore **nothing the next sweep does not collect**. SIGKILL (and a power cut) cannot be handled:
the folder and its claim stay, and the next `npm test` or `npm run itest:all`, or the janitor, collects them. The limits of that
sweep: POSIX only (on Windows the Node sweep only lists `unverified:`, F16, so a Windows leftover waits for E2.3's C# sweep or a
person), a folder younger than 10 minutes is left alone, and a folder whose owner is not provably dead is kept. A harness started
alone (`npm run itest:<name>`, or `node scripts/<name>.cjs`) does not sweep.

| Harness | Needs | Leaves behind if killed (SIGKILL; any other end removes its folders) |
|---|---|---|
| `src_vs_code/scripts/agent-broker-itest.cjs` | nothing | a loopback listener ends with the process; owned `agent-stub` and `agent` folders (the broker's endpoint file and socket are inside) |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/git-transport-itest.cjs` | `git` | owned `git-stub` and `git` folders (a bare repository and the clones) |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/creds-cli-itest.cjs` | the CLI built (`src_cli/src/CredsCli.csproj`) | owned `cli-stub`, `cli` (broker) and `ctmp` folders |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/creds-mcp-itest.cjs` | `creds-mcp` built (`src_mcp/src/CredsMcp.csproj`) | a `creds-mcp` child on stdio ends when its stdin closes; owned `mcp-stub`, `ctmp`, `empty`, `old` and `mcp` (one per broker window, and one more `mcp` folder that holds the two discovery files of the two-windows leg) folders |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/masked-run-itest.cjs` | nothing | owned `masked-stub` and `mrun` folders; the child dies with the pty |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/ssh-agent-itest.cjs` | OpenSSH (`ssh-add`, `ssh-keygen`) — built-in on Windows, on PATH elsewhere; CI runs it on Ubuntu | an owned `ait` folder (under the short socket root; key, stub, the managers' storage `m`, `l`, `w`, `w2` and `w3`) and — only on a run that reaches the live login (section 5; section 7 makes its own) — an owned `sshd` folder (host key, `authorized_keys`, `known_hosts`, `sshd_config`, and section 7's per-leg files). Under `-d` sshd neither daemonises nor forks per connection, but its privilege separation and the session still fork children that exit when the connection closes — which is why the run ends the ssh client FIRST, then sshd, by their own pids; each is awaited (5 s bound) and, if it did not exit, killed with SIGKILL and awaited again; only then is the `sshd` section's scope closed, and a folder sshd still holds is retried and then left claimed with one warning. Since G2, section 9 (`itestNoSharing.cjs`, POSIX only) has an owned `nsh` folder of its own under the short socket root, closed by its own scope in a `finally` (see *The extension's own ssh calls share no connection (G2)*). Since G1, section 10 (`itestPinnedRoads.cjs`, Linux only) has an owned `pin` folder of its own under the short socket root, closed by its own scope in a `finally`; the forwarded socket of its bridge is where the product names it, `/tmp/creds-<user>-<id>.sock`, and is removed by its exact path (see *The three roads of the person and the saved host key (G1)*). Since E3.S2 the `pin` folder also holds `k0/` (leg 0's config, known-hosts file and control socket), `program.json` per counterpart, `k0/session-programs.json` and `identities.json`; a master that a killed run strands ends by itself after 60 s with no client (`ControlPersist 60`) | **E3.S4:** the `pin` folder (section 10) also holds one folder per leg of the rows (shim, records, storage, home, handed config, `given.json`, its counterparts, `evidence.json`), a leg process per leg (ended by its pid and start time at its limit of 300 s), and a bridge's socket per bridge leg in `/tmp`, removed by its exact path. **E3.S5:** the `pin` folder also holds one folder per leg of M4 and M9 (25: the same files, and `tool.json` in the two Connect legs of M9), a leg process per leg, and, for a while in the legs of M4 (d), the window's key folder `keys/<pid>/` set to mode `0500` (it holds no file, and the leg puts `0700` back in a `finally` before its end checks; the scope puts it back too). **E3.S6:** the `pin` folder also holds one folder per leg of M2, M3 and M8 (7: the same files, a `pins/` folder of the controls' pin files, in the M2 legs the known-hosts file that the leg's config names, in the M3 legs a master's control socket `cm` that ends with the leg, and in `m8` the folder `khc/` with the recording command, its line file and its records), a leg process per leg, and a bridge's socket in `/tmp` for each bridge leg, removed by its exact path. **E3.S7:** the `pin` folder also holds one folder per leg of M7, M12 and M14 (34: the same files, and in each a folder `st/<name>`, the leg's storage folder, whose name holds a space, quotes, a backslash, `%`, `${` or a tab; in the two collision legs of M7 also the twins, files at the path that a bare word would resolve to (`st/xyz/keys/<pid>/known_hosts-e1-<uuid>`) and the control's pair `st/x'y'z/control/` and `st/xyz/control/`; in `m14-a` to `m14-c` the exec's own pin file `keys/<pid>/known_hosts-e1`, which nothing deletes until the leg's folder goes), a leg process per leg, and a bridge's socket in `/tmp` for each bridge leg of M7, removed by its exact path. **E3.S8:** the `pin` folder also holds one folder per leg of M5 (11: the same files; in `m5-g` and `m5-h` a folder `forced/` with the leg's forced script at mode 0700 and a `standin/` folder; in `m5-f` `kj_key`, `jump_known_hosts` and the two counterpart folders `j` and `d`; in `m5-e` and `m5-f` a `pins/` folder of the controls' pin files), a leg process per leg, and a bridge's socket in `/tmp` for each leg that opens one, removed by its exact path. The silent listener of `m5-d` and `m5-d-unpinned` is a socket of the leg process on 127.0.0.1 and ends with it (the leg's scope destroys every socket, then closes the server).
| `src_vs_code/scripts/bridged-ssh-itest.cjs` | OpenSSH (`ssh`, `ssh-keygen`, `ssh-agent`, `ssh-add`) and `/usr/sbin/sshd`; CI installs `sshd` first; legs (i) and (n) need the runner's passwordless `sudo` and no `~/.ssh/id_ed25519` | one owned `bridged` folder under the short socket root (the keys, `storage dir`, `rig home`, one `sshd-<leg>` folder for each run, the agent socket); the child `ssh` and `sshd` processes are ended by their pids, the client first, each awaited; leg (i) puts `/etc/ssh/ssh_config` back from a copy and compares hashes in a `finally`, and leg (n) removes the default key it made in a `finally`; a SIGKILL leaves the owned folder claimed for the next sweep, and may leave the hostile lines in `/etc/ssh/ssh_config` of a CI runner, which is thrown away |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/bridged-mysql-itest.cjs` | `mysql` of Oracle and of MariaDB on the runner | an owned `bridged-mysql` folder (the option file, the rig home, the marker file); a loopback listener ends with the process |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/bridged-psql-itest.cjs` | `psql` (the `postgresql-client` package) and `openssl` on the runner | an owned `bridged-psql` folder (the rig homes with `.pgpass` and `.postgresql`, the throwaway certificates and keys); a loopback listener and the `psql` children end with the process |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/creds-mcp-wsl-itest.cjs` | WSL + the .NET SDK, node and python3 inside it | nothing it started — the sweep stops the run's own processes by pid on both sides; an owned `wsl-stub` folder; `${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-~/.cache}/creds-itest/` (a build per changed set of inputs, a directory per run) stays by design, a few MB each, until the person clears it. Its run directory (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\creds-itest\creds-itest-<run>`) is not an owned folder and is removed by its exact name in `finishRun`. Windows-only, not run in E2.2 |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/wsl-agent-relay-itest.cjs` | the same, plus OpenSSH inside it | the same; a run's sockets and scratch files are under its own `…/creds-itest/<run>/`; it has no temp-root site and E2.2 did not change it |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/server-transport-itest.cjs` | a Cred Vault Server on `127.0.0.1:5113`, Local auth | an owned `srv-stub` folder |
| `src_minimalapi_server/scripts/backup-archive-itest.cjs` | the server built (`src_minimalapi_server/src/CredVaultServer.csproj`), or `CVBK_SERVER` | owned `backup-source`, `backup-work` and `backup-occupied` folders (the server child's `TMPDIR` is `backup-work`). CI runs it in `ci-server.yml`, which sets up Node 22 and the mount first |
| `src_vs_code/scripts/tree-perf-bench.cjs` | `npm run compile`; started by hand: `node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs scripts/tree-perf-bench.cjs` (`BENCH_OUT` names another `out/`) | an owned `bench-stub` folder |

Every harness carries its own timeout on the child processes it spawns; none of them waits for ever,
and each prints the command that would fix a missing prerequisite instead of hanging on it. Re-running
after a kill is safe for all of them — the two WSL ones included since 2026-10-11, because their sockets
are run-unique: a harness killed outright (so its `finally` never ran) leaves THAT run's relay or wrapper
alive inside the distribution, which the next run neither asserts on nor needs; stop it by its pid.

## Running them all at once

```bash
cd src_vs_code && npm run itest:all             # every harness this platform can run
npm run itest:all -- agent git masked-run       # or a named subset
npm run itest:all -- --with-server              # plus the one needing a server you started
```

`src_vs_code/scripts/run-itests.mjs` compiles once — the nine `itest:*` aliases each begin with their
own `npm run compile` — then runs each harness. On Linux and macOS it streams the output as it arrives rather than
holding it until the child exits, because a harness waiting on a socket looks identical to a hung one
when its output is held back; **on Windows the output appears when the harness ends** (the reference's `run` holds it so that
it can end the whole job, and a harness that writes more than 32 MiB there is ended and fails). It prints a summary separating
**pass**, **skipped** (a prerequisite is missing and the harness said so) and **not runnable here** (Windows-only, on a
non-Windows machine), and it names each harness's output size in bytes.

The runner has a lifecycle of its own (E2.2, `common/test-leftovers.md`), in this order:

- **It loads its helpers fail-closed.** No mount: one line naming `git submodule update --init .agents/conventions`, no
  harness started, exit 1.
- **It sweeps both roots at the start** (the temp root and the short socket root), through the same wiring as `npm test`. The
  lines start `run-itests:`; a sweep never changes the exit code. A single `itest:*` alias does not sweep. On Windows the sweep
  only lists `unverified:` (F16).
- **It exports its record once** as `TESTSTODELETE_RUN=<record>` to every harness, whatever the ambient environment held.
- **A timeout ends the harness's whole tree**: a process group and `endTree` on POSIX, the reference's job object on Windows. A
  tree not confirmed ended is one more phrase in that harness's FAIL line.
- **A signal stops the run**, from the moment the runner starts (also during the helpers, the sweep and the compile). SIGINT, SIGTERM or
  SIGHUP goes to the running compile or harness, no further harness starts, the summary is printed and the exit code is 128 + the
  signal's number.
- **A harness that exits but leaves a descendant holding its output pipe** is waited for at most 30 seconds, not for ever. The runner then
  says so, stops reading, and counts the harness by its exit code. Without a timeout it does not scan for that descendant and does not end
  it (the tree end runs only after a timeout; that is a recorded follow-up). On Windows a line before each harness says its output is held
  until it ends.
- **There is no harness-wide end audit**, unlike `npm test`: a harness's children may still be ending when it exits, and a removal
  by the run's record would remove a folder they use. Each harness removes its own folders through its scope; what a kill leaves is
  the next sweep's.

Three details are there because a review round asked for them, and one because Windows refused the
obvious answer:

- **A mistyped name is refused**, by name, with the legal values and a non-zero exit — a typo that
  quietly runs nothing must not report success.
- **Each harness is bounded at fifteen minutes** and killed with the reason named, so a deadlock
  inside one does not deadlock the runner.
- **`server-transport-itest.cjs` is opt-in.** It probes `127.0.0.1:5113` first and reports *skipped*
  when nothing is listening, because an "all tests" command that always exits 1 on a freshly built
  checkout cannot be the routine health check it advertises.
- **It spawns `node` on each script rather than `npm run`, with no shell.** A reviewer asked for
  `shell: false` and was right about the smell — but dropping the shell while still calling `npm`
  fails on Windows with `EINVAL`, measured here: since the CVE-2024-27980 mitigation Node refuses to
  spawn a `.cmd` without a shell, and `npm` on Windows *is* `npm.cmd`. Naming the script directly
  avoids the shell and the alias together.

Verified 2026-09-06 on this machine: `npm run itest:all` — **8 passed, 0 skipped, 0 not runnable
here**.

## What running them found

**`wsl-agent-relay-itest.cjs` could not start.** It crashed with
`TypeError: distros.find is not a function` before its first manager check. Two API drifts, neither
of which anything could have caught:

- `WslRelayManager.start(command, distros)` takes an ARRAY of distribution names. The harness passed
  the bare string `''`, from back when it took one. `['']` is the correct translation — an empty
  name means the default distribution, and `refuse` skips empty entries deliberately.
- `manager.socketPath` became `socketPathFor(distro)` when the manager gained multi-distribution
  support. The harness kept calling the old getter, got `undefined`, and crashed on `.length`.

**The repair was checked for vacuousness, because a reviewer asked whether `['']` exercises
anything.** It does: `refuse` skips empty entries rather than rejecting them, and `start` then calls
`launch('')`, which is the default distribution. The proof is downstream in the same run — *"a real
relay is running in the distribution"* asserts `ps -eo args | grep creds relay` finds one, and
*"disposing the manager takes it down"* asserts it is gone afterwards. A vacuous start would fail
both.

**The lesson is structural, not incidental.** These harnesses are untyped `.cjs` requiring compiled
`out/*.js`, so they can drift away from the API they drive with nothing to notice — no compiler, no
test, and for six of the nine, no CI. A harness that cannot start proves nothing, and it had been
proving nothing silently. The repair is in `wsl-agent-relay-itest.cjs` with the reason written above
each change.

## The bridge, proved on the real machine (2026-10-11, E5 of `PLAN_wsl_bridge_outlives_its_client.md`)

The defects of [RESULTS_wsl_bridge_orphans.md](RESULTS_wsl_bridge_orphans.md) — a server that never exits on
end-of-stream once a `subscriptions/listen` is open, a wrapper that dies from a signal without stopping its Windows
half, a relay that never closes its children's stdin — were each fixed under process tests on Linux CI (E2, E3).
What those cannot say is whether a real Linux process reaching a real Windows process through WSL interop behaves
the same, because the Windows half is in another process table. The two WSL harnesses now say it:

- **`creds-mcp-wsl-itest.cjs`** — a client shaped like Claude Code runs INSIDE the distribution
  (`scripts/wsl-mcp-client.cjs`, spawned by the distribution's node so it is the wrapper's real parent and no
  shell sits between — a bash `&` job starts with SIGINT ignored), sends the captured 2.1.289 session with its held
  listen, and ends it on one word from the Windows side: `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM`, `SIGHUP`, `SIGKILL` (sent by itself,
  as Claude Code does) or `eof` (the wrapper's stdin closed). For each: the session answered, exactly one
  `creds-mcp.exe` of the run alive on Windows (the positive control — a watch that sees nothing would pass every
  time), the wrapper's exit as the plan states it (130 / 143 / 129 / 0 / killed), 0 Windows survivors within 5 s,
  nothing of the run left inside WSL. The thirteen older checks (the vault read through the bridge, a create with
  consent on Windows, `--help` answered without a Windows process) are kept in front of it.
- **`wsl-agent-relay-itest.cjs`** — the relay is a child of the harness (its first stdout line names the socket;
  its stderr's connection lines are the positive control that a relay-pipe was started for each connection); ten
  `ssh-add -l` after a warm-up connection, then 0 `creds.exe relay-pipe` of the run on Windows within 5 s and the
  relay's descriptor count (`wslProcs.py fds`) back at its baseline; the relay stopped by its own pid, gone with
  its socket; then the manager's relay with a connection HELD open by `scripts/wslHoldSocket.py` — one relay-pipe of
  the run alive on Windows — and `manager.dispose()` taking both down within 5 s, socket included.

**Each check has teeth, shown rather than claimed.** Both harnesses were run unchanged from a scratch checkout of
`9494a67c` (E1 merged, E2–E3 not): 4 of 42 and 9 of 40 checks RED on the final harness (10 of 40 and 8 of 36 on its
first version, whose run also caught the old Windows half surviving a SIGHUP), with the defects' own words — *the wrapper
ends with exit code 130 — {"code":null,"signal":"SIGINT"}*, *the wrapper did not end within 20000 ms of eof*, *0
Windows survivors within 5 s — … did not take down: 15284*, *0 new creds.exe relay-pipe … did not take down:
46832, 15120, …* (eleven), *descriptor count … back at its baseline (83) — now 103*, *it removed its socket on
the way out — … still exists*; and the RED run's sweep stopped exactly the one survivor, by pid. The numbers are
in the RESULTS record, *E5 — the harnesses on the real bridge*.

**The release smoke moved into a script** — `.github/scripts/mcp-listen-smoke.sh <binary>` — so it runs here
against a locally published binary before a tag as well as in `release.yml` on every runner that can execute what
it built: the captured session, stdin held open until `tools/list` is answered, then closed; exit 0 within 10 s;
the log's start line, parent and `exited: code 0, reason clientClosed`. GREEN on this branch's Debug build and its
win-x64 AOT publish; RED on the pre-E2 binary (*exited with code 124 at 61s* — the guard). And `ci-clients.yml`
gained a `windows-latest` leg (bash, the `.exe` suffix), so the Windows half's process tests run on a pull request
for the first time; it becomes a required check only after it has reported once.

**The release binaries report their tag's version, and a smoke holds them to it (2026-10-11,
[PLAN_release_binaries_carry_their_version.md](PLAN_release_binaries_carry_their_version.md)).** Until then
