| A run closed during its own banner (E3 review, fix A) | `maskedTerminalClosedDuringStart.test.ts` (a real `/bin/sh` child) | a listener of the banner write closes the terminal: the child that appears afterwards is killed with its tree and the script (the values) is never written to it |
| The road host's spawn use (H6-H10; F-S1 F2) | `sshRoad.test.ts` | a spawn preparation never relies on the agent; `spawnLaunch` names the client, the storage folder and the extension's agent socket for a forwarding entry, says `ownAgent` only when the entry forwards AND the agent is up, always carries `withoutEnv` = `E3_INHERITED_DROP`, refuses with `NO_SSH_TO_SPAWN` and carries the agent warning; `handOverToRun` takes the session off the list only after the run exists and shares one once-only end; a throw while the run is made leaves exactly one cleaner |
| Run with Secrets over SSH (S1-S10, O1-O10, N1, R1-R6; F-S1 F3, F6 (3)) | `sshRoadSecrets.test.ts` | the order prepare, trust, dependencies, resolve, spawn; no child and no value before the trust answer; the child is ssh by argv with the script on stdin and no value in argv or env; the trust line is asked and one approval covers the arrow; the masked run's end cleans the session once (S7); the run adds only the askpass and agent variables and no value (S9); the launch warning is in the banner and no toast; ONE OWNER on each exit (declined trust, cancelled chain, failed resolution, a throw, a failed creation, a run that ended while it was made, a close before the start, a refused spawn launch); one snapshot; a Windows remote, a remote window, no reference and a missing SSH entry are said before prepare; a forwarding entry with no agent of ours asks the trust question on the line that holds ` -A ` and sends none (F3); the masked run gets `withoutEnv` for a key, a password and a forwarding session (F6 (3)); a linked script keeps the shared sentence and an unlinked command runs here as before (the two guards) E2.S2 (33 tests, 31 before): a pinned session asks the program and the folder of the spawn once, before the trust question; a client that cannot be read spawns nothing, says sentence F as the warning, asks nobody, reads no value, and the session (with Connect's pin file) is cleaned by its owner once. |
| No local sink (LN1-LN11) | `linkedNeverLocal.test.ts` | for a valid, a missing and a refused link: the arrow, Run with Secrets and a dependency chain never reach their local sink; an agent's VPN start and a chain with no lookup never type a linked step; the bare line is trusted too, so a bypassed road cannot stop at the local trust modal |
| The agent's gate and run (S2, G1-G24, X-guards) | `agentSshGate.test.ts` | the gate answers only for an SSH entry that is itself usable by agents (closed, PIN-hidden, another account, trashed, not SSH and unsafe target all give the one sentence, code `denied`); the gate runs in `validate`, so nobody is asked to allow a refused run; the summary names the SSH entry, its target and the trust line, and an unlinked entry's words are today's; a switch turned off, a link or target changed while the dialog was open is refused at `run`; a linked run with no shown summary or over another SSH entry than the one the broker holds is refused, a local one is not compared (G11, G24); `uses` names the SSH entry only after the gate (G23); `asksAfterConsent` is true only for a usable, forwarding entry with the agent up; a bridged call is refused with the allow-list's words and a journal line that says what the bridge was told; the agent reads no name, host or reason of the SSH entry; an agent's VPN start with a linked launcher stays refused; `exec` and `run` sit on the same rung; the broker hands `run` the summary it showed and the used entry once per call on both doors (G17); the mask table of a linked entry holds the SSH entry's password (G14) |
| The agent's linked run (AG1-AG8) | `agentSshRun.test.ts` | a usable, trusted linked entry runs the BARE line over SSH, and the recorded call says `ownAgentOnly: true` and `noTty: true`; the trust key is the trust line, not the local line; a busy window answers `too_many_requests` and runs nothing; the slot is released when the ssh call throws; the SSH entry is read once (one snapshot); a linked entry written for Windows is refused after the gate, naming only the Terminal entry; an unlinked entry runs here as before; a linked entry never reaches the local captured run, also with the gate bypassed (AG8) |
| One use through the REAL broker (U1-U11) | `agentSshOneUse.test.ts` over `agentSshWorld.ts` and `brokerWorld.ts` (`vault` option) | two linked runs in a row use a one-use SSH entry once, the second refused naming ONE entry (U1); a direct `creds_exec` and linked runs at the same moment start ssh once, in three arrival orders (U2); an SSH entry whose delete failed stays spent on both roads (U3); an ordinary SSH entry is never queued and burns nothing (U4); a one-use Terminal entry burns as before and both burn when both are one-use (U5); a linked run that did not answer 200 spends nothing (U6); the turns are taken in key order, so two calls that use each other's entry both end, even with a holder on one key (U7); a waiting rotated value of the SSH entry goes in before the table is read (U8) and the agent's output masks the password the run used (U9); every per-entry hook of the broker has a decision for the used entry, from a table the scan checks (U10); the sentence for a spent used entry names one entry (U11); a throw while the grant's entry burns still burns and marks the used one |
| `execOverSsh` shared by two callers (X1-X5; F-S2 F4, T2, F6 (4)) | `sshExecOver.test.ts` | the argv is `buildSshExecArgv` with the snapshot's jump and pin; the written key is removed after the call and after a throw; a password entry uses askpass and puts no password in argv; a PIN-locked password is refused and nothing is spawned; a jump chain that does not resolve is refused after the credential and the key is removed, and `ssh:exec` still resolves its chain after the credential; as the linked run calls it: `-T` first, no `-A` without our agent (and `-o ForwardAgent=no`), no host `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, no `CREDS_REF_` name, our socket only when it serves; as `ssh:exec` calls it: today's argv and environment, host socket included (the guard halves, watched red once with the two words written into `sshExecAction`) E2.S2 (19 tests, 11 before; its entry now holds a saved host key): the pin of a call off a bridge is decided before its file is written, of the program the call will start; a pinned call asks it once and runs with the six pairs, an older client without the `KnownHostsCommand` word; a client that cannot be read is `409 no_credential` with sentence F, nothing written and nothing spawned; a refused path is sentence E and echoes no path; an entry with no saved host key is asked nothing and runs with `accept-new`; a saved host key that cannot be used still runs with `accept-new` (C3); deps that name no asker refuse a pinned call without starting any `ssh`; a written path of a refused kind is refused and the entry's own file is not deleted. |
| The scans (F5, T2's scan) | `launchRoadScan.test.ts` | see the composer's scan row above: `only_ssh_exec_still_sends_dash_a_without_our_agent`, `no_tty_is_sent_by_exactly_two_production_sites`, each with its companion |
| The broker's call seam | `brokerCallLaunch.test.ts` | `callFor` builds a call with the new required `uses` key |
| The help (Hp6-Hp10) | `sshRoadHelp.test.ts` | all five languages say Run with Secrets runs over SSH from a local window, and `secret-references` says the value reaches the server on standard input and is in the command's environment there, that the run is not interactive and that the command can keep running after a close; the terminal bullet says an agent's run needs the SSH entry to be *Usable by agents* (Hp8) and that a linked run spends a one-use SSH entry (Hp10); the paragraph that advises choosing an ssh entry ends with the older-version note (Hp9). One table (`RUNS_BULLET_SENTENCE`) holds the bullet's sentence for Hp6, Hp8 and Hp10 |

**Teeth.** Watched red by reverting the line the behaviour depends on: the stdin write, the stdin destroy at the end (a killed child's pipe is destroyed anyway, so M9 alone does not see it: M9b emits an `error` while the child lives), the once-only end, the over guard of `open()`, the failed-start end, the argv-mode folder (M12, M13) and the type of `cwd` (M14); the spawn use of `prepare`, the storage folder, the list removal in `handOverToRun`; the window check, the no-reference check, the trust line, the owner's `own`. The no-local-sink tests were watched red by the temporary bypass of EVERY wall of their road (the routing, and the local road's own `sshLinkRefusal` where it has one); one wall alone leaves most of them green, which is why both are bypassed. S2: the gate answering `local` for every entry (AG8: *a local run printed the probe*), a closed switch counted as usable (G2), the compare removed (G10), `planLaunch` before the gate (G20), the bridge wall removed or moved after the link (G13), `validate` not asking the gate (G6), the gate's refusal not applied in `run` (G9), an `eval` wrapper (AG1), the local line's trust (AG2), the slot not released (AG4), `accountOnly` removed (G4). Epoch 4: `-T` and `ownAgent` and `withoutEnv` each watched red after a no-op type and again with the production line neutralised (T1, F1, F6 (1)-(4)); `ownAgentOnly: true, noTty: true` written into `sshExecAction` fails the guard halves of T2 and F6 (not committed); the turns without `.sort(byKey)` (U7: the two calls did not end), the burn without the `finally` (the used entry stayed), the one-use filter taken out of the turns (U4), the burn on any status (U6), `releaseWaiting(call.uses)` removed (U8), `burnAfterUse` left out of the table (U10).

**Not covered.** A real sshd: what a server does with `ConnectTimeout`, a wrong password and the closed stdin; that `-T` beats `RequestTTY force` in a real config, that a forwarding entry with no key in the extension's agent leaves `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` unset on the server, and that a key only in the person's own agent is not used (a stored key the server refuses ends with `Permission denied`, also with the Windows agent service); a Windows local host with a password entry; the POSIX jump helper after a close (`pgrep -f 'ssh .*-W'` empty a few seconds after closing the masked terminal); an unreachable host ending the run with ssh's error after about 10 s; and that the remote command can outlive the closed terminal (sshd's behaviour). These are **hand check 3 of the parent plan's §10, with the three lines epoch 4 added**, and the owner runs them: they are owed, not a gate for the merge, and if any of the three fails the measure is a comment, not a protection. Hand checks 1 and 2 (the arrow, with a key and with a password entry) are the owner's too. Hand check 4 (a real peer, the typed line) was run on 2026-11-10 by the lead, with the owner's leave. Not driven either: a real VS Code window (the masked terminal's pseudoterminal is exercised without one).

## The composer for a Terminal entry over SSH (2026-11-10, E1.S2 of PLAN_terminal_command_over_ssh.md)

Pure tests and real shells, no VS Code: `remoteLaunch.ts` has no `vscode`. Run from `src_vs_code`: `npm run compile`, then
`node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs --test out/test/remoteLaunch.test.js out/test/remoteLaunchDelivery.test.js out/test/remoteLoginShell.test.js out/test/launchRoadScan.test.js out/test/sshCommand.test.js`.

| Flow | Test | What it holds down |
|---|---|---|
| The link | `remoteLaunch.test.ts` R1-R8 | none, self (no lookup), missing, trashed at depth two, not SSH, unsafe host or user, linked; the candidate list of the form and `sshLinkOf` agree over one fixture vault; every sentence compared whole |
| The plan | `remoteLaunch.test.ts` R9-R15 | empty first; the `{name}` refusal beats the link's; Windows remote and line breaks (LF, CR, NUL, in the command and in a row) refused; each plan's remote command is the login form (`loginShellCommand`) of the wrapper or of the bare line (R15, U3); the plan has no trust line |
| Wrapper, trust line, typed line, secrets script | `remoteLaunch.test.ts` R16-R23 | the wrapper text and no `"`; the trust line changes with host, user, port and jump and not with the key; posix on every OS; PowerShell 5.1 refuses `"` only and TYPES a trailing backslash (R20, U8: the remote command of every plan ends with `'`); prefix, accept-new and `-t`, with the login form as the remote word in the posix and the PowerShell quoting (R21, R22); the snapshot, never the plan; the script text with its first and last marker line, the blank line, a bad name throws (R23, U9) |
| The moved refusal | `remoteLaunch.test.ts` R24 | S1's sentence through `sshLinkOf` (exact text, no link and an empty link are no refusal, self is a link); the old `sshLinkGate.ts` is gone |
| `buildSshCommand` | `sshCommand.test.ts` C1-C6 | byte-identical without `remote` over twelve fixtures, four option shapes, two platforms; the order `-t`, options, `--`, destination, word; `tty: false`; quoting by the family of `platform`; an empty command and an unsafe target give `undefined` |
| Delivery | `remoteLaunchDelivery.test.ts` | a fake `ssh` records its argv: bash (and pwsh where present) deliver every hostile command of the `CORPUS` list in the test as ONE element, byte-equal; dash, bash and zsh parse the element as the line; the wrapper keeps the login shell after a comment and an `&`, says a non-zero exit, and ends on `exit`; the secrets script runs through `secretsRemoteCommand(nonce)` with a stdin reader, and (F3) the command names the login shell (`exec ${SHELL:-sh} -l -c '…'`); a bash-only line runs when the login shell is bash and fails with 127 when it is dash, as typed there. Each element is delivered in three forms (the line, `loginShellCommand(line)`, `loginShellCommand(stayOpenWrapper(line))`) by bash and pwsh; the corpus is `remoteCorpus.ts`, shared with `remoteLaunch.test.ts`, and the remote side of every real-shell run has an owned `HOME` (`remoteShellHarness.ts`). `windowsParsers.test.ts` reads the remote word of the same three forms back through Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7 (the `runner-windows` job; Windows' own re-quoting of an argument under 5.1 stays a hand check) |
| The login shell (PLAN_ssh_line_runs_in_login_shell.md) | `remoteLoginShell.test.ts` T1-T14, `remoteLaunch.test.ts` U1-U9 | the owner's bug through the composer, in sshd's way (`<shell> -c '<the one remote command>'`) with an owned `HOME` that holds the profile: T1-T4 a tool that only the profile puts on `PATH` is found by the bare line, the stay-open roads and Run with Secrets on bash, dash and `sh` (zsh, ksh, mksh and ash where installed; the CI job installs none, a missing shell skips with its reason printed), bash reads `~/.bash_profile` first; T5, T8 the exit code, the folder and `exit` as before; T9 the typed line end to end through a real local bash and the fake `ssh`; T6, T7 a profile or a startup file that reads standard input gets no value and the run is refused when the script is not whole (bash with `SSH_CLIENT` set and no `SHLVL` reads `~/.bashrc` first), a profile that hides `cat` fails closed (127); T10 a logout file runs only after `exit` and never for Run with Secrets (the limit of the other roads is recorded); T11-T14 the script is not in a child's environment, a tracing profile prints no value, the script runs whole or not at all (every cut of one script, 145 of them), the real `cat` reads it. Each row of the Run with Secrets tests is watched RED under the rejected form it guards (`"${SHELL:-sh}" -l -s`, the first approved text, `eval` in the login shell, `exit 1` for `false`, plain `cat`, the first marker only). U1-U9: the builder text, no `"` added, the plan, the exact secrets command and its texts (with a fixed test nonce; U4b: the nonce is 32 hex digits, new for each run, anything else refused), the wrapper byte-identical, the trust line, the copy note, the ending, the script's marker lines. Also in `remoteLoginShell.test.ts`: a value that holds a marker line (the old marker, the first marker of another nonce, both markers of another nonce) runs nothing after a startup file read two lines (S1; RED before the nonce), a profile with a DEBUG trap prints no value and dash still runs (S2), and the stand-in login shell of the harness is tested. `sshRoadSecrets.test.ts` checks that the nonce in the argv is the one in the script and that two runs have two nonces |
| Fixes of the E1 review (S4, F1-F6) | `remoteLaunch.test.ts` F1, F4, F4b; `sshLinkShareArrival.test.ts` F2, F2b; `remoteLaunchDelivery.test.ts` F3; `sshCommand.test.ts` F5 | F1: the trust line changes with ONLY a forward and with ONLY agent forwarding, equals `sshOptionArgv`'s words, and omits a disabled or invalid forward; F2: *Update it* keeps the recipient's link and a forged payload link sets none (through the inbox and through `updateInPlace` itself); F4: the candidate list holds no copy of the link checks and `isLinkTarget` agrees with `sshLinkOf` over a fixture; F5: `withAcceptNew` puts the option after the program word whatever it is (a path too) |

**Measured, and different from the spec.** An unclosed quote in the line is a syntax error of `eval`, a special builtin: bash and
zsh say it and go on to the login shell, but dash ends the session (status 2). `command eval` would keep dash going; the wrapper
text is the spec's, so `the_wrapper_reports_an_unclosed_quote_and_a_login_shell_follows_except_in_dash` records both.

**Teeth.** Each mutation was applied alone, watched red, and restored: no `--` fails C2, C3, C4 and the bash delivery; the remote
word quoted for PowerShell on Linux fails C4 and the bash and dash delivery cases; the wrapper without `eval` fails R16 and the
comment and `&` cases; the self check off fails R2 and R8; the key path in the trust line fails R18; no blank line in the script
fails R23 and the secrets run; the typed line reading `plan.ssh` fails R21, R22 and R22b; no name check fails R23; the 5.1
refusal without the trailing backslash fails R20; NUL not a line break fails R14; no `-t` fails C2, C4 and the bash delivery; `--`
pushed without `remote` fails C1 and nine older tests; a road reading `.sshEntityId` or a new `sendText(` in a road file fails the scan.

**Not covered.** A real sshd round trip, Windows' own re-quoting of a native argument and PowerShell 5.1 (the parent's hand
checks); `pwsh` and `zsh` skip where absent (neither is on the machine this was written on).

## The folder run (2026-11-05, S3 of PLAN_terminal_folder_run.md)

The pure half, `folderRun.ts`, is tested by importing it with no `vscode` stub (`folderRun.test.ts`: importing it directly is the
proof that it holds none). The command, `credSshManager.runFolder`, is the real handler of the real `registerRunCommands`,
reached through `src/test/folderWorld.ts`: a fake host with a tree, a real display order (`siblingOrder`), the real trust store,
guard, pinned-shell decision and dependency chain, in the world of `vpnWorld.ts`. The world gained `disposed` on every terminal
record (a `dispose` COUNTS and closes nothing, so a terminal stays listed and live, the worst case VS Code allows), a settable
`window.terminals` that counts the reads made after the first dispose (`listReadsAfterDispose`), `list` and `exit` (a terminal the
person opened, and one that really ends), `open(name, shellPath)`, `modal: 'last'`, and `failCreate`, `failSend`, `onSend`; at S3
terminals a flow creates stayed unlisted, so no older test changed meaning (`busyTerminalGuard`, `launcherBusyTerminal`, `dependencyChain`,
`vpnGoneRequest` and `commandNameRoads` were run on it first and were unchanged), and since S5 one is listed at once, as VS Code does.
Run from `src_vs_code`: `npm run compile`, then
`node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs --test out/test/folderRun.test.js out/test/folderRunCommand.test.js out/test/folderRunEdges.test.js`
(22 + 49 tests at S3; 36 + 48 + 17 since S5, which split `folderRunEdges.test.ts` off at the 800-line limit).

| Flow | Test | What it holds down |
|---|---|---|
| The plan | `folderRun.test.ts` | every reason in order, first match wins; an earlier reason beats `open`; a duplicate counts only among rows that could run; `open` comes from the fact only; `details.name` names the row and fills `{name}`; display order is kept and subfolders are named, not rows; a disabled row cannot refuse; the cap counts every row with a line (open ones too) and ignores the rest; no line reports with no modal, and an empty folder says so |
| The modal | `folderRun.test.ts`, `folderRunCommand.test.ts` | the question holds every line whole (a 400-character line too), `(new)` before the name, the open rows, the rows not run, the subfolders and what each button does, and ends with the chain modal's own sentence; it drops what does not apply; it is shown even when every line is trusted; a dismissal runs nothing and trusts nothing, a trusted line included; approving trusts each typed line though the store rewrites its whole list; the typed line is the approved string even if the entry changed while the modal sat; a name with a control character is not run and draws no row (`a_name_with_a_newline_is_not_run_and_cannot_draw_a_row_in_the_folder_modal`), a line with one is shown escaped and typed unchanged, a `·` stays readable; a question over 4,000 characters, as it would be shown, is refused naming the count |
| The report | `folderRun.test.ts`, `folderRunCommand.test.ts` | the exact sentence, what must not be lost first (`Not run`, then `Left alone`, then `Typed:`), reasons grouped with a closing full stop trimmed, "Typed" and never "started"; information when something was typed and nothing failed, a warning otherwise; a terminal closed for a line that failed says so; a stopped chain says no entry's terminal was closed |
| Left alone, and the re-check | `folderRunCommand.test.ts` | `Run` leaves an open terminal alone and does not trust its line; the open test is ANY shell; a terminal that appeared while the modal sat or while the chain ran moves its row to open; an open row whose terminal closed under `Run` is not run, saying so, and never "already open"; a second click reads what the first typed into as open, and the arrow reads the object the folder run typed into (`folderRunEdges.test.ts`) |
| `Close Open Terminals and Run` | `folderRunCommand.test.ts` | the only button when only open rows exist, both when both kinds do; it closes every live terminal of the name in any shell and types into a NEW one, never a find (`a_new_terminal_never_comes_from_find`); nothing reads the terminal list after a close; a terminal opened while the modal sat is not closed and its row is left alone; one that exited is not disposed and its line is typed; one that started a command is still closed; closing comes after the chain is ready; a refused or declined chain closes nothing |
| The chain | `folderRunCommand.test.ts` | roots are only the lines to be typed; `Close` makes an open row a root; `runDependencies: false` opens no chain; a sibling named under Depends on is neither a step nor awaited, and a refused one refuses nothing; a sibling passes on its own dependencies when a typed line names it; a circle refuses and types nothing; a missing dependency with no step asks nothing more; a failed step the person stops types nothing; the chain modal names the folder, and its missing-row note says to edit the entry that lists the row (`dependencyRun.test.ts` holds the owner's wording unchanged) |
| Refusals and overlap | `folderRunCommand.test.ts`, `folderRun.test.ts` | a non-folder, a non-Terminal folder, a folder in the Trash and no target are refused in code; a second click while a modal or a chain is open says so and does nothing; the next click asks again after a dismissal; another folder is not held up, not even one whose account and folder ids join to the same text with a slash; 33 lines refuse with no modal and 32 run |
| Failures | `folderRunCommand.test.ts` | one failure at opening, or at typing, does not stop the rest and is named in the report; the tracker hears of the send before the keystrokes leave; a pinned entry opens with the pinned shell and a default one without |
| The manifest | `folderRunCommand.test.ts` | the command's title and category, hidden from the palette (`when: "false"`) and on the folder menu |

**Teeth.** Written first, in this order, and watched red for the right reason. The pure suite ran against a stub whose every
function threw `not implemented`: 22 of 22 red. The command suite then ran against a handler that closes by name and at once,
before the chain (the naive way): `closing_comes_after_the_chain_is_ready` failed with `[1] !== [0]` (the recorded `disposed` of
`o` at the moment the chain's step ran), `a_chain_refusal_types_nothing_and_closes_nothing` and
`a_declined_chain_types_nothing_and_closes_nothing` with `[ [ [], 1 ] ]` against `[ [ [], 0 ] ]`,
`a_terminal_opened_while_the_modal_sat_stays_open_and_its_row_is_left_alone` (the newcomer closed: `[ [ [], 1 ], [ [], 1 ] ]`),
and `nothing_reads_the_terminal_list_after_a_close` with `3 !== 0`. That handler closing the one terminal `findEntryTerminal`
answers instead failed `close_closes_every_live_terminal_of_the_name_in_any_shell_and_types_into_a_new_one` (`disposed` `0`,
`0` against `1`, `1`: the pinned road's find saw neither terminal). Then each mutation was applied alone, watched red,
and restored (the source is clean of them):

| # | Mutation | Red |
|---|---|---|
| M1 | `typeLine` asks `findEntryTerminal` first and types into what it finds | `a_new_terminal_never_comes_from_find`, `nothing_reads_the_terminal_list_after_a_close` |
| M2 | `appeared` always false | `a_run_row_whose_terminal_appeared_while_the_modal_sat_moves_to_open`, `a_terminal_opened_while_the_chain_ran_moves_its_row_to_open`, `a_terminal_opened_while_the_modal_sat_stays_open_and_its_row_is_left_alone` |
| M3 | every authorised terminal disposed before the chain | nine: the closing rows of the table above and `a_new_terminal_never_comes_from_find` |
| M4 | the snapshot taken after the modal | `a_terminal_opened_while_the_modal_sat_stays_open_and_its_row_is_left_alone` |
| M5 | the `catch` in `typeLine` rethrows | `one_failure_at_opening_does_not_stop_the_rest`, `one_failure_at_typing_does_not_stop_the_rest` |
| M6 | roots are `linesToType(plan, true)` whichever button | `roots_are_only_the_lines_to_be_typed_a_skipped_entry_does_not_rerun_its_dependencies` |
| M7 | `oneAtATime` ignores the mark | `a_second_click_while_the_modal_is_open_does_nothing`, `a_second_click_while_the_chain_runs_does_nothing` |
| M8 | `isOpen` always false | ten, among them `nothing_reads_the_terminal_list_after_a_close` (which needed a positive assertion, that `o` WAS closed, to go red) |
| M9 | `noteSent` after `sendText` | `the_tracker_hears_of_the_send_before_the_keystrokes_leave` |
| M10 | no `plan.directIds` passed to the chain | the three sibling rows (`a_sibling_named_in_depends_on_is_not_a_step_and_not_awaited`, `a_refused_sibling_named_in_depends_on_does_not_refuse_the_chain`, `a_sibling_passes_on_its_own_dependencies_when_a_typed_line_names_it`) |
| M11 | `trustEach` a `Promise.all` | `all_lines_are_trusted_though_the_store_rewrites_its_whole_list` |
| M12 | `typeLine` closes by name instead of the snapshot | `nothing_reads_the_terminal_list_after_a_close` |
| M13 | `liveTerminalsOfEntry` keeps only terminals with no `shellPath` | `the_open_test_is_any_shell`, `close_closes_every_live_terminal_of_the_name_in_any_shell_and_types_into_a_new_one` |
| M14 | a dismissal runs when every line is trusted | `a_dismissed_modal_runs_nothing_and_trusts_nothing` |
| M15 | `typeLine` disposes without reading `exitStatus` | `an_authorised_terminal_that_exits_while_the_modal_sat_is_not_disposed_and_its_line_is_typed` |

M7 first HUNG the suite rather than failing it: the second click sat in a second held modal and the test awaited it. The test now
answers any extra held modal before awaiting, so a broken mark fails the test and cannot stall the run.

**Not covered, and why.** Driving a real terminal stays not covered, for the reason the sections above give: nothing here drives
VS Code. What a real `dispose` does to `window.terminals`, and how long it takes, is not observed (the world models the worst
case, a terminal that stays listed and live); how a native dialog shows 32 lines (the parent's hand check 8) is not observed;
nothing here reaches the command from a menu: `folderMenu.test.ts` evaluates the menu's `when` clauses, and a menu is not clicked.

The built code brought to §2 and §3 as revised for epoch 2. Run from `src_vs_code`: `npm run compile`, then
`node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs --test out/test/terminalActivity.test.js out/test/busyTerminalGuard.test.js out/test/launcherBusyTerminal.test.js out/test/commandNameRoads.test.js out/test/folderRun.test.js out/test/folderRunCommand.test.js out/test/folderRunEdges.test.js out/test/transitSecretReport.test.js out/test/dependencyRun.test.js out/test/shareDiagnostics.test.js out/test/dependencyChain.test.js`.

| Flow | Test | What it holds down |
|---|---|---|
| `armed`, and evidence only after our own send | `terminalActivity.test.ts`, `busyTerminalGuard.test.ts`, `launcherBusyTerminal.test.ts` | every cell of §2's table; a start and an end with no send of ours never read finished, pure and in the stub world, on the arrow and the launcher; `Type It Anyway` then a seen start and end reads finished; `armed` reads unknown; a terminal the arrow opened asks while its command runs and repeats silently once it ended |
| The escaper | `transitSecretReport.test.ts`, `dependencyRun.test.ts` | C0, DEL, C1, the separators and the bidi controls (U+202A-E, U+2066-9, U+200E, U+200F, U+061C) escaped at both ends of each range, and their neighbours not; `·` readable through `escapeControl` and escaped through `logSafe`, which measures its bound on the escaped field; the chain modal unchanged for clean inputs, and escaped for the owner, each step and each line |
| What a flow creates is listed | `folderRunEdges.test.ts` | a second folder click reads what the first typed into as open; the arrow reads the object the folder run typed into, and types silently once its command is seen finished; after a split name the folder run and the arrow find the same terminal, in either order |
| The closing button | `folderRunEdges.test.ts` | the `open` rows' lines are typed in row order, trusted first and roots of the chain; a closed terminal with no replacement says it was closed; a failure with nothing closed claims no close |
| The title | `commandNameRoads.test.ts` | the arrow's title is `details.name`, not the node's; a history row titles its terminal from that version's `details.name` |
| The in-flight mark | `folderRunEdges.test.ts` | a rejecting trust store: the click rejects, and a second click reaches the modal |

it listed until the close arrives, were read in VS Code 1.93.0's source and not observed. Whether `Terminal.name` keeps a control character
through a reload is not observed, which is why such a name is not run.

## The arrow on the folder row, and the words (2026-11-05, S4 of PLAN_terminal_folder_run.md)

A menu `when` clause is data nothing compiles, so the arrow is proved by evaluating every clause of the REAL `view/item/context` over every
value `folderContextValue` produces. The evaluator reads the five forms the manifest uses, and throws on any other. Run from `src_vs_code`:
`npm run compile`, then `node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs --test out/test/folderMenu.test.js out/test/folderRunHelp.test.js out/test/treeProviderTrash.test.js`.

| Flow | Test | What it holds down |
|---|---|---|
| The token | `folderMenu.test.ts` | the older values byte-identical, `:runall` last, present exactly for an untrashed `terminal` over every flag and every type |
| The menu | `folderMenu.test.ts` | menu(`x:runall`) is menu(`x`) plus `runFolder` on `inline@0` and `2_actions@0`, for all four untrashed values; a companion that the scan found `addEntity`; the whole arrow clause pinned |

| The words | `folderRunHelp.test.ts` | every language holds the labels and the commands byte for byte, says the seven facts in its own words, and renders the headings; `helpCoverage` holds no excuse for the title |

**Teeth.** Each mutation was applied alone, watched red, and restored; the suites run were the three of this story and the guards it can break (`manifest`, `commandsRegistered`, `helpCoverage`, `helpCatalog`, `helpContent`, `listingCoverage`, `readmeClaims`, `sizeRatchet`, `notForExportMenu`, `treeRowText`, `folderRunCommand`).
