`itest:ssh-agent` is unchanged (section 7, the live login window, is E3.4); on Windows it passes with the POSIX-only skip
line, and in a `node:22-alpine` container with `openssh` (non-root user, `CI=true`) sections 5 and 6 RUN and pass.

### The review of E3.3 (Opus + Fable's security judgement, 2026-10-22) — what was added, each watched

New tests: **L13b / L13c** a Git signature, and a SHA-1 RSA login (flags 0 on an RSA key), that WAITED behind a login prompt are not covered by the login window that prompt opens (`w.dialogs.length === 2`) · **L8c** an auto-lock that became overdue while the modal was up: no list, allowed once, told why · **L9** (+1 row) a prompt in the overdue gap offers three buttons and says *… idle auto-lock is about to lock it* · **L19b** the list's time left is the TURN's (two of five minutes spent on the modal read *3 minutes*) · **L21b** a shorter pick under a longer standing login window keeps it, logs one line and tells once · **L6b** a late *Allow longer…* on a vault that locked meanwhile is told it was too late (the aborted check precedes the middle check) · **L20** (+1 row) abort, then a REJECTED list: silent, no presence · **W7b** (+1 value) `'x'` as the setting ends login windows, and every row now also proves the window ENDED rather than was merely hidden (the setting is put back to 60 without an event) · `agentConsent.test.ts` (+1, 2 changed): the overdue reason, and a length the list never offered is `declined-longer` · `sshAgentTexts.test.ts` (+1): `keptLaterLine` / `keptLaterNotice`. **Wording only** (no behaviour; the tests that quote the text changed with it): the list items read *from your pick, about 14:40, or until the vault locks in this VS Code window (idle auto-lock: 60 min)* and `loginWindowsEndedNotice` says *a lock in this VS Code window*.

| Behaviour fixed | RED (verbatim, on the unfixed code) |
|---|---|
| F2 the offer in the overdue gap (`longerOffer` ignored `overdue`) | unit: `actual: { offer: true }, expected: { offer: false, why: 'Longer windows need the vault unlocked — idle auto-lock is about to lock it' }` · **L9** *an auto-lock that is overdue (the lock lands within the minute): the button is absent* — `[ 'Allow once', 'Allow for 10 minutes', 'Allow longer…', 'Deny' ]` · **L8c** *the click was an Allow: this one signature goes ahead* `false` |
| F2 a kept-later pick was silent | **L21b** `actual: [], expected: [ 'Your earlier login window until … still stands — the shorter length you picked was not applied.' ]` |
| F4 an unoffered length allowed once | `actual: { kind: 'once' }, expected: { kind: 'declined-longer' }` (and *Escape on the list*, frozen: `false` where `true`) |
| L4 the listener's `<= 0` | **W7b** *x: the login window ended, not merely hidden* — `{ login: { generation: 1, … } }` where `{}` was expected |

Guards that were green at once (the production code was already right) and then shown to have teeth — each break-it run once and restored:

| Break-it | Red |
|---|---|
| `coveringAt` asks `this.scopes(this.loginLive())`, the claim dropped (M1) | **L13b** *the waiting Git signature asked for itself: the login window did not cover it* `1 !== 2` · **L13c** the same for SHA-1 `1 !== 2` (also L11, L12) |
| the offer's overdue input back to `false` (F2) | L8c and L9 as above |
| `reportKeptLater` not called (F2) | **L21b** the notice and the log line missing |
| `answerFromPick` returns `ONCE` for an unoffered length (F4) | the three `agentConsent` tests above |
| the listener's `<= 0` back (L4) | **W7b** *x: the login window ended, not merely hidden* |
| `longer` checks the middle (eligibility) before `turn.aborted` (L1) | **L6b** *a refused request counts no presence* |
| the list's deadline taken after the modal's await (L2) | **L19b** *five minutes in all, two spent* — `… refused after 5 minutes.` where `3 minutes` was expected |
| `this.stale(pick.kind !== 'escaped')` after the pick (L3) | **L20** *abort, then a rejected list: the message only for a click that meant something* `1 !== 0` |

**Which leg of L17 carries the R4 break-it.** An `await new Promise((r) => setImmediate(r))` between the `turn.aborted` check and the grant was re-run on this head: only the **`setImmediate`** leg goes red (*signed=false but window=true: a pick applied outside its turn*). The test schedules the client's `close` with `setImmediate` right after the pick, i.e. BEFORE the break-it's own `setImmediate` can be queued, so the abort runs first and lands inside the gap. In the **`setImmediate` twice** leg the abort is queued for the second macrotask, so the gap's single hop resumes first and the grant completes before the abort; in the **`setTimeout 0`** leg the timer (clamped to at least 1 ms) fires after the gap's `setImmediate` as well. Those two legs are the guard against a gap of a different size, not the proof of this one; a gap long enough to outlast them is the one thing none of the three sees (see the existing *Not covered* paragraph above).

### The coai code gate and the review of the whole of E3 (2026-10-22) — what changed, each watched

| Behaviour fixed | RED (verbatim, on the unfixed code) |
|---|---|
| `autoLockIsOn` read a string as on (`"60" > 0` is true) — `agentConsent.test.ts` *idle auto-lock is on only for a positive finite NUMBER* | `AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: 60: not a positive finite number reads as off` — `true !== false` (the first non-number, the string `'60'`) |
| the manifest guard did not cover `autoLockMinutes` (it was checked by its own W6, a second list) — `manifest.test.ts`, run against the manifest with the scope REMOVED (scratch edit, restored) | `+ [ 'credSshManager.autoLockMinutes (scope: undefined, restricted: undefined)' ] - []` |

Break-its, each run once and restored:

| Break-it | Red |
|---|---|
| `autoLockIsOn` back to `return minutes > 0;` | *60: not a positive finite number reads as off* `true !== false` |
| `autoLockIsOn` as `typeof minutes === 'number' && minutes > 0` (no `Number.isFinite`) | *Infinity: not a positive finite number reads as off* |
| `autoLockIsOn` with `Number.isFinite` and without the `typeof` | **stays green, on purpose recorded:** `Number.isFinite` does not coerce (`Number.isFinite('60')` is `false`), so the `typeof` is not observable on its own; it stays as the explicit statement of the rule (the code-gate finding asked for it) and `minutes > 0` is the break-it that has teeth |
| the itest's SHA-1 leg answered *Deny* instead of *Allow once* (`ssh-agent-itest.cjs`, container) | `FAIL RSA: and, answered once, the SHA-1 login still succeeds (exit 0): SHA-1 stays signable, it only never gets a long window (owner, 2026-10-21) — exit 255: sign_and_send_pubkey: signing failed` |

**One list, not two.** `REPOSITORY_MUST_NOT_SET` in `manifest.test.ts` now also names `credSshManager.autoLockMinutes`, and a listed setting passes when it is EITHER `machine` + `restricted` OR `application`-scoped (`keepsRepositoriesOut`); the reverse test (every `machine` + `restricted` setting is listed) and the description test (every listed setting carries the **Machine-wide:** clause, `autoLockMinutes` now included) are unchanged. `autoLockSetting.test.ts`'s separate **W6** (the scope) is gone; what it also checked — the manifest default equals the reader's default (60) — stays as W6. Why `application` and not `machine`: see `module_extension.md` (the SSH agent rows).

**The scenario, shown by revert (section 7, E3.4).** The fix landed in E3.3, so the teeth are shown by reverting it: with `coveringAt`'s scopes forced to `['all']` the live scenario prints `FAIL Ed25519: login 2 raised no dialog — 2 dialogs` and 9 checks fail; dropping `PubkeyAcceptedAlgorithms +ssh-rsa` from the RSA leg's sshd config makes the SHA-1 leg fail (the server then refuses the algorithm the client was told to ask for). Section 7 took 1667–2452 ms in the Alpine container (six `sshd -d` runs; 1667 ms on the final run, 67 checks ok).

## A refusal names only what the agent may view (2026-11-11, E1 and E2 of [PLAN_agent_refusal_names_unlisted_entry.md](PLAN_agent_refusal_names_unlisted_entry.md), rules A and B)

Rule A: when the agent may not view the node whose id a request carries, the answer is the answer of an id that does not exist. Two places
prove it, and neither one proves the other's half. Rule B (a refusal that would name a SECOND entry, such as a linked key or a jump host, names
it only when the agent may view it) is proved by part B of the class test, below the harness.

**Part A of the class test, at the door.** `src_vs_code/src/test/agentNamesOnlyViewable.test.ts`, over the vault of
`src_vs_code/src/test/unlistedWorld.ts` (one account, one node for each reason an agent may not view a node: no switch, `mcp: {}`, the
Trash, a PIN of its own, a config entry with no switch; each node has the name `MARK-<id>`, so a leak is a plain text search). The command is
`npm test` (one file: `node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs scripts/run-tests.mjs "out/test/agentNamesOnlyViewable.test.js"`, after
`npm run compile`). The REAL broker runs over the world of `brokerWorld.ts`, with the real `folderHooks`, the real create hook and the real
entry lookup.

| flow | covered | note |
|---|---|---|
| each route of `contract/broker-v1.json` that reads a node id: eight use actions and one verb that is not in the list, delete, the folder verbs, a move, create by folder NAME, the config snippet | **unit, the real broker** | one row each. Each hidden id is sent, then an id of the same length that is not in the vault; the two answers are compared on six items (the plan's section 2.4): the status, the body (after the sent id is replaced), the body's length in bytes, a hidden name or id in the body, a dialog, and the journal lines. A row collects every pair that differs and fails once, with the list; a move whose destination is an entry has its own test |
| the routes are DERIVED from the contract through `contractRoutes.ts` | **unit** | a route with no row, and a route that the table names and the contract does not hold, fail the test, so the file cannot become empty |
| the answers are exactly three sentences, each one the sentence that the same route gives today for an id that does not exist | **unit** | no new sentence |
| each of the five lookups was reached, and a refused lookup took no throttle slot | **unit** | counters in the world |
| the list check: `creds_list` and `creds_folders` hold the four written exceptions (N1 `dependsOn`, N4 `folder`, N5 `command`, N6 `parent`), each one as it is today, and nothing else of a hidden node | **unit** | the four fields are owner questions; the test pins them and does not close them |
| controls: a node that the agent MAY view is named, so the file of negative assertions can fail | **unit** | |
| on a Remote Bridge, an entry in scope that the agent may not view answers as one out of scope and as one that does not exist | **unit** | `bridgeWallWorld.ts` |

**The harness, through the real binary.** `src_vs_code/scripts/creds-mcp-itest.cjs` (`npm run itest:mcp`; it needs `creds-mcp` built, and
prints `SKIP` without it) holds six checks. H1 and H2 are in its folder level (the real `StorageManager` and the real `folderHooks`). H3 to H6
are in `src_vs_code/scripts/lib/mcpTwoWindowsLeg.cjs`: TWO `CredsAgentServer` windows at once, each with its own broker folder, its own real
`StorageManager` and the real `mcpUseHooks` lookup, wrapped only to count its calls. The two announcements are written under distinct
`window-*.json` names with distinct `startedAt`, in a folder that the binary gets as `CREDS_ENDPOINT_DIR`, and the leg asserts that two ports
are discovered and that each answers the health probe, before H3.

| flow | covered | note |
|---|---|---|
| H1: an edit of a folder that nobody opened reads as an edit of a made-up id, and does not hold its name | **binary** | `No CredsForDevs window answered.` with the folder hint; nobody is asked |
| H2: a move into a folder that nobody opened reads as a move into a made-up id | **binary** | `"<folder>" was not moved: there is no folder with that id.`; nothing moved |
| H3: with two windows, a use call on an entry that the newer window hides and the older does not hold | **binary** | `No CredsForDevs window answered for that entry.` with the stale-id hint, no name; each window's lookup called once; nobody asked |
| H4: an id that no window holds | **binary** | the same sentence as H3 and the same counts; the sentence itself is asserted, not H3's text, so H4 stays green when a mutation turns H3 red. A guard |
| H5: an entry that the newer window may view and not use | **binary** | the sentence names the entry and `Usable by agents`; the older window's lookup is NOT called, because a denial stops the walk. A guard |
| H6: the newer window hides an entry that the older window holds with `use` on | **binary** | the walk goes on; the OLDER window answers, raises one dialog that names its own entry, and the answer and the dialog hold nothing of the newer window |
| each leg contributed every check it owns | **binary** | the pattern of the quiet leg: a green run and a run that happened are different claims |

**Watched red, by mutation** (temporary, in `src`, never committed): without the `isOpenFolder` test in `findFolder`, H1 fails; without it in
`moveRefusal`, H2 fails; without the `shown` test in `verdictFor`, H3 fails (the text names the entry) and H6 fails (the newer window's
`denied` stops the walk, and the older lookup is not called). H4 and H5 are guards: green with and without each mutation. The unit mutations
of the plan (its section 5.6) are in the story document.

**Part B of the class test, at the action (rule B, E2).** The second half of the same file, `agentNamesOnlyViewable.test.ts`, over
`src_vs_code/src/test/secondEntryWorld.ts`. The REAL actions run (`sshExecAction` and `execOverSsh`, `rotateAction`, `terminalRunAction`,
`vpnAction`) over the real resolver (`sshCredential.ts`, `sshExecAuth.ts`, `sshOptions.ts`), the real opener and a real vault. Only the I/O edges are
replaced, the way `bridgeLinksWorld.ts` does it: `keyInstaller` writes a real file in a folder that the world owns (and the world checks that it is
removed), `hostKeyTrust`, `sshExecRunner` (it records the argv) and `sshConnect`. The SSH entry `prod` (`e1`) holds a plain key of its own, so that a
call is never refused at the credential step before the chain is read. The entries that the agent may not view are a key entry (a plain key, a key
under a PIN of its own, or a damaged value), a chain of up to five hops, a linked SSH entry, a launcher and a dependency; each one is named
`MARK-<id>` and has an id of 13 characters that is no part of another text, so a leak is a plain text search. The question "may the agent view this id"
is the product's own `agentMayView`. The command is `npm test`; for these files alone, after `npm run compile`:
`node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs scripts/run-tests.mjs "out/test/agentNamesOnlyViewable.test.js" "out/test/agentNaming.test.js" "out/test/sshUseActions.test.js" "out/test/agentSshGate.test.js" "out/test/bridgeLinks.test.js"`.

| flow | covered | note |
|---|---|---|
| the fixture holds what it says: the hidden entries are hidden, the visible ones are viewable, no hidden id is a part of another text, only hop 3 has an unsafe address, a sealed key entry stops the real resolver, and with no second entry the call reaches `ssh` | **unit, the real actions** | proofs 1 to 5 of the story document; a world that proves nothing would make every negative assertion green |
| six rows, one for each reference field: R1 `sshKeyEntityId` (exec with a sealed key entry, exec with a damaged one, `creds_rotate`, a linked run: 4 cases), R2 `jumpHostEntityId` (a hidden unsafe hop at depth 1, 2 and 3 on exec and on a linked run, and one on `creds_rotate`: 7 cases), R3 `sshEntityId`, R4 `vpnLauncherEntityId`, R5 `dependsOn`, R6 `ssh:terminal` | **unit, the real actions** | each case is asserted on the status, the code, the sentence when the case has one, that `ssh` was not started, and that the answer holds no hidden name and no hidden id. A row collects each case that fails and fails once, with the list. R1 and R2 are the rows that were red before the fix. R3 to R6 are guards: the sentence of those roads names the addressed entry only, and they stay green before and after |
| the scan: each `string` or `string[]` field of `EntityMetadata` is a scenario of the table or on the written list of fields that hold no reference to another entry | **unit** | the test reads `types.ts`, takes out comments, and splits on `;`, so a line that holds more than one field is read field by field (two `...ChangedBy` fields share a line). On `b15a5410` it finds **27** fields: 5 reference fields in the table and 22 on the written list (the parent plan wrote 25 and 20). A field that is in neither list fails the test with its name, and so does a name of the written list that the scan does not find. The companions: it still finds the six fields it must, and the vault holds the two fields that R4 and R5 read |
| each row ran | **unit** | each of the six rows made at least one call |
| row R: no agent action resolves a `creds://` reference off a bridge | **unit** | the three action modules import none of `secretRef.ts`, `entityFieldReading.ts` and `runPlan.ts` (the scan finds a known importer, `remoteLaunch.ts`); and a `creds_run` on a Terminal entry whose line holds a reference to a hidden entry gives the reference to the run as text, with no hidden marker in the answer. If an action starts to resolve references, this row is red and rule B needs that site |
| B1, B2: a hidden key entry that stops the call, and a hidden jump host with an unsafe address at depth 1, 2 and 3 | **unit** (`sshUseActions.test.ts`) | the whole body is deep-equal to `errorBody('no_credential', SECOND_ENTRY_REFUSED(<entry>))` and the status is 409; nothing was spawned; exactly one journal line (the relation line); no hidden name and no hidden id in the answer or in the journal. X5: depth 4 on exec and on the linked run. X6: a chain of five hops is refused by the depth cap with the sentence that names the starting entry, and no note |
| B3, B4: the same for a linked `creds_run` | **unit** (`agentSshGate.test.ts`) | the line starts with the Terminal entry; no slot is taken; nothing is run. The fixture row: the same chain with no unsafe hop gets past the refused step to the run |
| B5, B6, X3: the resolvers say WHICH entry a refusal is about | **unit** (`sshOptions.test.ts`, `sshExecAuth.test.ts`, `sshCredential.test.ts`) | `second` is set for an unsafe hop at depth 1 and at depth 3, and for a stopped value of another entry; it is not set for a circle, a missing hop, the depth cap, or a stopped value of the entry that was asked for. `ownerId` is the id of the entry that owns the stopped value (X3) |
| B7: the chooser `agentRefusal` and the fixed sentence | **unit** (`agentNaming.test.ts`) | the module has no import; a hidden second entry gives the fixed sentence and the line of its relation, with neither the id nor the message; a visible one or none gives the message and no note, and nobody is asked when there is no second entry. The table of lines is keyed by `Record<relation, string>`, so a third relation does not compile without its line |
| X4: the journal throws | **unit** | on exec with a hidden key entry and in a linked run the call rejects with the error of the journal; on exec with a hidden unsafe hop the catch answers 500; in no case does the old sentence come back, and no hidden name is in the answer |
| controls: a visible jump host with an unsafe address, a visible key entry with a damaged value, and a visible unsafe hop behind a hidden good hop | **unit** | the whole body is deep-equal to `errorBody('no_credential', m)`, where `m` is what the real resolver gives on the same world, and 0 notes. The third one names hop 2 and not hop 1. Green before the fix and after it |
| G9: on a Remote Bridge the bodies and the notes are as before | **unit** (`bridgeLinks.test.ts`, L2 and L3) | the bridge still gets `BRIDGE_USE_REFUSED` and the exact line of today. The same two worlds on the MAIN port now read the fixed sentence, and the test says that neither the id nor the name is in the body (those two controls pinned the old sentences, so they changed with the fix) |
| G11: a hidden second entry that works stops no call | **unit** | a hidden jump host with a safe address is in the argv (`-J`) and the call answers 200; a hidden key entry with no PIN answers 200, its file was written and is removed; 0 notes in both |

**Watched red, by mutation** (temporary, in `src`, never committed; the code was restored and `git diff` was empty after each): `agentMayView` answers
`true` (B1, B2, B3, C-B, and L7 of part A); `second` not set in `refuseHop` (B2, B5, X5, C-B); `second` not set in `stoppedAuth` (B1, B6, C-B);
`agentRefusal` gives the message back, the no-op (B7, B1, B2, B3, C-B); one name taken off the written list of fields (the scan); the message, and then the id,
put into the note (B4, B7); `ownerId` filled with `''` (X3, and the control of the visible damaged key); no `second` passed at each of the three places
(`prepareAuth`: B1; `execOverSsh`: B2 and X5; `remoteRefusal`: B3 and X5). The first RED run of part B, before the fix, was 11 cases in R1 and R2; the
first failure was `exec, hop 1 of 3: the answer holds MARK-hid-hop-1-x7q and hid-hop-1-x7q: The jump host "MARK-hid-hop-1-x7q" cannot be used: its address is
not a valid host.`, and after the fix the file passed (40 of 40 on that run).

**What none of this proves** (the plan's section 5.8):

- Time. No test measures it: the two cases run the same synchronous code, but a node that exists also walks the folders above it.
- A real `ssh` and a real server: B1 to B3 refuse before anything is started, so no `ssh` runs in part B (`sshExecRunner` only records the argv). So the text that `ssh` prints on a call that answers 200 is NOT checked: it can show the address of a jump host and, for a key that is written to disk, the path of the key file (since rule C `<storageDir>/keys/<pid>/call-<uuid>.key`, with no id: part C, below) (for example `Load key "<path>": invalid format`).
- The path of a borrowed key in the other refusals. A key that cannot be written (`Could not write the stored key to disk: …`, code `internal`) and a run error (`Could not run ssh: …`) can carry the path. Off a bridge those sentences reach the agent as they are (`bridgeLinks.test.ts` pins them); on a bridge they
  are closed. Since rule C the name of the file holds no id: part C, below, proves it on the three doors.
- A reference in a field that is not a `string` or a `string[]`. The scan of part B does not see an object or an array of objects: today `EntityMetadata` has two `CommandArg[]` fields and one `Record<string, string>` field that hold text, and they hold no id of an entry (read on the plan's `360d45b7`); `PortForward[]` is not seen either.
- Sentences outside the sites of the plan's section 3. The class test covers every route of the contract and every reference field that
  exist today; a new sentence on an existing route that names a node by another road is covered only when the fixture reaches it.
- H1 to H6 run only where `creds-mcp` is built. The unit suite proves the window's half (part A) and the binary's decision
  (`CredsMcp.Tests`, `WindowsTests`: `not_found` goes on, `denied` stops) apart; the harness is the one check of the two together.
- The config-snippet lookup stops at the first account that holds the id (N8 of the plan): with two accounts it can answer differently
  for a hidden config and a missing one. Nothing is named and no value is given.

## The path of a call file holds no id (2026-11-12, E1 of [PLAN_agent_refusal_key_file_path.md](PLAN_agent_refusal_key_file_path.md), rule C)

Rules A and B above prove what an answer says about an ENTRY. Rule C proves what an answer says about a FILE: the key file that an agent's
SSH call writes for a borrowed key (an SSH entry that uses the stored key of a second entry) is named `call-<uuid>.key` by
`materializeCallKey` (`keyInstaller.ts`), with `callKeyName()` (`materializedKeys.ts`), so no path that reaches the agent holds the id of a
key entry. The path reaches the agent in three ways: (a) the write fails, (b) the run fails, (c) `ssh` prints it.

**Part C of the class test.** `src_vs_code/src/test/agentKeyPath.test.ts`, over `src_vs_code/src/test/secondEntryWorld.ts` (the world of part
B: the SSH entry `prod` borrows the key of a hidden entry whose id reads like a name, `hid-key-8f3k2`). The world has four options for what the
edge of the world does: `writeFails` (the writer throws what Node throws, with the path it was asked to write), `startFails` (the runner
rejects with a text that holds the whole argv, a stand-in for the worst case), `sshPrints` (the pretended `ssh` answers 255 and prints the
word after `-i` in the two shapes of a real `ssh`), and `pinned` (the real `materializeKnownHosts` writes the pin file). One suite:
`node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs scripts/run-tests.mjs "out/test/agentKeyPath.test.js"`, after `npm run compile`. No row guesses a
name: a path comes from the lists of the world (`attempted`, `materialised`, `spawned`).

| Row | File | What it proves |
|---|---|---|
| P1 to P7, the fixture | `agentKeyPath.test.ts`, three tests (one for each door) | With no option the call reaches `ssh` one time and answers 200; the key file written is the word after `-i`; the key entry is one that the agent may not view; the SSH entry is one that it may view; the world's list of hidden ids holds the id; the id is a part of no other text of the world |
| K1 | the same, three tests | No word of the argv holds a hidden id or a hidden name, and the word after `-i` is the file that was written |
| K2, K3, K4 | the same, nine cells (three ways, three doors: `creds_exec`, `creds_rotate` on an SSH entry, a linked `creds_run`) | Assertion 1 is the one positive: the answer holds the path of the file of this call, or for (a) the path that the writer was asked to write. Then the status and the sentence of today (`internal`, 500, `Could not write the stored key to disk: ` and `Could not run ssh: `; 200 with exit code 255 for (c)). Then no hidden id and no hidden name |
| G5 | the same, two tests (`exec`, `run`) | The pin file of the call carries the id of the SSH entry of the call, which the agent may view. Green before the fix and after it |
| K5 | `sshExecAuth.test.ts` | `resolveExecAuth` with a stored key of a key entry whose id is `deploy-key`: the last part of `keyPath` is a call name and holds nothing of the id |
| K6 | `bridgeLinks.test.ts`, beside L5 | The key path that `ssh` prints, through the bridge and through the main port: the last part is `call-<uuid>.key` and does not start with the id of the key entry; `stderr` is passed on as it was returned. The option `printsKeyPath` of `bridgeLinksWorld.ts` builds the pretended `stderr` from the word after `-i` |
| U1 | `materializedKeys.test.ts` | `callKeyName()` is `call-` and a uuid; two calls give two names; `safeFileComponent` gives the name back with no change; the function has no parameter |
| U2 | `keyInstaller.test.ts`, on the real file system | `materializeCallKey` writes the body with one end-of-line into `keys/<pid>/` as `<a call name>.key`; mode 0600 where the system has modes; two calls, two paths; no parameter for an id |
| U3 | the same | A purge takes a call key with the folder of the window |

**The scan, S1 and S2** (`keysDirPaths.test.ts`, below the older guard of the same file: nothing joins a name into `keys/<pid>/` by hand).
S1: each occurrence of the identifier `materializePrivateKey` in `src_vs_code/src` (comments apart; a call, an import, an import with `as`, a
member of a namespace import) is in a file of a written list: `keyInstaller.ts` (the writer itself and `materializeCallKey`) and `transportFactory.ts` (the git sync of the vault). Each has its reason in the test.
`sshSession.ts` left the list with N17: the key file of Connect, `creds_open_terminal` and the runs over SSH is a call name too. A file
outside the list fails the test with the file and the line. S2 is the companion: the scan finds a call in each of the two files, so a
change of formatting cannot make it match nothing, and the list is compared with these two names, so a third name fails.

*What S1 does not find.* A name built at run time (`mod['materialize' + …]`); a new call inside a file of the list; and the other writers that
name a file from an id through `materializedKeyPath` (`known_hosts-<id>`, `agent-script-<id>`), which are safe today because their entry is
one that the agent may view, but a pin or a file for a hop on the agent's road would not be found. It is a narrow guard against a regression,
not a proof; part C is the proof for the three doors.

*Watched red.* On the unchanged call site the first S1 failure named `sshExecAuth.ts:7` (the import) and `sshExecAuth.ts:162` (the call).
With the options, part C, K5 and K6 written and the call site unchanged, 15 tests failed and no other (S1, K1 on three doors, the nine cells
K2 to K4, K5, K6; the fixture proof and G5 passed); for example K1: `exec: the argv holds hid-key-8f3k2: <folder>/hid-key-8f3k2-<uuid>.key`.
Six mutations each turned a test red: (1) the old line at `sshExecAuth.ts:162` back: 15 red (K1 to K6, S1); (2) `callKeyName()` gives one
fixed name: U1, U2 and the two tests of G7; (3) `sshExecAuth.ts` added to the list of S1: S2 at its first assertion; (4) the `stderr` of
`sshPrints` made a fixed text: the three (c) cells at assertion 1 (the channel is not live), and G5; (5) the id of the key entry taken off the
world's list of hidden ids: the fixture proof and part B's own fixture test; (6) a product file with `import { materializePrivateKey as
writeKey }` and a call: S1, at the line of the import. The full suite after the fix: 7770 tests, 7733 pass, 0 fail, 37 skipped.

**What it does not prove.**

- A real `ssh`. In K4 and K6 `ssh` is pretended: it prints the word that it was given. The fix does not depend on it: no word of the argv holds the
  id (K1).
- Windows. The extension suite runs on Ubuntu only. The ACL step and `ssh.exe` take each file name, by reading. The first real `creds_exec` with a
  stored key on a Windows computer after the release shows it.
- A new thing that throws inside the `try` of `execOverSsh`.
- The sentences of (a) and (b) off a bridge still hold the local path (G2, G3 of the plan). This is a decision, not a gap.
- The person's roads. Since N17 every road of `prepareSshSession` (Connect, `creds_open_terminal`, the runs over SSH) writes `call-<uuid>.key`, so their key file names no longer hold the id of the key entry; only the pin `known_hosts-<id>` (`known_hosts-<id>-<uuid>` when isolated) still holds the id of the SSH entry itself; the askpass script has a fixed name. No test of this plan reads the person's terminal; `sshConnectKeyName.test.ts` (N17) does.

## A bridge, its socket check and the install delete their key file (N16)

The three roads of the person that write a key file through `resolveExecAuth` for a non-interactive `ssh` (the bridge socket check, *Install
creds on host*, *Open Remote Bridge…*) delete the file they wrote: the first two when they finish, a bridge when its `ssh` has ended. The
rule is written once, in `writtenKey.ts`: only a credential with `materialized` is deleted, never a key file of the person's own, a password
or a refusal. The tests run the REAL `registerAgentCommands` and the REAL `SshBridgeManager`, with `ssh` pretended. Run them with
`node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs scripts/run-tests.mjs "out/test/<file>.test.js"`, after `npm run compile`.

| File | Tests | What it holds |
|---|---|---|
| `personRoadsWorld.ts` (not a suite) | n/a | The world of the three roads: `registerAgentCommands` loaded under a stubbed `vscode`, a handler called as a click does. Stand-ins by request string: `../sshExecAuth` (what each resolve answers: `clicks` for a command, `checks` for the socket check), `../sshExecRunner` (no `ssh`; each run is recorded with how many key files were already deleted), `./keyInstaller` (the recorder of `forgetMaterializedKey`; `realForget` leaves the real one), and with `noExecArgv` `../sshExecCommand`. A `heldChild` is a bridge's `ssh` whose end the TEST decides. The host record is untyped on purpose (only the fields the roads read) E2.S2 adds the question about the client as a kind of its own: `client` (one `PinClient` for every question, or the answer of the n-th), `realClient` (the real `./pinClient` asks), `clientAsks` (each question: what it was asked of, the pin files on disk and the runs started at that moment), and the stand-in of `./pinClient`; the question is never one of `runs`, so every count of runs is a count of connections. `KNOWS`, `OLDER` and `unreadable(n)` are the three answers. | **E3.S4 adds:** `realResolve` (the product's own `resolveExecAuth` answers instead of the stand-in), `vault` (a stored key, a key named by its path, or a password, answered by the host's `storage`), `World.said` (every message, quick pick and log line in the order shown) and `World.replaceHostKey` (a new snapshot of the entry from the next read on).
| `personRoadsForgetKey.test.ts` | 15 | C1 to C6, C3b: the socket check deletes its file after a normal check and not before its second run, when the socket is missing, when the mode cannot be read, when it is wider than 600 (the warning is shown), when the host gives no argv and when a run throws; it deletes nothing for a key file of the person (C6). I1 to I7: the install deletes its file after a success (and the install run still had it, and the question was open with the file still there), after a failed install, a timed-out probe, a host that cannot be installed to, a *no*, a question that throws (the same error reaches the caller); nothing for a key file of the person. R1: a real key file in an owned folder is on the disk for both runs of the check and gone when it ends. Every test asserts its road first (the run count, the message, the log line) |
| `writtenKey.test.ts` | 22 | U1 to U8, U3b, with the pin writer of an unpinned entry as the second argument (U9 to U17 are in the G1 section), of `withWrittenKey` and `withWrittenKeyUntilExit` alone: the value, a rejection and a synchronous throw (the same error); nothing deleted for a key file of the person, a password credential (`auth: 'askpass'`, no `materialized`) or a refusal; the file goes only when the process has ended; at once when nothing was started or the start throws; one delete when the attach itself throws (U6); an `exited` that rejects deletes too and leaves no unhandled rejection (U7) E2.S2: the pin writers answer promises and every call of `withWrittenKeyUntilExit` is awaited (U1 to U17 keep every assertion); U18 to U21: both guards hand the writer the login AND the environment of the credential; nothing is started or deleted while the writer has not answered; a writer whose promise rejects is inside the guarded span of both guards; in the source of `withWrittenKeyUntilExit` the one `await` is the decision and none stands between the start and the flag. |
| `bridgeKeyHandoff.test.ts` | 9 | B1 to B9: an open bridge keeps its file and it goes once when its `ssh` ends by itself; *Close* alone deletes nothing, the end of the `ssh` does; a second *Open* of an open bridge starts nothing; a bridge that replaces another (two overlapping *Opens*): the first file goes when the first `ssh` ends and the second stays; no argv, a throw while the argv is built and a start that throws delete at once (the same error); a key file of the person is never deleted; B9 with a real key file: on the disk while the `ssh` runs, gone when it has ended |
| `bridgeSpawner.test.ts` | 12 | P1 to P12, of `bridgeProcessOf` and `bridgeSpawner` (`bridgeSpawner.ts`): `exited` settles with `null` on the `'error'` of a child that never started and with the code on `'exit'`, once, with the first; the `'error'` of a started child settles nothing; the child is started as before (argv copied, the given environment, hidden, no stdio); P6 and P7 with two REAL processes (a program that does not exist: the row goes and the cause is reported; a program that runs and ends: its code and no report); every `'error'` is reported, the same object, and a reporter that throws does not keep `exited` from settling; P8 reads `extension.ts` (it spawns with this spawner and reports to the log); P12 the log line is one sentence and the text of the error, never the argv |

**The common rig of the real-road rows (E3.S4).** *Files.* `scripts/lib/rowRecord.cjs` holds the pure rules (decision 15: what a start of the client, a connection start, an authentication attempt and an accepted login are; the sentences of §3.5; the handed client config; the lines that turn password logins on; E1's words; how a leg process's output line is read). `scripts/itestRowRig.cjs` is the one rig every row-story drives the roads through: the leg process, the leg folders, the counterparts (its one `startServingSshd`), the worlds, the gate, the sampler of `keys/<pid>/`, warm, the switch, the control client, the calls of a leg, the evidence. `scripts/itestRigProof.cjs` proves the parts that no row of E3.S4 uses (RP1 to RP5). `scripts/itestRowM1.cjs` and `scripts/itestRowM6.cjs` are the two rows. *The leg process, and why.* The real resolve hands `ssh` the PROCESS's own environment (F9 of the spec), so every leg runs as `node itestRowRig.cjs --leg <folder>` with the shim first on `PATH` and `HOME` the leg's own folder; section 10 reads its lines (`ok`, `FAIL`, `info`, `evidence`) one by one, and counts each check as its own. `HOME` does not move the client's `~` (OpenSSH takes it from the password database), so the handed config also carries an `IdentityFile` line for a file that is never made and `IdentityAgent none`: without them a password call looks for the person's own `~/.ssh/id_*` (F7, F23). *The real resolve.* Only `vscode` is a stand-in on the rows' roads: the world runs with `realResolve` and `realClient`, the host's `storage` answers a disposable vault, and the observer of the `-V` run, the gate and the recording spawner call the real runner and spawner and pass their answers on. *A counterpart that takes passwords* adds `Match all` and `PasswordAuthentication yes` as the LAST lines (the serving launcher writes `PasswordAuthentication no` first and `sshd` keeps the first value; a counterpart run by the user cannot check a password, so the result is `Failed password`). *The four counts* come from their own sources: the shim's records (starts, the question included; connection starts), and the counterpart's log after the leg's mark (authentication attempts by client port, accepted logins with the kind of their session: the log names the session kind and port, not the command). *Cold and warm* are made by the leg: a new world in a new process is cold; `warm` makes one pinned call in the same world and marks the leg. *The gate* takes a sample at each call's first run and, when asked, holds that run until the leg releases its pin file; *the sampler* reads the keys folder every 50 ms and at each named point; `callsOf` derives the calls from the pin files that the argvs name, never from the folder's names. *The identities:* `recorded` and the shims walk every depth (`m1/k1`), and R7 compares the record with an independent walk. *What it does not prove:* which command an accepted login ran (the log does not say), a forward in the counterpart's log (VERBOSE writes none), a password accepted by the counterpart, the vault's own code, another operating system.

**M1 and M6, seen when they were built (2026-11-24, commit `0f8081b7`; the code of `07a22de1` and `0f8081b7`).** NOT §10 of the plan; the runs pass no row. OpenSSH 10.2p1 for the client and for the unpacked `sshd` (the run of the paragraph above), loopback, Node 26.12.1, Linux (WSL2), a clean tree (`trackedChanges` `""`), `npm run itest:ssh-agent` with both variables, three runs in a row: 411 ok lines and 4 FAIL each, 78 s, 72 s and 72 s; section 10 is 338 checks of them (RP 42, M1 23, M6 166); 16 leg processes, each ended with 0; R1 to R7 ok; after each run no `creds-pin` folder, no `/tmp/creds-*` and no process left. *M1* (leg `m1`, a key named by its path, cold, K1 with the stand-in): every check M1.2 to M1.19 ok in each run: 6 starts of the client (one question and five connection starts), 5 accepted logins (4 with a command session, 1 with none), the 3 pin paths, the stand-in's four conditions. The wait of the one question `ssh -V` (C20, the wait of a normal run): 17.9 ms, 12.6 ms and 17.4 ms. *M6* (ten legs): every check ok except M6.11 in the four legs where a bridge bound its socket (`m6-stored-bridge`, `m6-named-bridge`, `m6-stored-replaced`, `m6-named-replaced`), in each of the three runs. **M6.11, kept as the plan words it, is red, and what it says:** after the last end the dead remote `-R` socket file is still there; the info lines of the same runs say (a) the file exists and (b) nothing listens on it (`ss -lx`, the product's own liveness test): in the key legs of the bridge and of the replaced bridge the file stays (in the replaced leg the first spawned bridge's file is gone: it never bound), in the password legs and the K2 leg it is gone (the bridge never bound). It is the sshd that leaves it (measured apart from the harness: SIGTERM, SIGINT and SIGHUP, the file still there 3 s after the client exited), and the product knowingly leaves it, to be swept by the next bridge's check (`sweepCommand`). Whether M6 means "no live forward" or "no file" is open (the lead, the owner). *M6.13, the password entry with K1:* `Failed password` in every run (the counterpart cannot check a password): 2 lines in `m6-password-installs`, 3 in `m6-password-bridge`, 5 in `m6-password-replaced`, 0 `Accepted password`; the connection starts of the replaced leg were 6, 5 and 5 (the first bridge is replaced while it starts). *M6.14, K2:* a connection for each of the 4 connection starts, 0 authentication attempts, each run 255 with the verification line. *RP1 to RP5* all ok (warm: 0 questions and one connection start after the pin was replaced, 255, sentence C once; the switch, whole and listener-only; the control client against K1 (0, one accepted login) and K2 (255, no attempt); the real resolve of each credential through the shim).

**M6.11 after the owner's reading (2026-11-24, commit `1ad5736b`).** The owner read "no forwarded socket left" as no LIVE forward: the check now asks that nothing listens on any bridge socket of the leg after the last end (`ss -lx`, as the product's own sweep decides liveness; a missing `ss` fails it), and the dead socket file that `sshd` leaves (`StreamLocalBindUnlink no`) stays an info line. One proving run from the clean commit `1ad5736b` on the unpacked `sshd` 10.2p1: exit 0, 415 ok lines, 0 FAIL; M6.11 ok in all ten M6 legs (0, 1 or 2 bridge sockets each); the info lines say "(a) the file exists; (b) nothing listens on it" for the five legs whose bridge bound its socket, as in the three runs before. A hand control: `ss -lx` listed a live unix socket by its full path and did not list it after close.
