*Mutations* (each alone, restored byte for byte, `git diff` empty after, none committed). On the unit test: MU1 red RR1; MU2 and MU3 red RR2; MU4 red RR3; MU5 red RR4 and RR4b; MU6 (a row starts a counterpart; the rig names a stand-in of the resolve) red RR6 and RR7 respectively; MU8 (`byStoredKey` answers no `materialized`) red RR8 (`call-<uuid>.key` stays after the click). MU7 (`recorded` reads one level again) in a proving run: R7 FAILs (and R1, as a tracked file is then modified).

*Hand checks* (the product made to do the wrong thing; each in the story's tree (a deviation: spec §12 says a scratch worktree; no product file was in the range of the commits, and the review of E3.S4 asked for HC5 again in a scratch worktree, below), one run each; R1 FAILs in each, as it must; hand checks, never results). HC1 (`StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new` for a pin; spec §12's expected-red column for it was wrong: HC1's own leg is M1.10, and the hand check of M6.14 and of RP1 to RP4 is HC1b, `no`): M1.10, M1.11, M6.2, M6.3 and M6.6 red, legs 1 and 2 of section 10 red, RP5 red; M6.14 and RP1 to RP4 stay green, because `accept-new` still refuses a CHANGED key (the spec expected them red; the pin file holds K1's key for K2's port, and a client with `accept-new` refuses that). HC1b (`StrictHostKeyChecking=no`): additionally M6.14, RP1, RP2, RP3 and RP4 red, and every M1 check from M1.2 on. HC2 (the asker keeps no answer): M1.8, M1.9 and RP1 red (three questions in place of one), legs 1 and 2 red. HC3 (every pin at one name): M1.11, M6.2 to M6.7 and M6.12, M6.13 red. HC4 (the pin file is not deleted): M6.5, M6.6 and the "0 files of these calls" check red, M1 and RP5 too. HC5 (a credential with no `materialized` is deleted too): the person's key file is deleted by M1, which runs first, so every later leg stops at its first read of that file; M6.8 itself is not reached (the key file is shared by every leg: a leg of its own copy would see it). HC6 (`byStoredKey` answers no `materialized`; MU8 on the real `sshd`): M6.5, M6.6 and the "0 files" check of the stored-key legs, and RP5 red.

**E3.S4 review fixes (2026-11-24, commits `b061a030`, `464968aa`, `4862ac47`, `5a4b1820` and `1a015583`; no pass criterion changed).** (1) Each leg now holds a copy of the person's key file and its `.pub` in its own folder (mode 0600), and the key itself is a pair of its own for the rows (`rows_user_key`, made by `rowsTop`): the section's key is used by legs 1 and 2 of section 10, and under HC5 they deleted it, so no row reached M6.8; M6.8 now reads `absent` for a missing file and FAILS as a check. (2) `openRoads` gives the stand-in resolve no credential (`clicks: []`, `checks: []`): a fall-back to it fails loudly. (3) M1.9 also requires that the question ENDED before the first connection started (the observers' clock: the shim's start times are ticks of 10 ms). (4) After the last end `keys/<pid>/` may hold only `known_hosts-e1` and, in a password leg, `askpass.sh` (the closed list; M4 (d) names the one other kind it records, C14). (5) M6.6 asks the number of ended calls exactly (`!==`); it showed that in the password legs of the bridge and replaced scenarios the bridge ends by itself (its login is refused), so those scenarios take O2 after that end and say the exact number. (6) A check name must not hold " — " (it splits a FAIL line): M6.11's name lost it, and the leg's `check` throws on any name that holds one. (7) The M6 (d) check now reads the probe (255). *HC5 again, in a scratch worktree of `1a015583`* (a credential with no `materialized` is deleted too; R1 FAILs there, as it must): M6.8 red in `m6-named-installs`, `m6-named-bridge` and `m6-named-replaced` (with M6.12 and M6.6 of the same legs); in the run before the fix of `1a015583` the leg died on the read of the deleted file instead. These were hand checks, never results.

**M4 and M9 on the rig (E3.S5).** *Files.* `scripts/lib/refusedRecord.cjs` holds the pure rules (the table of the 25 legs, the message that a refused leg expects read from the product's `unusablePinSentence` and `pinWriteFailedSentence`, and the judge of R.1 to R.5 over the counts of the rig); `scripts/itestRowRefused.cjs` is the refused leg that M4 (b)-(d) and M9 share (and the liveness control, `toolOnPath`, the write probe of (d)); `scripts/itestRowM4.cjs` has the 15 legs of M4 ((a) on the five runs of M1's drive), `scripts/itestRowM9.cjs` the 10 legs of M9 (the eight road legs, Connect's repair, the C1 record). *What makes each case.* No product code is changed and nothing is patched: (b) the entry's `hostKey` is `not a host key` (`World.replaceHostKey`); (c) the entry keeps K1's text and its `host` becomes `not a host` (`World.replaceHost`: `isSafeSshHost` refuses the space, so no known-hosts line can be formed); (d) the window's key folder `keys/<pid>/` is made readable and not writable (mode 0500), so the product's own `materializeKnownHosts` throws `EACCES` at its write, on a key named by its path (a stored key or a password would be refused by its own credential first, which writes into the same folder). The leg first writes a probe file into that folder and must see `EACCES` (R4.d0); as root the write goes through and the leg fails as "could not fail" (the CI runner is not root: its owned folders are `testsToDelete-u1001-…`; R4.d0 shows it on CI). The mode is put back in a `finally`, before the end checks. *One road per refused leg and one leg per state:* the refused cases run on the install and on the bridge, cold and warm, each in a world, a process and a counterpart of its own (a second refused call in one cold world finds the answer that the first kept and starts nothing). The socket check is never reached in these legs: its judge reads 0 starts of any kind, and the record shows it. *Counts.* Per leg the four counts of decision 15 from the shim and from K1's log, and the order of the question `ssh -V` and the sentence (the observers' one clock, `performance.now()`); cold exactly one `-V` that ended before the sentence, warm exactly 0 starts of any kind. *The liveness control:* after the counts the real client by its path signs in to the same K1 and K1 logs one more accepted login, so "0 connections" cannot come from a counterpart that was down (its checked pin asks the client `-V` through the shim and its login is in K1's log, both after the counts: the evidence line carries the counts the checks judged). *Connect's repair* (`m9-key-repair`, the repair by the person as sentence A's key form advises it; no product change): the state of the key legs in a new world (the lead's answer Q1: the refused roads are the four key legs, each in a world of its own; M1's "exactly one question" cannot hold in a world that already asked), `connectionOptions` with the real scan of K1 (the rig hands the world the real `runBounded`: D1), *Trust and connect* answered only to a question that holds K1's fingerprint; the one update holds K1's `<algorithm> <base64>` as K1's `.pub` holds it; Connect's file `known_hosts-e1` holds K1's line and is removed by its exact path (its session would delete it); then the runs of M1 in the SAME world, cold, with every check of M1 under the label `M9 m9-key-repair: M1`. A password entry gets the password form and the same numbers, and no password attempt in K1's log (R.8). *Records, not judged:* C9 (the bridge's command mints its token before the refusal), C14 (a pin file that a failed write left) and C1 (`m9-password-connect`: with the scan given no answer Connect's options answer "go on", name no pin file, save no pin and ask nothing: for a password entry Connect would then send the password with `accept-new`; no leg goes past the options). The program of Connect's scan is named in a `tool.json` and the record of identities holds `ssh-keyscan` with its path, its SHA-256 and the version of the Debian package that holds it (`dpkg -S`, `dpkg-query`; `versionSource: "dpkg"`: it has no `-V`), checked by R8. *What it does not prove:* no row passes (§10 is E3.S10's); the socket check's own refusal (its unit tests hold it); a partly written pin file (an `EACCES` at the `open` writes nothing); whether a minted token can be used after the refusal; Connect's session after its options; a root runner, another system, Windows.

**M4 and M9, seen when they were built (2026-11-24, commit `1a015583`; the code of `edda3461`, `a583a64d` and the review fixes above).** NOT §10 of the plan; the runs pass no row. OpenSSH 10.2p1 for the client, for `ssh-keyscan` and for the unpacked `sshd` (`CREDS_ITEST_SSHD` and `CREDS_ITEST_SSHD_CONFIG`), loopback, Node 26.12.1, Linux (WSL2), a clean tree (`trackedChanges` `""`), `npm run itest:ssh-agent`, three runs in a row: exit 0, 869 ok lines and 0 FAIL in each (M1 24, M4 265, M6 176, M9 177 of them), about 3 minutes each; 41 leg processes, each ended with 0; R1 to R8 ok; after each run no `/tmp/creds-*` name of the run, no process of the run, no `0500` folder. *Per leg* (evidence lines of run 1; the three runs agree): `m4-a-none`, `m4-a-empty`, `m4-a-space`: 5 starts, all connection starts, 0 with `-V`, 5 accepted logins in K1's log. Each of the 20 refused legs: 0 connection starts, 0 connections and 0 authentication attempts in K1's log, 0 bridges; the 10 cold ones exactly 1 start (the question `-V`, 10.7 ms to 27.4 ms over the three runs, ended 0.45 ms to 1.79 ms before the sentence was shown), the 10 warm ones 0 starts of any kind; the liveness control: exit 0 and ONE more accepted login in each. `m9-key-repair`: Connect asked ONE question (K1's fingerprint, the button *Trust and connect*), the storage got ONE update that equals K1's `.pub`, Connect's file held K1's line for `[127.0.0.1]:<port>`; Connect's scan was 5 connections and 0 authentication attempts in K1's log; Connect asked the client nothing; then M1's checks (M1.2 to M1.19) all ok in that world, 6 starts (1 question, 5 connection starts), 5 accepted logins, the question 11.5 ms to 20.3 ms over the three runs. `m9-password-connect` (C1): "Connect's options answered go on, named no pin file, saved no pin, asked 0 questions"; the shim recorded 0 starts. C9: in each of the 10 bridge legs (6 of M4, 4 of M9) "the bridge's command minted 1 token(s); the first before the refusal message: yes", in all three runs. C14: "pin files that the failed write left: none" in the four (d) legs. *The identities line's new entries* (home as `~`, user as `<user>`): `{"role":"ssh-keyscan, the scan of Connect's options, named by the leg m9-key-repair","path":"/usr/bin/ssh-keyscan","sha256":"1d4db24a1a24c276c81b3ac184ac7d72a29b68d3e604240e7140dc5334d5d1bc","version":"1:10.2p1-2ubuntu3.8","versionSource":"dpkg","package":"openssh-client"}` and the same for `m9-password-connect`. *Mutations* (each alone, restored byte for byte, none committed): MV1 to MV4, MV7 and MV9's unit half turned RF2, RF2, RF3, RF3, RF1 and RF8 red; MV5 (`replaceHost` changes the snapshot in place) RF4; MV6 (`updateDetailsFields` keeps the old snapshot) RF6; on the real `sshd` in a scratch worktree MV8 (`tool.json` not read) turned R8 red and MV9 (the observer answers `runSshExec` alone) turned R9.r1 red. *Sizes.* Lines of the files at `edda3461` and after the review of E3.S5 (all at most 800): `scripts/lib/refusedRecord.cjs` 96 (104), `scripts/itestRowRefused.cjs` 199 (204), `scripts/itestRowM4.cjs` 108, `scripts/itestRowM9.cjs` 135 (144), `scripts/itestRowM1.cjs` 178, `scripts/itestRowM6.cjs` 235, `scripts/itestRowRig.cjs` 620 (622), `scripts/itestIdentities.cjs` 167, `scripts/itestPinnedRoads.cjs` 555, `src/test/personRoadsWorld.ts` 434, `src/test/itestRowsRefused.test.ts` 354 (the spec's budget was 340: a deviation, taken by the test's own helpers; 372 with RF3b). The commit body of `edda3461` carries no line counts; they are here. *The review of E3.S5 (PASS WITH FIXES), one commit after `dde170af`:* R.6b (above); R.7 and C14 read only the samples taken after the leg's mark, not those of the warming call; M6.8 also requires that the key's hash was read (`absent` cannot pass); R9.r1 and R9.r2 also compare, apart from the product, the first two fields of K1's `.pub` with the stored text and `ssh-keygen -lf` of K1's `.pub` with the fingerprint; `warm` in a password leg waits for K1's authentication attempt, not for an accepted login; the note that this story had added to the E3.S4 spec is removed (the spec keeps no ledger; the facts stay in this file).

*Hand checks* (the product made to do the wrong thing, in a scratch worktree of the commit; R1 FAILs there, as it must; two on one run where their legs do not overlap; hand checks, never results): HC1 (`pinAbsent` true only for `undefined`) and HC8 (`confirmHostKey` goes on with no pin for a first contact), one run: `m4-a-empty` and `m4-a-space` red in A.2, A.3, A.4, A.5, A.7 and A.8 (the entry is refused with A: the spec named A.2, A.3 and A.5), `m9-key-repair` red in R9.r1, R9.r2 and R9.r3 and M1.3 on. HC2 (`writePersonPin` answers ok for `unusable`) and HC3 (`written` answers none for a throw), one run: 109 FAIL: (b) and the `m9-key-*` legs red in R.1, R.3 and R.6, (c) red in R.6, (d) red in R.1, R.3 and R.6. Each `m9-password-install-*` leg: R.1, R.3, R.5 (cold: not exactly one `-V`; warm: one start, not 0), R.6 and R.8 (K1's `Failed password` of an unpinned call); each `m9-password-bridge-*` leg also R.2 and R.4. HC4 (the pin is written before the question) and HC6 shared one run; their reds are in different checks: HC4 turned R.5 red in every one of the 10 cold refused legs. HC6 as the spec worded it (`unusablePinSentence` itself gives the password entry the key form) stayed GREEN at first: the expected message is read from the same product function, so R.6 could not see a change of the sentence's text. The review of E3.S5 asked for R.6b: a password leg also requires that the expected text is not `unusablePinSentence(name, 'key')` and that the shown text never holds "Trust and connect" (RF3b holds both, with two mutants). HC6 as worded, run again alone on the real `sshd` in a scratch worktree of the tree with R.6b: R.6b red in the four `m9-password-*` road legs (868 ok, 5 FAIL: those four and R1); a wrong CHOICE of the form (`writePersonPin` hands `refusalOf` the key form for a password) had turned R.6 red in the same four legs. HC5 (`pinClientAsker` keeps no answer) and HC7 (`persistPin` writes nothing), one run: HC5 turned R.5 red in all 10 warm refused legs (and M1.8, M1.9 and RP of the earlier legs); HC7 turned R9.r2 red in `m9-key-repair` and M1.3 on (the install is refused with A on the unrepaired entry).

**The apostrophes of a bare word on an older client (the lead, 2026-11-25; the cause of plan change 3.2).** The client of probe P3 (OpenSSH 8.4p1, Debian `8.4p1-5+deb11u3`, unpacked from the two packages of `~/.cache/creds-itest-oldssh/deb`, both checked against their `SHA256SUMS`) and the client of the rows (10.2p1), each with `HOME` an owned folder and `-F /dev/null`, `ssh -G` only (no connection): `-o "UserKnownHostsFile=/tmp/kh/x'y'z"` gives `userknownhostsfile /tmp/kh/x'y'z` on 8.4p1 and `/tmp/kh/xyz` on 10.2p1; `-o "UserKnownHostsFile=\"/tmp/kh/x'y'z\""` gives `/tmp/kh/x'y'z` on both. So an older client reads a bare word with a pair of apostrophes as the one intended file: M7's collision does not exist there, and M11 (b)'s "M7's control showing acceptance through the colliding file" cannot be measured as written (decision 18). The E3.S9 spec writer had predicted it from the source (8.4 reads a value with `strdelim`, which knows double quotes only). A measurement, not a row.

**After the review of E3.S5 (2026-11-24, commit `c9d0279e`).** One proving run on a clean tree (`trackedChanges` `""`), the same set-up as above: exit 0, 873 ok lines and 0 FAIL (M1 24, M4 265, M6 176, M9 181: R.6b in the four password legs, the new pins of R9.r1 and R9.r2); 41 leg processes, each ended with 0; R1 to R8 ok; after it no `/tmp/creds-*` name, no process, no scratch worktree. CI (`ci-extension.yml`, run 45406155481, GitHub's own `sshd`, OpenSSH 10.2p1, uid 1001): all four jobs green; the unit suite 8440 tests, 8405 pass, 0 fail; the integration run with M1, M4, M6 and M9 and no FAIL line; R4.d0 ok in the four (d) legs: the runner is not root and the write into the `0500` folder throws `EACCES` there (the lead's answer Q2, now seen). *HC1 of E3.S4, the owner's reading (2026-11-24):* that HC1 turned M1.10 and M1.11 red and left M6.14 and RP1 to RP4 green (they turned red under HC1b) is a record of what was seen, not a plan change: every row and every instrument ran as written and no pass criterion changed. The consultant could not be asked: coai refused every call (its data folder was not found) and the owner decided.

**M2, M3 and M8 on the rig (E3.S6).** *Files.* `scripts/lib/otherKeyRecord.cjs` holds the pure rules (the table of the seven legs; the words of the controls and the witnesses; a known-hosts line made from a `.pub`; the sessions of a log; the judge of the four refused runs; the recording command and the reading of its records); `scripts/itestRowOtherKey.cjs` what the three rows share; `scripts/itestRowM2.cjs` (four legs), `scripts/itestRowM3.cjs` (two) and `scripts/itestRowM8.cjs` (one). *The seven legs.* In each the entry is pinned to ONE key and the host at its address presents ANOTHER, which one more source of trust would accept: `m2-mismatch-user` and `m2-mismatch-global` (K2 answers from the start; the three roads are refused: four runs start, no install run), `m2-switch-user` and `m2-switch-global` (K1 answers the probe, the install's question is held, K1 is ended whole and K2 takes its port; the install run is refused), `m3-mismatch` (the entry is pinned to K2, K1 answers, a master to K1 is up) and `m3-switch` (K1 answers the probe, K1's LISTENER alone is ended so that its master's session stays, K2 takes its port), and `m8` (K2 answers, the config names a `KnownHostsCommand` that prints K2's line). Each leg is cold, in its own process, world, counterpart(s), key copy and handed config. *Every other source is made by the client config that the shim hands the real `ssh` (`-F`), in the leg's own folder, never by the person's `~/.ssh`:* `handedClientConfig` takes an optional second parameter, and the known-hosts value goes IN PLACE of `/dev/null` (the first value wins, so a line added after it would change nothing); M2's file (a) is the `.ssh` folder of the leg's own home folder and file (b) is `<leg>/known_hosts_global`. The client's `~` is the person's real home, not `HOME` (S4.F23), so (a) is the USER-FILE SLOT that the config names, not the compiled-in default (the owner's answer A1, 2026-11-24); check M2.0 shows each by `ssh -G` of the real client and compares it with the leg's own paths. *The words of the controls and the witnesses* are literal (E1's six words, the two pairs of before E1, `StrictHostKeyChecking=yes` alone, G2's two options) or the road's own recorded argv with the path of the two known-hosts options replaced by a pin file that the lib writes from a `.pub` (the lead's answer A3); never `hostKeyWords`, so a hand check of it cannot change a control. They run after the counts (M8: before the mark), by the real client's path, so their logins are no logins of a road. *The bridge's verification line* is read from the bridge's OWN stderr: the product starts the bridge with `stdio: 'ignore'`, so the shim keeps a client's stderr in `<records>/<pid>.stderr` only when it is `/dev/null` (the lead's answer A2: an instrument like the shim's record; a stderr that the product reads is never taken). A file that is not there fails the judge. *"0 forwards"* is the bridge's remote socket: after the bridge's `ssh` ended its path does not exist and `ss -lx` lists nothing at it (read while the master is up in M3). *The master* (M3) is made by the real client through the handed config (`ControlMaster auto`, a control path in the leg's folder, `ControlPersist 120`), checked by `ssh -O check`, in no count of the leg, and ended first (`-O exit`, then its pid by its start time). *The recording command* (M8) is a `sh` script in `<leg>/khc/` that records each start (pid, parent pid, start time, argv) and prints K2's line; it is a config text of the record of identities (R9). Every counterpart carries the install's stand-in; each leg reads its marker and the listing (§1.8 of the spec). *What it does not prove.* No row passes (§10 is E3.S10's); the client's compiled-in default user file (it is in the person's real home); a forward in the counterpart's log (VERBOSE names none: the socket stands for it); which command an accepted login ran, beyond the session's kind; which process starts the `KnownHostsCommand` (its parent pid is recorded, not judged); the version of `sh`; the real `/etc/ssh/ssh_known_hosts` (M2 (b) names an owned file); a root runner, another operating system, Windows.

**M2, M3 and M8, seen when they were built (2026-11-24, commit `3c2ebef2`).** NOT §10 of the plan; the runs pass no row. OpenSSH 10.2p1 for the client and for the unpacked `sshd` (`CREDS_ITEST_SSHD` and `CREDS_ITEST_SSHD_CONFIG`), loopback, Node 26.12.1, Linux (WSL2), a clean tree (`trackedChanges` `""`), `npm run itest:ssh-agent`, three runs in a row: exit 0, 991 ok lines and 0 FAIL in each (M1 24, M2 64, M3 39, M4 265, M6 176, M8 14, M9 181, RP 42 of them), 189 s, 187 s and 173 s; 48 leg processes (the 41 of before and 7 new), each ended with 0; R1 to R9 ok (R9: the one `known_hosts_command` of the run is in the record with its SHA-256); after each run no `/tmp/creds-*` name, no process, no master, no control socket. *Per leg* (evidence lines; the three runs agree in every count): every leg is cold with ONE question `ssh -V` (12.9 ms to 56.6 ms over the runs). `m2-mismatch-user` and `m2-mismatch-global`: 5 starts (the question and four connection starts: the probe, the bridge, the sweep, the mode check), no install start; the three runner runs ended with 255 and the whole line `Host key verification failed.`; the bridge ended with 255 and its own stderr file holds the real client's `REMOTE HOST IDENTIFICATION HAS CHANGED` banner and the line once; K2 logged 4 connections, 0 authentication attempts and 0 accepted logins; the bridge's socket path does not exist and nothing listens on it; M2.0: the client resolved the user file to the `.ssh` folder of `<leg>/home` (a) and the global file to `<leg>/known_hosts_global` (b), the other one `/dev/null`; the control of (b) (the two pairs of before E1) exit 0 and one more accepted login with a command session in K2's log; the witness of (a) (`StrictHostKeyChecking=yes` alone) the same. `m2-switch-user` and `m2-switch-global`: 3 starts (the question, the probe, the install run); the probe ended with 0, the install run with 255 and the line; K1 logged 1 connection, 1 authentication attempt and 1 accepted login with a command session; K2 logged 1 connection, 0 attempts and 0 accepted logins; the marker was not written; the control and the witness exit 0 against K2 at the port. What the install showed after the answer, word for word (one error, no host-key sentence): `Could not install on "box": the installer produced no result and exited 255.` `m3-mismatch`: 5 starts as the M2 mismatch; each connection start carried `ControlPath=none` and `ControlMaster=no` once; K1 logged 4 connections, 0 attempts, 0 accepted logins and no `Starting session` line after the mark; the master answered `ssh -O check` with 0 before the roads and after them; the witness (the probe's six words pinned to K2's line, no G2 option) exit 0, printed its nonce and a `PATH` that starts with the stand-in's folder (so a session through the master gets the stand-in's `PATH`, H18 seen), and K1 logged one new command session, no new connection and no new accepted login. `m3-switch`: the master was alive directly before the answer and after the install run (0 and 0); the install run 255 with the line and G2's two options; between the switch and the end of the run K1 logged no session and no accepted login (its log line for the control's session did arrive after the listener-only stop: H11 seen); K2 logged 1 connection, 0 attempts, 0 accepted logins; the control (the install run's six pairs, its pin file replaced by a file of K1's line, no G2 option) exit 0 THROUGH the master, printed its nonce and the stand-in's `PATH`, K1 logged one new command session and no new accepted login, K2 no new connection. `m8`: the control first (E1's words, a file of K1's line) exit 0 with one accepted login in K2's log, and the command's record held 2 starts (both with the same parent pid, recorded and not judged); the roads: 5 starts, four connection starts each with `KnownHostsCommand=none` once, the three runs and the bridge 255 with the line, the command's record 2 before and 2 after the roads (0 starts for them), K2 4 connections, 0 attempts, 0 accepted logins. The command lives under the owned folder in `/tmp` and the client ran it (H16 seen: no `Unsafe KnownHostsCommand`). *Mutations* (each alone, saved first, restored byte for byte, none committed): MO1 to MO8 turned OT3, OT2 and OT2b, OT4, OT5, OT5, OT6, OT7 and OT1 red; MO9 (`known_hosts_command` out of the record's config names), in a scratch worktree, left R9 red (`{"commands":1,"recorded":[]}`) beside R1. *Hand checks* (the product made to do the wrong thing, in a scratch worktree of the commit with the `.agents/conventions` snapshot copied in; R1 FAILs there, as it must; hand checks, never results; HC1 and HC3 shared the first run as the spec allowed, and the other runs held one hand check each, because the legs overlap through the words that a hand check changes): **HC1 and HC3 shared one run first** (hostKeyWords with no `UserKnownHostsFile` pair and with no `KnownHostsCommand=none`): `m2-mismatch-user` red in M2.1, M2.2, M2.3, M2.4 and M2.5 (the probe read the file of (a), K2 was accepted, the install ran the stand-in) and `m2-switch-user` in M2.s3, M2.s4 and M2.s5, as predicted; `m8` red in M8.1 to M8.5, as predicted; the `-global` legs all green, as predicted. NOT as predicted: (1) both M3 legs stopped with an exception, not a check: the witness and the control take the road's RECORDED words, and `withFile` throws for words with no `UserKnownHostsFile` ("replaced 0 and 1"), so M3 was not green; (2) a red check beside the predicted ones in `m2-mismatch-user` and `m8`: *keys/<pid>/ holds nothing but known_hosts-e1* (the rig's `pinOf` names a call's pin file only from ONE path as both files, so the bridge's pin file, left after `bridges.dispose()` of the open bridge, had no owner; `m8` shared the HC1 words). **HC3 alone** (a second run): `m8` red in M8.1 to M8.5 exactly, M8.0 and M8.c green, every check of the M2 legs green; NOT as predicted: `m3-mismatch` M3.7 and `m3-switch` M3.s7 red (exit 0 and the nonce, but their own check that the six options of E1 and E2 are in the recorded words found no `KnownHostsCommand`), the rest of M3 green. **HC2** (no `GlobalKnownHostsFile` pair), alone: `m2-mismatch-global` red in M2.1 to M2.5 and `m2-switch-global` in M2.s3 to M2.s5, as predicted; the `-user` legs and `m8` all green, as predicted; NOT as predicted: both M3 legs stopped with an exception (`withFile`, "replaced 1 and 0"), and `m2-mismatch-global` also red in the closed-list check of the keys folder (as above). **HC4** (no G2 words in the runs and in the bridge): exactly as predicted: `m3-mismatch` red in M3.1, M3.2, M3.3, M3.4, M3.5 and M3.8, green in M3.0, M3.6 and M3.7; `m3-switch` red in M3.s3, M3.s5 and M3.s8, green in M3.0, M3.s1, M3.s2, M3.s4, M3.s6 and M3.s7 (the control rode the master); every check of M2 and M8 green. The bridge that rides a master ended with 0 (not predicted by the spec: the exit code; seen: 0). Run once M3.8 and M3.s8 were green in the three proving runs. Reds outside the seven legs, listed as seen and no part of the prediction: HC1 and HC3: the legs of M4, M9, M6 and RP, `leg 1` and `leg 2` of section 10 and M1.10, M1.11 (115 FAIL in all, 514 s); HC2 likewise (107 FAIL, 490 s); HC3 alone: `leg 1`, `leg 2`, M1.10 and M9's M1 (12 FAIL, 179 s); HC4: 16 checks of section 9's G2 tests (`P-B`, `P-D1` and the like) and R1 (26 FAIL, 176 s). Nothing was left after any run: no `/tmp/creds-*` name, no process, and the scratch worktree was removed. *Sizes.* Lines at `3c2ebef2` (all at most 800; the spec's budget in brackets): `scripts/lib/otherKeyRecord.cjs` 155 (200), `scripts/itestRowOtherKey.cjs` 175 (380), `scripts/itestRowM2.cjs` 143 (220), `scripts/itestRowM3.cjs` 166 (320), `scripts/itestRowM8.cjs` 90 (180), `src/test/itestRowsOtherKey.test.ts` 329 (340), `scripts/itestRowRig.cjs` 622 to 672 (700), `scripts/lib/rowRecord.cjs` 166 to 168 (185), `scripts/itestPinnedRoads.cjs` 555 to 561 (565), `scripts/itestIdentities.cjs` 167 to 171 (178). *One step beside the spec:* before its leaving checks a leg closes any bridge that a defect left open (`bridges.dispose()`, which the scope runs anyway). The CI proving run is the lead's.

**Review of E3.S6 (2026-11-24, PASS WITH FIXES).** *What changed.* (1) M3 no longer depends on a correct `hostKeyWords` for its own sake: `withFile` replaces each known-hosts option that the recorded words hold and throws only when none is there; the check that the witness's or the control's own argv holds the six options of E1 and E2, each once, is a check of its own (M3.7a, M3.s7a), no longer part of M3.7 and M3.s7; a witness or control that could not be made (or whose login failed) is a named FAIL and the leg goes on to M3.8 or M3.s8, the leaving checks and the evidence line. OT3 holds the new `withFile` (a missing option is no exception; none at all is). (2) The A2 check, on the real run, in every leg: each `<pid>.stderr` file in the records belongs to a recorded start of the bridge (`-N`); a leg with no bridge has none. (3) The evidence takes `cold` from the counts (exactly one `-V` start) and the master's `-O check` codes are the real ones (`masterOf` and `masterCheck` return them). (4) M2.s6 also requires that the install showed at least one thing. (5) M3.s2 and M3.s4 also compare the pid that `-O check` names with the master's. (6) `itestRowOtherKey.cjs` no longer imports `itestRowM1.cjs`: it needs the rig and the two libs only. *Hand checks again* (each alone, in a scratch worktree of the commit with this change applied, on the real `sshd`; R1 FAILs there; hand checks, never results): HC1 (no `UserKnownHostsFile` pair): `m2-mismatch-user` red in M2.1 to M2.5 and `m2-switch-user` in M2.s3 to M2.s5, as before, and in both M3 legs the leg now reaches its end with ONE red check, M3.7a and M3.s7a (`UserKnownHostsFile` is there 0 times); the other legs green. HC2 (no `GlobalKnownHostsFile` pair): the same for the `-global` legs, and M3.7a and M3.s7a red in the M3 legs (`GlobalKnownHostsFile` 0 times). HC3 (no `KnownHostsCommand=none`): `m8` red in M8.1 to M8.5, M3.7a and M3.s7a red, every other check of the seven legs green. In HC1 and HC2 the closed-list check of the keys folder is red beside the predicted ones in the one mismatch leg of the changed option, as in the first hand checks. *MO6 in a proving run* (the shim keeps EVERY stderr in its file; scratch worktree; 661 ok, 244 FAIL, 1244 s): the question `ssh -V` prints its version on stderr, so the product reads no version and starts no connection: the roads of the whole script go red. In the seven legs: A2 red in every leg (the files belong to no bridge), M2.1, M2.3 and M2.4 in the M2 mismatch legs, M2.s1, M2.s2, M2.s3 and M2.s6 in the M2 switch legs, M3.1, M3.3, M3.4, M3.5, M3.7, M3.7a and M3.8 in `m3-mismatch`, M3.s1, M3.s3, M3.s7, M3.s7a and M3.s8 in `m3-switch`, M8.1, M8.2 and M8.4 in `m8`. After each run nothing was left: no `/tmp/creds-*` name, no process, and the scratch worktree was removed.

**M7, M12 and M14 on the rig (E3.S7).** *Files.* `scripts/lib/pathRecord.cjs` holds the pure rules (the tables of the legs of M7 and M12; the coverage judge; the word that a pin file's path needs; how `ssh` prints a path in its `Offending` line; the bare word's twin; E1's words with a path as it is; the `/dev/null` scan; the no-path judge; the listing of a folder); `scripts/itestRowPaths.cjs` what the three rows share; `scripts/itestRowM7.cjs` (17 legs), `scripts/itestRowM12.cjs` (13 legs and `devNullScan`) and `scripts/itestRowM14.cjs` (4 legs). The rig gains the option `onPin` of `openRoads` and `openExec`; `itestRowM2.cjs` exports `mismatch` and `switchLeg` with an optional third parameter `more` (called after M2.5 or M2.s6; the fields it answers join the evidence), `itestRowM8.cjs` exports `writeCommand`, `productOf` has `knownHostsPath`, and the world has `execWorld`. No product file changed. *The case is made by the storage folder alone.* A leg makes `<leg>/st/<name>`, a folder whose name holds the characters that the row names, and sets it as the leg's storage BEFORE its world opens, so the product makes the pin file's complete pathname itself (`knownHostsPath`: the folder, `keys/<pid>/`, `known_hosts-<id>-<uuid>`). No product function is patched and no path is handed to a builder. The leg's own folder must hold none of a space, `'`, `"`, `\`, `%`, `$`, `~` or a control character, or the leg throws, so the folder's name is the only cause of a kind or a quote. The person's key is a key named by its path, in the leg's folder and outside the storage folder, so the pin file is the only file that a call writes there. *The 34 legs.* M7: for each of (a) a space, (b) one apostrophe, (c) a pair of apostrophes, (d) a space and an apostrophe, (e) a backslash as the folder's last character, three legs (`-m1`: M1's runs and every M1 check on that folder; `-m2-mismatch` and `-m2-switch`: M2 (b)'s two legs with every M2 check), and for (c) the two legs of the collision. M12: `m12-rule` and, for the six kinds that a folder can give ((a) a double quote, (b) two backslashes, (c) `%h`, (d) `${HOME}`, (e) a backslash and an apostrophe, (f) a tab), one leg per road (install, bridge): a defect that asks `ssh -V` on one road would leave the asker warm and hide itself on the next road of the same world. M14: `m14-a` to `m14-d`. Every leg is cold, in its own process, world, key copy and counterpart(s). *The word.* M7.w reads both known-hosts options of each connection start with `valuesOf` (never through `pinOf`, which strips quotes) and holds each to the table's word: the path in double quotes for (a)-(d), bare for (e), `<storage>/keys/<pid>/known_hosts-e1-<uuid>` with the uuid read from the recorded name; for (e) also one backslash inside and none at its end. The table, not `checkedPin`, is the expectation (PT2 holds the product to the table). Sentence C on both roads is read by M7.c1 to M7.c3, and the `Offending` line with the harness's own printing of a backslash as two (M7.c3 does not read the product). *The collision and the twin writer.* The path that the bare word of (c) resolves to holds the call's uuid, which exists only when the call has written its pin file, so no file can be put there before the call. The rig's gate and its recording spawner already see each call's pin file before its client starts; with `onPin` they call the leg once per distinct pin file, at that moment, and the leg writes the twin (the apostrophes taken out, K2's line, folders `0700`, file `0600`) and records the time. The product's spawn, argv, environment and outcome are unchanged (the lead's answer A3). M7.x0 holds that each twin was written before its call's first run started or its bridge was spawned, so a twin that is late is red. The control (M7.y3, in the K2 leg, after the counts) is the real client by its path with E1's words with the path BARE and as it is, on a file of K1's line and its twin of K2's line: it must be accepted by K2, which shows that the twin is what a bare word reads. The witness (M7.x2, in the K1 leg) is the same bare words against K1: refused, so the leg could have failed. *The exec world.* `execWorld` loads the real `sshUseActions` under the stubbed `vscode`, assembles its deps with the product's own `sshDepsFor` (the real `windowPinClient` and `resolveExecAuth`; no stand-in of either), over the same host as the roads' world, and answers `run(command)`: the action's own `run`, off a bridge (no `bridge` in the context), as the broker would call it; `openExec` of the rig gives the world the question's observer over the recording runner and answers a `roads`-shaped object, so `mark`, `countsOf` and `evidence` take it as they are. The broker's HTTP layer and its consent stand above `run` and are not proven here (the lead's answer A1). *M12.* `m12-rule` reads the kinds from the product (`PIN_PATH_KINDS`) and fails when one of them is neither a leg (a)-(f) nor (g) or (h); (g) (a backslash at the END of a pathname) and (h) (a tilde at its START) cannot come from a folder, so the rule is asked with such a pathname and the real client alone is the control (`ssh -G -F /dev/null`: (g) in double quotes says `invalid quotes`, (h) is expanded in the person's list and not in the system-wide one), and no road is driven for them. Each road leg asks the rule with the complete pathname of its folder before a road is driven, and holds 0 starts of the client of any kind (the question included), 0 connections in K1's log, no file written into the storage folder (the sampler's samples and the listings before and after; folders are recorded as info), no bridged row, and sentence E: the road's prefix (a literal) and the product's sentence with the kind's words pinned from the plan and no path in it; then the liveness control. `devNullScan` reads every `<pid>.argv` of every `records` folder at depth 1 and 2 of the section's folder and fails when one names `/dev/null` as a known-hosts file, or when it read none. *What none of it proves.* No row passes (these are proving runs; §10 of the plan is E3.S10's). The equals sign has no leg (the lead's answer Q1 of the stories document). A folder that is a real network path (M12 (b) stands for it with a folder of that name on Linux). The warm state of the asker (every leg is cold; the refusal of a path stands before the question). *Run with Secrets* over SSH, the agent's linked run and a bridged exec. The broker's HTTP layer and consent. What the install shows when the client ends before it connects (HC1, not predicted). A root runner, another operating system, a client older than 8.5.

**M7, M12 and M14, seen when they were built (2026-11-24, commit `27d83a3f`).** NOT §10 of the plan; the runs pass no row. OpenSSH 10.2p1 for the client and for the unpacked `sshd` (`CREDS_ITEST_SSHD` and `CREDS_ITEST_SSHD_CONFIG`), loopback, Linux (WSL2), a clean tree (`git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no` empty), `npm run itest:ssh-agent`, three runs in a row: exit 0, 1553 ok lines and 0 FAIL in each (M7 349, M12 164, M14 40 of them, the ok lines headed by the row's label, the legs' end checks included), 252 s, 253 s and 253 s; 82 leg processes (the 48 of before and 34 new), each ended with 0; R1 to R9 ok; M12.z read 290 argvs in 83 records folders and found no `/dev/null`; after each run no `/tmp/creds-*` name, no process, no scratch worktree. The three runs agree in every count of every leg. *Per leg* (evidence lines). Every leg that starts a client is cold with ONE question `ssh -V` (12.3 ms to 16.6 ms over the 60 questions of the 34 legs). M7, `-m1` (a)-(e): 6 starts (the question and five connection starts: the probe, the install run, the bridge, the sweep, the mode check), K1 logged 5 accepted logins, one per run (4 with a command session, 1 with none), the four runner runs ended with 0. M7, `-m2-mismatch` (a)-(e): 5 starts (the question and four connection starts), no install run, the three runner runs ended with 255 and the whole verification line, K2 logged 4 connections, 0 authentication attempts and 0 accepted logins; sentence C was the one error message of the install and `The bridge to "box" is not open: ` plus sentence C the one error message of the check, no error held sentence D; the `Offending` line of the probe and of the mode check named the pin file of its own call as ssh prints it (in (e) the storage folder `end\` was printed `end\\`; the folder and so the pathname `<leg>/st/end\/keys/<pid>/known_hosts-e1-<uuid>` hold ONE backslash). M7, `-m2-switch` (a)-(e): 3 starts (the question, the probe, the install run), the probe accepted by K1 (1 login), the install run ended with 255 and the line, K2 logged 1 connection, 0 authentication attempts and 0 accepted logins. M7.w: the word was the path in double quotes in (a)-(d) and the bare path in (e), in every connection start of all 15 legs and of the two collision legs. M7.e0: the rule's answer for the pathname of (e) was no refusal in the three legs of (e) (and no refusal for the folders of (a)-(d)). The collision: `m7-c-collision-k1`: 6 starts, K1 logged 5 accepted logins (it logs 6 connections after the witness) and the runs ended with 0; the three twins were written 0.10 ms, 0.05 ms and 0.00 ms (to the clock's resolution) before their calls' first runs (0.002 ms to 0.096 ms over the eighteen twins of the three runs), the leg process wrote 3 twins for 3 pin files; the witness (E1's words with the path bare, against K1) ended with exit code 255 and the verification line and K1 logged no new accepted login. `m7-c-collision-k2`: 5 starts, K2 logged 4 connections, 0 authentication attempts, 0 accepted logins, the runs ended with 255 and the line; 3 twins in time; the control (E1's words bare on `<leg>/st/x'y'z/control/known_hosts-control` with K1's line, its twin with K2's) was accepted by K2: exit 0, one more accepted login with a command session. M12: `m12-rule`: 8 kinds, coverage ok; (g) and (h) refused with `lastBackslash` and `tilde`; the client alone: (g) in double quotes ended with 255 and `command-line line 0: invalid quotes`, (h) resolved `userknownhostsfile` to the person's home followed by `/known_hosts-e1-<uuid>` (the home written `~` here) and `globalknownhostsfile` to `~/known_hosts-e1-<uuid>`. The twelve road legs: the rule gave the kind of the leg for the complete pathname (`quote`, `doubleBackslash`, `percent`, `variable`, `backslashApostrophe`, `control`) and none for the leg's folder alone; 0 starts of any kind, 0 questions timed, K1 logged 0 connections and 0 authentication attempts, no sample of `keys/<pid>/` held a file and the listings before and after were empty (the product made no folder either); no bridged row and the spawner was called 0 times; ONE error message, `Cannot reach "box": ` or `Cannot bridge to "box": ` and sentence E with the kind's words and no path; the liveness control signed in to K1 (exit 0) and K1 logged ONE more accepted login. M14: `m14-a`: status 200, exit code 0 and the nonce on stdout, 2 starts (the question and one connection start, the question ended first), the argv held the six pairs of M1 each once with the entry's own file `<storage>/keys/<pid>/known_hosts-e1`, K1 logged one accepted login with a command session; `m14-b` and `m14-c`: status 200 with exit code 255 and the verification line in the answer's body and in the run's outcome, one connection start, K2 logged one connection, 0 authentication attempts and 0 accepted logins; the witness of (b) (the two pairs of before E1) was accepted by K2 (exit 0, one more login), the witness of (c) (E1's words of a file of K1's line, before the mark) was accepted by K2 and the command's record held 2 starts then and 2 after the call (0 for the call); `m14-d`: the rule gave `quote` for the exec's own file, 0 starts of any kind and 0 questions, the answer was status 409 with the code `no_credential` and sentence E of the quote kind (the place `The folder where this editor keeps the files of CredsForDevs` and `a double quote`), it held no path, nothing was written into the storage folder, K1 logged 0 connections, and the liveness control logged one login. *Mutations of the lib* (MP1 to MP7, in the commit's body; each restored, `cmp` equal): each turned red the test the spec names (MP1 PT1, MP2 PT2, MP3 PT3, MP4 PT3 and PT9, MP5 PT4, MP6 PT4, MP7 PT6). *Hand checks and mutations that need an `sshd`* (HAND CHECKS and mutations, never results: each in a scratch worktree of the commit, one proving run with R1 red there as it must be, each file restored and checked with `cmp` and `git diff --quiet`, the worktree removed): MP8 (the gate calls `onPin` after the runner returned) and MP9 (`devNullScan` reads no folder), in one run: M7.x0 red in both collision legs (the install's and the check's twins were late; the bridge's was not) and M12.z red with 0 argvs; every other check green. HC1 (every pin path written bare) and HC3 (the agent's exec runs with no pin), in one run: HC1 turned red `m7-{a,b,c,d}-m1` (M1.3, M1.4, M1.5, M1.6, M1.8, M1.10, M1.11, M1.12, M1.14 to M1.17 and M7.w; in (a) and (c) also the no-sentence check), `m7-{a,c}-m2-mismatch` (M7.w, M7.c1 to M7.c3), `m7-{b,d}-m2-mismatch` (the same and M2.1, M2.3), `m7-{a,b,c,d}-m2-switch` (M7.w, M2.s1, M2.s2, M2.s3, M2.s6), `m7-c-collision-k1` (M7.w, M7.x1) and `m7-c-collision-k2` (M7.w, the two refused checks, M7.y2, M7.y4); the legs of (e), M12 and M14 stayed green, and so did M7.x0, M7.x2, M7.y3. HC3 turned red `m14-a` (M14.a2, M14.a3), `m14-b` (M14.b1, M14.b2), `m14-c` (M14.c1, M14.c2, M14.c3) and `m14-d` (M14.d1, M14.d2 and the K1 half of d4, see below); M14.0, a1, a4, b0, bw, c0, cw, d0, d3 and every check of M7 and M12 stayed green. HC2 (the rule refuses no path) and HC4 (`unprinted` answers the name as printed), in one run: HC2 turned red every `m12-<k>-<road>` (M12.k, M12.1, M12.3, M12.5; M12.4 in the six bridge legs; M12.2 in (b) to (f) and not in (a), where `invalid quotes` ends the client before it connects), `m12-rule` (M12.g, M12.h) and `m14-d` (M14.d0, M14.d1, M14.d2, M14.d4); M12.cov, gc, hc, M12.0, M12.6, M12.7, M14.d3, every check of M7 and of `m14-a` to `m14-c` stayed green (M14.d3 was named "not predicted"). HC4 turned red M7.c1 and M7.c2 of `m7-e-m2-mismatch` and nothing else (M7.c3 of that leg stayed green: the harness's own reading). *Where a prediction was wrong, plainly.* (1) HC1, `m7-c-collision-k2`: the spec named M7.y1, M7.y2 and M7.y4; the leg also turned red the check `M7.y1.2` (named `M7.y.2` when it was seen; the review renamed it, see below), "the record of the client's starts holds no install run" (it is part of `refusedChecks`, which the leg calls for M7.y1): K2 was accepted through the twin, so the install ran. The row's words are not touched. (2) HC3, `m14-d`: the spec named M14.d4 as one check; the leg splits it into d4 (no file written) and d5 (K1 logged 0 connections), and under HC3 only d5 turned red (the exec ran with no pin, so it wrote no file and connected); the file half is red under HC2 (the pin file of the entry is written and never deleted, J11). (3) The M7.x0 margins are tiny (0.00 ms to 0.1 ms) because the twin is written synchronously just before the run's own clock starts; the check holds the order, not a margin. Outside the legs of E3.S7 no red was seen in any of the three runs. *CI* (the lead's `ci-extension.yml` on the head sha, the runner's own `sshd`): not run by this build.

**M7, M12 and M14: deviations from the spec, and the review of E3.S7 (2026-11-24, PASS WITH FIXES).** *Deviations of the build, recorded.* (1) `ssh -G` of M12.gc and M12.hc runs through the rig's `viaReal`, which puts the leg's handed config first: the real argv is `-F <handed config> -G -F /dev/null -p 1 …`, not `-F /dev/null` alone. The last `-F` wins (`ssh.c` sets `config = optarg` for each `-F`; measured on OpenSSH 10.2p1: `ssh -G -F /nonexistent-file -F /dev/null` reads no config and does not fail), so no config is read. (2) `m7-c-collision-k2` also runs the A2 check (`stderrFilesCheck`), and both collision legs also run `nothingLeft` (the closed list and the files of the calls); the spec lists neither. (3) M14 uses no stand-in install: the exec runs `printf %s <nonce>`, so no installer can run. (4) The check of the install's absence in the K2 collision leg is part of `refusedChecks`, and the liveness control is `liveness` of `itestRowRefused.cjs`: its check line ends with `(R.9)`. *Mapping of ids:* that line is M12.6 in the `m12-<k>-<road>` legs and the liveness control after M14.d4 and M14.d5 in `m14-d`; M14.d4 (no file written) and M14.d5 (K1 logged 0 connections) are the two halves of the spec's M14.d4. *What the review changed.* (1) `devNullIn` reads every form that ssh accepts (measured on OpenSSH 10.2p1): the name, then `=` or white space (also around the `=`), then a list of files split at white space with a double-quoted word kept together; any word that is `/dev/null` is a hit, for `-o Name=v`, `-oName=v` and a word that holds `Name v`. PT4 got the new cases and was watched RED on the old matcher ("a space in place of =") before it passed; the old matcher turned the new PT4 red again as a mutation (restored, `cmp` equal). (2) The ids of the refused checks of the K2 collision leg are `M7.y1.1` and `M7.y1.2`, apart from `M7.y2` (the HC1 note above). (3) M12.hc also requires `userknownhostsfile` to be the home folder that the password database names, followed by the file's name (ssh expands `~` from that database; the run only reads the home path as a string, it reads and writes nothing in `~/.ssh`). (4) M7.w counts the backslashes of the word that ssh got (the recorded value), not of the path that the harness made. (5) The two collision legs have a cold check, M7.c0, as M12.0 and M14.0 have. (6) The evidence of `m12-rule` carries the four counts from `recordedStarts` (0 starts, 0 questions, 0 connection starts, no counterpart log).

**M5 on the rig (E3.S8).** *Files.* `scripts/lib/toldRecord.cjs` (153 lines) holds the pure rules: the table of the eleven legs; `C_WORDS` and `D_WORDS`, the words that only one of the two sentences holds, and the two openings of the bridge's message, as literals of the plan (never read from the product); `toldOf`, `shownOf`, `logsOf`, `sameShown`; the two forced scripts; the jump host's config; `pinLines`; `controlOfH`. `scripts/itestRowTold.cjs` (294 lines) holds what the legs share (the forced script writer, the silent listener, the jump config writer, the drive, the reading of the screen, the told checks); `scripts/itestRowM5.cjs` (342 lines) the eleven legs and the comparisons across legs. The rig gains one name in `ROW_FILES` and section 10 one call. No product file, no workflow and no `package.json` changed. *The eleven legs.* (a) K2 answers at the entry's address; (e) as (a) under a handed config with `LogLevel QUIET`; (f) the entry's address is K1 (D) through a jump host J, a second `sshd` with a host key KJ of its own, whose key the jump file refuses; (g) K1 accepts the logins and the remote side (the `sshd`'s forced command) writes `Host key verification failed.` and ends with 255; (h) the same and then runs the asked command, to 0; (b) the counterpart is started and ended before the world opens, its port stays; (c) K1's `authorized_keys` holds the stored key's `.pub` alone, so the call's key is refused; (d) a listener of the leg process on 127.0.0.1 that accepts and never sends a byte. (b), (c) and (d) each have a twin leg on an UNPINNED entry (`replaceHostKey(undefined)` after the world opens). Every leg is cold, in its own process, world, key copy and counterpart(s), and every question of the install is dismissed, so no install run starts in any leg. *The screen is read twice.* What the person was shown is read from the world's `said` (messages and log lines, in order) and compared with the product's own sentences AND with the pinned words: a check that asks for sentence C once asks that one message equals the product's sentence, that this sentence holds `C_WORDS`, and that nothing after the mark holds `D_WORDS`; a check that asks for 0 times asks that no message and no log line equals or holds the sentence, its pinned words or its opening. A log line of the right text never passes for the bridge's message (`toldOf` counts the `error` messages and the log lines apart). *The silent listener.* (d) is the lead's answer A1 to Q1 of the spec: an address on the loopback whose connection is made and whose SSH answer never comes; the leg records which limit ended each run (`ConnectTimeout` or the runner's) and the client's own stderr, and judges neither. The listener's `bytesWritten` counters are what the check of its 0 bytes reads. *The jump config.* S3.P4's text: `Host jump` with its nine lines, `Host 127.0.0.1` with `ProxyJump jump`, then the rows' own `Host *` block, written by the leg to its handed config before its world opens; the jump file holds K2's line for J's port. The route witness (M5.fw) rewrites the jump file with J's own line and shows the route works with E1's literal words, so that D's 0 connections come from the refusal of J's key. *The forced scripts.* Four lines each (the shebang, a comment, the `printf` of the verification line to stderr, and the last line): `zero` ends with `exec "$SHELL" -c "$SSH_ORIGINAL_COMMAND"`, `fail` with `exit 255`; the two counterparts' `extra` differ in the script's name only, so (g) is the control of (h) with the exit code as the one thing changed. *The comparisons across legs* run in section 10's process from the legs' `evidence.json`: M5.b9, M5.c9 and M5.d9 (`sameShown` of a pinned leg's messages and its twin's: kind, text and order, both lists not empty) and M5.hc (`controlOfH` of (g) and (h)). A file that is not there is `null` and fails the check by name. *What it does not prove.* The row's pass (these are proving runs; §10 of the plan is E3.S10's); that the socket check READ the mode in (h) beyond "no error message and an owner-only mode in the run's stdout" (Q3: `StreamLocalBindMask` is never widened); a hop under a quiet config (no row has both); a client that does not keep `LogLevel=ERROR`; the install run in any leg (every question is dismissed); Windows.

**M5, seen when it was built (2026-11-24 for the runs, 2026-11-25 for the hand checks; commit `ab375f29`).** NOT §10 of the plan; the runs pass no row. OpenSSH 10.2p1 for the client and for the unpacked `sshd` (`CREDS_ITEST_SSHD` and `CREDS_ITEST_SSHD_CONFIG`), loopback, Linux (WSL2), a clean tree (`git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no` empty; the record of identities says `trackedChanges` empty), `npm run itest:ssh-agent`, three runs in a row: exit 0, 1733 ok lines and 0 FAIL in each (M5 178 of them, the ok lines headed by `M5 <leg>`, the legs' end checks and the four checks of section 10's process included), 362 s, 362 s and 363 s; 93 leg processes (82 of before and 11 new), each ended with 0; M12.z read 342 argvs in 94 records folders and found no `/dev/null`; after each run no `/tmp/creds-*` name, no process, no listener on a bridge's socket. The three runs agree in every count of every leg (the order of the session kinds in a log differs, never their count). *Per leg* (evidence lines; the four counts: starts of the client, connection starts, the counterpart's log, the runs). Every leg that starts a client has 5 starts (the question `ssh -V`, 12.2 ms to 18.8 ms over the 24 questions of the three runs, and four connection starts: the probe, the bridge, the sweep, the mode check), the unpinned twins 4 (no question). **(a)** K2 logged 4 connections, 0 authentication attempts and 0 accepted logins; the probe, the sweep and the mode check ended with 255 and the bridge with 255 by itself (28 ms), no install run; told: sentence C once on the install, once in the bridge's form and once as a log line, D 0, `C_WORDS` held 3 times and the opening `is not open: ` twice. The messages in order (at 218 ms, 222 ms and 1778 ms after the mark): the install's error, word for word: `"<name>" did not present its saved host key, so CredsForDevs did not connect. Nothing was sent to that host. Do not replace the saved key because of this message. If this server was rebuilt, get its key fingerprint from the server's owner. If it was not, do not connect.`; the info `Bridge open to "<name>". Open an SSH session there, paste the setup block, and `creds` works there.`; the bridge's error, the opening `The bridge to "<name>" is not open: ` and the same sentence. The log line is the bridge's text again. The `Offending` line of the probe and of the mode check named the pin file of its own call. **(e)** the same counts and messages; `ssh -G` under the quiet config printed `loglevel SILENT` (see the deviations); the control (E1's literal words of a file of K1's line for K2's port, under the same config) ended with 255 and an empty stderr (K2's log then holds 5 connections: the control's one comes after the counts). **(f)** before the witness J logged 4 connections, 0 authentication attempts and 0 accepted logins, D logged 0 connections; the four runs ended with 255, no install run; told: D once on the install, once in the bridge's form and once as a log line, C 0; the messages: the install's error, `The connection to "<name>" ended with "Host key verification failed", and ssh did not name the saved host key of this entry as the reason. It can be the key of a jump host in your own ssh config, which your own known_hosts file checks, or your ssh config gives this host another name or port than this entry does. Do not replace a saved key because of this message. Check this host's key fingerprint with the server's owner, and check your own ssh config for a HostName or Port that renames this host and for a jump host.`; the same info; the bridge's error, the opening `The bridge to "<name>" could not be checked: ` and the same sentence. No stderr held a line with the pin file of its call, and the probe's held the `Offending` line of the jump file. The route witness ended with 0 and D and J each logged one more accepted login (D with a command session, J with none). **(g)** K1 logged 4 connections, 4 authentication attempts and 4 accepted logins (3 with a `forced-command` session, 1 with none); the probe, the sweep and the mode check ended with 255 and the whole verification line and no `Offending` line; told as (f); the same three messages as (f); the bridge stayed (open in the manager, its `ssh` alive by pid and start time, its socket there and listened on) until `Close Remote Bridge` ended it within 5 s; the script's SHA-256 was that of the lib's text (`baafb4f6…`), its line `Host key verification failed.`. **(h)** K1 logged 4 connections, 4 authentication attempts and 4 accepted logins (3 with a session, 1 with none); the probe, the sweep and the mode check ended with 0, the probe's and the mode check's stderr held the verification line; no message and no log line held C or D (no log line at all); the messages were the question of the install, `Replace `creds` on "<name>"?` (this machine already has `creds`, so `confirmationFor` builds the replace form), and the info `Bridge open to ...`; one question before the bridge's click, the one `confirmationFor` builds; the mode check's stdout was an owner-only mode; the bridge stayed; the script's SHA-256 `c0a5cf65…`. M5.hc: (g) ended its probe and mode check with 255 and showed D on the install and in the bridge's form, (h) ended with 0 and showed neither, the forced line of both was the same text. **(b)** K1 stopped (a connection to its port was refused): K1 logged 0 connections; the probe, the sweep, the mode check and the bridge ended with 255 (12 ms to 13 ms); C and D 0; the messages: the warning `Cannot install on "<name>": the host did not answer the probe, so nothing is known about it yet.` and the info `Bridge open to ...`. **(c)** K1 logged 4 connections, 4 authentication attempts and 0 accepted logins; the same four 255 and the same two messages. **(d)** the listener accepted 4 connections, read 168 bytes and wrote 0; each run ended with 255 BY THE CLIENT ITSELF, after 10.03 s to 10.07 s (the bridge 10.06 s), none by the runner's limit; the client's stderr in the pinned leg was `Connection timed out during banner exchange`, in the unpinned leg that line and `Connection to 127.0.0.1 port <port> timed out` (the second line is at `INFO`, which the pinned words' `LogLevel=ERROR` keeps out), so `ConnectTimeout=10` does bound the wait for the server's first line on this client (T9 of the spec was read and is now seen); the messages as in (b). The C7 line, the socket check's log line, was the same in (b), (c) and (d) and in their twins: `<socket> is there, but its mode could not be read on that host`. The pinned legs of (b), (c) and (d) showed exactly what their unpinned twins showed (two messages each; M5.b9, M5.c9 and M5.d9 ok). *Mutation of the listener (MT8,* in a scratch worktree of the commit, the listener writing `SSH-2.0-x` to each connection*):* M5.d4 red in `m5-d` and `m5-d-unpinned` (`bytesOut` 44: eleven bytes for each of 4 connections), and no other check of M5 changed (the 178 checks of M5: 176 ok and those 2 FAIL, in a run of M5 alone); restored, `cmp` equal and `git diff` empty. *Hand checks* (the product made to do the wrong thing, in a scratch worktree of the commit, outside the story's tree; R1 FAILs in each, as it must; hand checks, never results; the worktree and its copy of the shared rules removed after). HC1 (`hostKeyTold` ignores the exit code), 1 run: `m5-h` red in M5.h3i, M5.h3b, M5.h4 and M5.h5, and M5.hc; nothing else, in M5 or outside it. HC2 (tell C for every verification line), 1 run: `m5-f` red in M5.f3, M5.f4 and M5.f5, `m5-g` in M5.g3, M5.g4 and M5.g5, and M5.hc; nothing else. HC3 (never tell C), 1 run: `m5-a` red in M5.a2, M5.a4 and M5.a6, `m5-e` in M5.e2, M5.e4 and M5.e6; outside M5 leg 2 of section 10 (the three checks that the spec named), the rig's proof `rig-warm` (RP1: "the second call, after the pin was replaced") and M7.c1 and M7.c2 in the five `m7-*-m2-mismatch` legs; nothing else. HC4 (the install's caller hands `-1`) and HC5 (the socket check's caller hands `null`), ONE run, their reds do not overlap: `m5-h` red in M5.h3i, M5.h3b, M5.h4, M5.h5 and M5.hc (HC4: h3i, h4, hc; HC5: h3b, h5, hc); nothing else; the run cannot say which half of the union is which (the spec allowed the shared run). HC6 (tell D for a pinned run with no verification line), 1 run: `m5-b`, `m5-c` and `m5-d` red in M5.b2, M5.c2, M5.d2 (D shown on the install and in the bridge's form) and M5.b9, M5.c9, M5.d9 (the pinned leg and its twin differ); outside M5 `m6-password-installs` red in M6.12 (the probe's refused password login is told D in place of the `Cannot install` warning), as the spec named. *Where a prediction was wrong, plainly.* (1) HC3 also turned M5.a6 and M5.e6 red: those checks hold that the check's error stands beside the `Bridge open to ...` info ("beside the check's error"), and with sentence C never told the bridge's error is D; the spec named a2 and a4 (e2 and e4) only. The row's words are not touched. (2) Three hand checks could not be made as the spec worded them: `hostKeyPin.ts` fails to compile with the one condition changed, because `exitCode` (HC1), `namesFile` and `file` (HC2, HC3) are then unused (`noUnusedParameters`, `noUnusedLocals`); each was made as `exitCode === 1000` in place of `exitCode === 0`, `true || lines.some(...)` and `false && lines.some(...)`, which keep the names in use and change nothing else. The first attempt of each is kept as a compile error and not counted. (3) The spec says HC2 and HC3 may share one run, but both change the same condition (`hostKeyPin.ts:409`), so they cannot; each ran alone. Outside M5 no red was seen in any hand check that the spec did not name. *Deviations of the build, recorded.* (1) `ssh -G` prints the level of `LogLevel QUIET` as `loglevel SILENT` (OpenSSH's first name of that level; measured with 10.2p1): the spec, TT9 and M5.e0 say QUIET, so both accept QUIET or SILENT and M5.e0 names what ssh printed. The meaning of the check, that the handed config resolves the quiet level, is the same. (2) TT4 says "four lines each, the first three equal" and the spec's section 3 names three lines: the forced scripts have a comment as their second line, so they have four lines (the first three equal, the last differs). The comment is inert in `sh`. (3) MT2 (`C_WORDS` is D's words) turned TT3 red as well as TT2 (TT3's pinned-words case reads `C_WORDS`). (4) The shared file's budget was 330 lines and the row file's 380: the row file first came to 402, so eight helpers moved to the shared file (294 and 342 lines; the lib 153, the test 268). (5) M5.x0c (cold) is also in the three unpinned twins (the spec names it for every pinned leg). (6) In (e) and (f) the evidence's counts of the counterparts include the control's and the witness's login (they run after the counts, as the spec says; the checks read the counts taken before them). *CI* (the lead's `ci-extension.yml` on the head sha, the runner's own `sshd`): not run by this build.

**Review of E3.S8 (2026-11-25, Opus: PASS WITH FIXES; the work judged `fits` in the epoch-4 re-review).** *What changed* (one commit; no pass criterion changed; no product file). (1) M5.hc no longer rests on the forced script on the disk alone: `controlOfH` also reads the stderr that the product really read, `runs[probe].stderr` and `runs[mode].stderr` of (g) and of (h): each must hold `Host key verification failed.` as a whole line and no `Offending` line, and (g)'s and (h)'s must be one text (CR LF read as LF); the `forced.line` comparison stays. TT8 got the cases and was watched RED first ("g's probe stderr not read", `true !== false`) before the lib was changed; as a mutation, dropping the equality turned TT8 red again (restored, `cmp` equal, `git diff` empty). (2) M5.h5 also holds the literal pin `/^0?600$/` on the trimmed stdout of the mode check, beside `isOwnerOnlyMode` of the product. (3) The evidence of every leg has `countsAtChecks` (the starts and the counterparts' connections, authentication attempts and accepted logins as the checks judged them, taken before the control's login in (e) and the witness's in (f)); the counts at the top level of the record (`starts`, `counterparts`, `bridges`) stay the leg's END counts and say so in `endCountsAre`. (4) "The install showed sentence C/D once" (M5.a2, e2, f4, g4) also requires the count on the install's road alone (`toldParts.install`), not only over the whole screen, and `controlOfH` requires the same of (g)'s D and of (h)'s C and D. (5) M5.a6 and M5.e6 no longer repeat M5.a4's count of the bridge's error; they hold that the info `Bridge open to "<name>".` stood once on the screen and once after the bridge's click (`at >= bridgeAt`). *The extra reds of HC3 in the build came from that repeat:* with sentence C never told, the bridge's error was D, so the repeated condition turned a6 and e6 red beside a4; with the repeat gone they are about the info only (the hand check was not run again). (6) M5.e0 also holds that the handed config holds the whole line `LogLevel QUIET`. (7) The silent listener of (d): after its close every accepted socket is destroyed (the leg's closing check says so). (8) (d) records the bridge's exit code and the text of its stderr file in the evidence and has a check M5.d5 that the bridge's `ssh` ended by itself with 255 and its stderr file was read, as (b) does. *Proof.* Unit: TT8 RED then GREEN, 9 of 9; tsc, lint, the guards green. One full proving run on the committed head is reported by the build to the lead; its numbers are not part of this paragraph (a commit cannot hold the numbers of its own run).

**The rows off the rig (E3.S9).** *Files.* `scripts/itestOffRig.cjs` (290 lines) is the ONE entry: `m10`, `m11`, `m13` and `unit-older`, with `--legs <a,b,...>` for hand checks only (a run that gives it says it is no proving run). `scripts/lib/offRigRecord.cjs` (177) holds the pure rules (the leg tables, the boundary 8.7 between the two parsers of the client, the expectations per parser, the two programs of (c) and (d), sentence F's pinned words, the times of (d), the reader of `ssh -G`); the rows are `itestRowM10.cjs` (111), `itestRowM11.cjs` (251), `itestRowM11Bare.cjs` (252), `itestRowM11Never.cjs` (138) and `itestRowM13.cjs` (169). None runs in section 10 and CI runs none. *Where each runs.* `m10` inside a `wslc` container (the host part starts it, `--inside` is the part in it: the resolver, the counterpart and the client are all that container's); `m11` here, with the unpacked OpenSSH 8.4p1 of P3 first on `PATH` and the rig's own client as `top.newer`; `m13` in the entry's own process, `ssh.exe` of the owner's Windows started from WSL; `unit-older` starts `run-tests.mjs out/test/pinWords.test.js` as ONE child with the older client first on `PATH`. *The image.* `creds-itest-e3-resolver:<b>`, `<b>` the first 12 hex digits of the blob of `e3-resolver.Containerfile` at HEAD (`399d9a71f272`); the container's init (`e3-resolver-init.sh`) runs the harness it is given. *Two clients, two parsers.* Each leg reads the `-V` of ITS client (`M11.p`, the product's `versionTokenOf`) and takes the constants of that version's parser (`BARE`, `EXPECT` of the lib; the boundary is 8.7, held equal to `TOKENISER_SINCE` of `pinnedArgv.ts` by TX11); no code path chooses an expectation from what the client did. A pair of apostrophes: before 8.7 it is read as itself (no collision), from 8.7 on the path without them (M7's collision). One equals sign: before 8.7 two names, the first the path up to the sign (a collision), from 8.7 on itself, whole. `DEBUG1`: a leg of M11 sets `leg.logLevel`, the launcher writes `LogLevel DEBUG1` IN PLACE of `VERBOSE`, and `logCounts` drops `debug1:` to `debug3:` lines, so the four counts read the same lines; `M11.v` reads `remote software version <the client's word>` in each counterpart's log. *The programs of (c) and (d)* are written by the parent before the leg process, handed to the rig as the leg's real client (`runLeg(…, { real })`), record their start as the shim does, answer `-V` (`-F <config> -V` after the shim) at once with `not a version: OpenSSH_10.2p1` and exit 0, or never, and pass any other argv to the older client. *M13* starts only `ssh.exe -V`, `ssh.exe -G` and `where.exe ssh`, with the product's own builders (`buildSshExecArgv`, `buildBridgeArgv`, each with `await checkedPinOf(out, <pin path>, SSH_EXE)`), a made-up Windows user folder (two words joined by a space, an apostrophe, a space and an apostrophe), K7's control and the UNC folder asked of `decidePinOfCall` with a counting asker. *Test 34*: `pinnedArgv.ts` `TOKENISER_SINCE` and `sshParserOf`; `pinWords.test.ts` P6 (pure), `parserOrFail()` in G1 to G6 (a version that cannot be read fails, it does not skip), G5 one branch by parser, G6 (the refused rows) asserted on a newer client and recorded on an older one. `itestRowsOff.test.ts` holds TX1 to TX13 (13 tests).

The lead built it again from the committed file with the owner's word of 2026-11-25 for E3.S9 (`wslc --session wslc-cli-strug build -f scripts/containers/e3-resolver.Containerfile -t creds-itest-e3-resolver:399d9a71f272 scripts/containers`): every step came from the cache, nothing was downloaded, and the image id is `sha256:2e760f27a6a0…`, the same image as the tag `20261124` (so not the id `40514e7c…` that the build of E3.S9 recorded; the run records the id it used). `m10` once on `d5abc739`, tree clean: 41 ok, 0 FAIL (9 on the host, 32 inside); `resolver.before` lists `AAAA NODATA`, `AAAA NXDOMAIN`, `A records`, `A records`, `AAAA NODATA`, `SSHFP records`. No container and no `/tmp/creds-*` left.

**What none of them proves.** A real `ssh` and a real host: the runner is a stand-in, so what is proven is WHEN the file is deleted relative to the runs,
not that an `ssh` could read it (the only real `ssh` of the set is the missing program of P6). Windows, by reading only: whether `ssh.exe` has released
the file when the delete runs was not measured. A delete that fails is silent, as everywhere, and the file stays until the purge (`purgeMaterializedKeys`,
on the next activate or deactivate). A window killed in the middle of a road runs no `finally`: the purge retires the file. A started `ssh` whose
`kill()` fails and that never exits keeps its key file until that purge; no test makes a kill fail for good.

## The extension's own ssh calls share no connection (G2)

The tunnel of a Remote Bridge and every run of `buildSshExecArgv` (the five bounded roads: the bridge's socket sweep and mode check, *Install creds on host*, an
agent's `ssh:exec` and run over SSH, *Run with Secrets* over SSH) say `-o ControlPath=none -o ControlMaster=no` (`NO_CONNECTION_SHARING`,
`sshExecCommand.ts`), whatever the person's `~/.ssh/config` says (`research/PLAN_bridge_no_connection_sharing.md`, S1 to S3; `module_extension.md`, *The bridge's `ssh` and
no connection sharing (G2)*). Three layers, each asking a different question: the unit tests ask what is SENT; one real `ssh -G` asks what a client RESOLVES; a real
`ssh` against real `sshd -d` runs asks what the options DO. Run the first two with
`node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs scripts/run-tests.mjs "out/test/noConnectionSharing.test.js" "out/test/sshBridge.test.js" "out/test/sshExecCommand.test.js" "out/test/bridgeLinks.test.js" "out/test/sshRunsLoginHelp.test.js"`
after `npm run compile`; the third with `npm run itest:ssh-agent` (section 9).

| File | Tests | What it holds |
|---|---|---|
| `noSharingArgv.ts` (not a suite) | n/a | What the three suites below ask of an argv, so they ask the same question: `TWO` (the two options, spelled once more on purpose, so the constant is pinned to it and not to itself), `pairAt`, `saysNoSharingBefore` (each pair exactly once, side by side, before every word of a list), `withoutTheTwo` (the argv of before G2, for a control) and `sharingWords` (the words before `--` that name sharing in any letter case or in the one-word spelling, the two pairs and the caller's own `extra` left out) |
| `noConnectionSharing.test.ts` | 8 | Part 1, the real client: under a config file with `ControlMaster auto`, a `ControlPath` and `ControlPersist yes`, `ssh -G -F <file>` resolves the tunnel, a non-bridged run and a bridged run, key and password, to `controlmaster false` and no `controlpath` line with a path (this client prints none for `none`; one that prints `controlpath none` passes too), and the tunnel keeps its `remoteforward` line; the CONTROL: the same argvs without the two options print `controlmaster auto` and the file's path, so the file can show the fault; a caller's own `ControlMaster` and `ControlPath` in `extra` change nothing (the first value wins); a canary that where `CI=true` an `ssh` is on the PATH (a skip on the runner proves nothing). Part 2, the list of places: every file and function that starts an `ssh` from an argv (`runSshExec(`, `bridges.start(`, the secrets launch) and every one that builds an argv (`buildSshExecArgv`, `buildBridgeArgv`) is named, so a new one fails the list until its argv has been looked at; and the two option words are spelled with an equals sign in `sshExecCommand.ts` only. **Seen when it was built (2026-11-17).** OpenSSH 10.2p1 for the client and for an `sshd` unpacked from the distribution's package and run by the user (this PC has none at
`/usr/sbin/sshd`; the first run on the runner's own `sshd` is the CI run of the head sha), loopback, one user, key authentication, Node 26.12.1, Linux (WSL2).
*Green:* section 9 alone, three runs in a row, 42 ok each, about 2 s; the whole `ssh-agent-itest.cjs` 119 ok lines. *RED on the product before S1* (the two product files as
they were on `main`): 26 ok and 16 FAIL, every control ok: P-B four checks (the forward is served while it runs, the control socket is made, `stop()` answers false, the forward
is served after `stop()`), P-D1 four, P-D2 two, P-D3 two, P-X one, P-I1 two and P-I2 one. *Mutations*, each a temporary edit restored and compared with `cmp`: the tunnel says
nothing, 12 FAIL (P-B, P-D1 to P-D3), P-X and P-I ok; a run off a bridge says nothing, 4 FAIL (P-X, P-I1, P-I2); the tunnel clears its own forward, 5 FAIL, with the controls red too;
only `ControlMaster=no`, 12 FAIL and P-B ok (alone it keeps an `ssh` from becoming a master and does not keep it from joining one); only `ControlPath=none`, 0 FAIL (on this client
that option carries the whole effect). The unit and `-G` tests were watched RED the same day on the unchanged product (nine failing, one skipped locally) and the fourteen mutations of the
S1 spec each turned red what the spec names.

**What none of them proves.** A host over a network, so the time of one login per call (a bridge makes three, an install two). Windows and macOS: the Windows job shows only what
`ssh.exe` resolves, and Win32-OpenSSH is believed, unverified, to have no `ControlMaster` at all. A password entry, a jump host (its nested `ssh` gets none of the outer's `-o`
options; follow-up H1), `git` over SSH (H2), an extension host that is killed without `dispose` (H3), `ControlMaster` values other than `auto`, an older OpenSSH. Each option alone was
measured on one client and one server. A security key that needs a touch (read from ssh(1), not run). That the row of a closed bridge goes back to *Open Remote Bridge…* before G2 was read from the code
and no window was driven.

## The three roads of the person and the saved host key (G1)

An SSH entry can hold a saved host key. G1 makes `ssh` read it as the ONLY known-hosts file on every call the extension starts with no terminal
(E1.S1), and makes the three roads of the person (the bridge socket check, *Install creds on host*, *Open Remote Bridge…*) decide the saved host key
of ONE call (E1.S2), and hand it to `ssh` (E1.S3). The tests prove, for the words: what is SENT by both argv builders
and what a real `ssh -G` RESOLVES from it; and, for the roads, the three states of a saved host key for one call (absent: nothing is written and the
call runs as before; usable: one file of this call alone, kept while the call needs it and deleted with it, whether or not a key file was written;
there and unusable, or not writable: no CONNECTION starts (a cold window runs the local `ssh -V` first) and the person reads one sentence), and that a road reads ONE value, the answer of its guard.
The road tests run the REAL `registerAgentCommands` over the world of the section above, with a real, owned storage folder and the real
`materializeKnownHosts` and `forgetMaterializedKey`. Run them with
`node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs scripts/run-tests.mjs "out/test/<file>.test.js"`, after `npm run compile`; the smoke with `npm run itest:ssh-agent` (section 10).
