`stopListener` ends the listener alone by its own pid; L0.3 and L0.4: the listener exited and what it forked for the session is alive; L0.5 to L0.7: the master still answers `ssh -O check`, a connection without the master ends with 255 and `Connection refused`, and the master carries one more command that prints the run's nonce and ends with 0; L0.8: K1 logged ONE accepted login; L0.9: the master, its session and K1 are gone by pid and start time and the control socket too. **The record** (`scripts/itestIdentities.cjs`, R1 to R6, after leg 0): see *The counterpart of the real runs (E3.S2)* below | **E3.S4:** after leg 0 section 10 runs the rig's proof, M1 and M6 (`itestRigProof.cjs`, `itestRowM1.cjs`, `itestRowM6.cjs`), each leg in a leg process of its own through `itestRowRig.cjs`; in the proving runs of 2026-11-24 (commit `0f8081b7`) section 10 was 338 checks, 334 ok and 4 FAIL (M6.11), and the whole script 411 ok and 4 FAIL in each of three runs. **E3.S5:** section 10 runs M4 and M9 after M6 (`itestRowM4.cjs`, `itestRowM9.cjs`; 25 more leg processes, 41 in all); in the proving runs of 2026-11-24 (commit `1a015583`) the whole script was 869 ok lines and 0 FAIL in each of three runs, M1 24, M4 265, M6 176, M9 177 of them (see *M4 and M9, seen when they were built*). **E3.S6:** section 10 runs M2, M3 and M8 after M9 (`itestRowM2.cjs`, `itestRowM3.cjs`, `itestRowM8.cjs`; 7 more leg processes, 48 in all); the shim also keeps a client's stderr in `<records>/<pid>.stderr` when the product gave it none (the bridge's), and `installShim` takes the real client as an optional parameter; in the proving runs of 2026-11-24 (commit `3c2ebef2`) the whole script was 991 ok lines and 0 FAIL in each of three runs, M2 64, M3 39 and M8 14 of them, and the record of identities has R9 (see *M2, M3 and M8, seen when they were built*). **E3.S7:** section 10 runs M7, M12 and M14 after M8 (`itestRowM7.cjs`, `itestRowM12.cjs`, `itestRowM14.cjs`; 34 more leg processes, 82 in all) and then the scan of every recorded argv for `/dev/null` as a known-hosts file (M12.z); in the proving runs of 2026-11-24 (commit `27d83a3f`) the whole script was 1553 ok lines and 0 FAIL in each of three runs, M7 349, M12 164 and M14 40 of them (the ok lines headed by the row's label, the legs' own end checks included; see *M7, M12 and M14, seen when they were built*). **E3.S8:** section 10 runs M5 after M14 and before the `/dev/null` scan (`itestRowM5.cjs`; 11 more leg processes, 93 in all), so the scan also reads M5's argvs; in the proving runs of 2026-11-24 (commit `ab375f29`) the whole script was 1733 ok lines and 0 FAIL in each of three runs, M5 178 of them (see *M5, seen when it was built*).

**The counterpart of the real runs (E3.S2).** *Where it comes from.* In CI, `/usr/sbin/sshd`, which the step *Install sshd for the live OpenSSH login* installs, with no parameter. Locally, any `sshd` named by `CREDS_ITEST_SSHD` (an absolute path to an executable file: an `sshd`, or a wrapper that sets its library path and `exec`s it), with its extra config lines in the file named by `CREDS_ITEST_SSHD_CONFIG` (an absolute path; each line trimmed, blank lines and `#` lines left out; an unpacked `sshd` 10.x needs `SshdSessionPath` and `SshdAuthPath`). No tracked file is edited to hand a parameter. A value that cannot be used (a relative path, a file that is not an executable file, a config file that cannot be read, a `Match` line) ends the harness at once with a `TypeError` that names the variable, and is never replaced by the default; an empty value counts as not set. With no parameter the executable is `/usr/sbin/sshd` and every config text is, byte for byte, what it was before E3.S2 (33d). *The command,* in `src_vs_code`, with `<SP>` the folder of the unpacked files (the packages `openssh-server` and `libwrap0` unpacked as data with `dpkg-deb -x` into a folder of their own, nothing installed into the system; a wrapper `<SP>/run/sshd-wrap.sh` with the two lines `#!/bin/sh` and `LD_LIBRARY_PATH=<SP>/root/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu exec <SP>/root/usr/sbin/sshd "$@"`; `<SP>/run/sshd-extra.conf` with the two lines `SshdSessionPath <SP>/root/usr/lib/openssh/sshd-session` and `SshdAuthPath <SP>/root/usr/lib/openssh/sshd-auth`): `CREDS_ITEST_SSHD=<SP>/run/sshd-wrap.sh CREDS_ITEST_SSHD_CONFIG=<SP>/run/sshd-extra.conf npm run itest:ssh-agent`, from a tree with no modified tracked file (R1 below). *Where the config is written.* `scripts/lib/sshdRun.cjs` writes both texts, and reads the two variables; `itestSshd.cjs` reads the run once (`RUN`), its `startSshd` (`sshd -d`, one connection) and the serving launcher of `itestServing.cjs` (`sshd -D -e`, every connection) call `oneConnectionConfig` and `servingConfig` with it. Order of the lines: the starter's own lines, then the run's lines, then the caller's (`sshd` keeps the first value of a keyword, so a line of the run cannot change a line of a starter, and a caller's `Match` block cannot take the run's lines in). *Leg 0 shows,* by group of checks: L0.3-L0.4, the listener alone was stopped by its own pid and what it forked for the master's session was listed before the stop and is alive after it; L0.5-L0.7, the master outlived its listener, a connection without the master is refused, and the master carried one more command; L0.8, K1 logged one accepted login, so that command did not log in again. *The record.* At the end of section 10 `identities.json` is written into the section's owned `pin` folder (it goes with that folder) and printed as one line, `identities <json>`; that line is what a commit body and this file quote. Fields: `commit` (`git rev-parse HEAD`), `trackedChanges` (`git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no`, whole: `""` for a clean tree, `null` when git failed), `conventions` (`git rev-parse HEAD:.agents/conventions`), `parameters` (the two variables as the process got them, or `null`), `executables` (`role`, `path`, `real`, `version`, `sha256`: the `sshd` as the run names it with its `-V` line, each helper that a line of the run names, with `version: null` because a helper is not started alone, the program each listener ran as `/proc/<pid>/exe` answers it, the programs the listener of leg 0 forked for the session, and the real client with its `-V` line) and `configs` (`file`, `sha256`: every `sshd_config` and `client_config` under the folder, the run's config file, the shim). A value that could not be read is `null`, never `0` or `''`. R1: the run is a run of its commit: the answer of `git status` is read and is empty; so a run of section 10 from a tree with an edit of a tracked file FAILS, on a developer's machine too, and `null` fails. R2 to R6: the commit and the pin are 40 hex digits; every executable has its path and SHA-256, the `sshd` and the client their version, at least one listener was recorded; every config text has its SHA-256; every listener ran one program; every listener ran an `sshd` itself (its `/proc/<pid>/exe` ends with `/sshd`: a wrapper must `exec`). *What it does not prove.* A counterpart of another operating system; an `sshd` run by root; that `sshd` keeps the first value of a keyword (read in its manual and relied on since E1.6, not measured here); a helper's version (the SHA-256 is its identity; the package it was unpacked from is for the person to note).

**The smoke, seen when it was built (2026-11-18).** OpenSSH 10.2p1 for the client and for an `sshd` unpacked from the distribution's package and run by the user (this PC has none at `/usr/sbin/sshd`), loopback, Node 26.12.1, Linux (WSL2). Section 10 alone, three runs in a row: 40 ok each, about 3.9 s; the whole script 159 ok lines (119 before section 10), about 24 s. *The control* (`hostKeyWords` answering `accept-new`, in a scratch copy): 16 FAIL, among them T38 (K2 logged `Accepted publickey` for 4 logins, with the `Postponed` and `Accepted key` lines before them) and T24, T25, T28, T29, T30 and the pin-file checks T15 to T17 and T32 to T34; T40 stays ok. On the runner's own `sshd` (9.6p1 on `ubuntu-latest`) the reviewer measured OpenSSH 9.6p1 client and `sshd` (2026-11-18): 40/40 in three runs, the fallback of the starter without `PerSourcePenalties` exercised, T37's lines present; the CI run of the head sha is the runner's own. Here the fallback was not exercised.

**The smoke on the changed words, seen when E2.S4 built it (2026-11-19).** OpenSSH 10.2p1 for the client and for an `sshd` unpacked from the distribution's package and run by the user (`Ubuntu-2ubuntu3.8`; `SshdSessionPath` and `SshdAuthPath` set in a scratch copy only), loopback, Node 26.12.1, Linux (WSL2). Section 10 alone, three runs in a row: 48 ok each, 3.9 s, 4.0 s and 3.9 s. The whole script when E2.S4 built it: 152 ok lines (104 before section 10) and 15 FAIL, all in the two sections that handed a plain string to the argv builders as `knownHostsFile` (`scripts/itestForwardAgent.cjs`, `scripts/itestNoSharing.cjs`): since E2.S2 c2 (581d96c0) that option is a checked pin, a string gave `UserKnownHostsFile=undefined`, and the control and production legs there ended with `No ED25519 host key is known`; no test caught it, because no CI job runs the script and nothing typed the two `.cjs` files. *The follow-up fix:* both scripts take their pin from `checkedPinOf` of `scripts/itestSshd.cjs` (the real `ssh -V` read by `pinClientOf`, then `checkedPin`, as the product makes one), and `itestCallers.test.ts` scans every `scripts/*.cjs` that calls a builder and goes red, naming file and line, on a `knownHostsFile` that is not `checkedPinOf(...)` (RED on the two scripts as they were: three lines named). The whole script after it, twice in a row on the same `sshd`: 167 ok, 0 FAIL each (section 9: 42, section 10: 48). *RED* on `c20be822` (E2.S1's `pinClient.ts`, no road asks the client, no three pairs): 6 FAIL and 42 ok, exit 1: the 5-starts check (`"questions":0`), the question-time check and the three-pairs check, in each leg. *Mutations*, each alone on the final product: MA (no `realClient`) 4 FAIL (the 5-starts and the question-time checks of each leg); MB (`hostKeyTold` never finds the evidence) 3 FAIL (the install's sentence C, the bridge's message, and the no-sentence-D check; the two `Offending` checks stay ok); MC (no `LogLevel=ERROR` pair) 2 FAIL (the three-pairs check of each leg); MD (a fourth name in `NEW_PAIRS`) 2 FAIL (the same check, `"namesAgree":false`). *The control MC1* (`hostKeyWords` answering `accept-new`): 20 FAIL, among them K2's accepted login, both `Offending` checks, the install's and the bridge's sentence C and every pin-file check; the precondition, the question checks and the no-sentence-D check stay ok. The CI run of the head sha is the runner's own.

**The instrument, seen when it was built (2026-11-23).** OpenSSH 10.2p1 for the client and for an `sshd` unpacked from the distribution's packages and run by the user (`openssh-server` `1:10.2p1-2ubuntu3.8` and `libwrap0` `7.6.q-36build2`, both amd64, unpacked as data, nothing installed; the helpers `sshd-session` and `sshd-auth` come from the first package; `sshd -V` through the wrapper answered `OpenSSH_10.2p1 Ubuntu-2ubuntu3.8, OpenSSL 3.5.5 27 Jan 2026`), loopback, Node 26.12.1, Linux (WSL2), from the tree of `4063c56b` (the code of E3.S2's two commits, a clean tree: `trackedChanges` `""`). The whole `scripts/ssh-agent-itest.cjs` with both variables, three runs in a row: exit 0, 182 ok lines and no FAIL each, 38.9 s, 38.2 s and 38.1 s; section 10 is 63 of those lines (47 up to the end of leg 2, L0.1 to L0.9, R1 to R6, and the closing `known_hosts` check; counted again by the Opus review of E3.S2). A local run prints the parameter paths as given, under the home folder: a line quoted into a tracked file writes that folder as `~` (the public-hygiene check refuses a real home path). The same script on the tree before E3.S2 (`55d6a828`, in a scratch copy of that commit with the executable and the two lines edited in by hand, for the comparison only, not a result of the story): 167 ok lines, the line of the end of leg 2 at the same line of the output, so the 15 new lines are leg 0 and the record and legs 1 and 2 are as before. Leg 0 on 10.2p1: SIGTERM to the listener of `sshd -D -e` left the processes of the session alive, the master answered `ssh -O check` and carried one more command that printed its nonce, a connection without the master ended with 255 and `Connection refused`, K1's log held one accepted login; `session-programs` named `sshd-session`. Listener programs: all three counterparts ran `/proc/<pid>/exe` = the unpacked `sshd` (R5, R6). The `identities` line of run 1, whole (the home folder written as `~`; the SHA-256 of a config text differs from run to run, because the owned folder's name is in it):


*Mutations on test 33* (each alone, restored byte for byte, none committed): MU1 33a and 33b red; MU2 and MU3 33e; MU4 33a and 33b; MU5 33c; MU6 33f; MU7 33f and 33g. *Mutations that need the `sshd`, on this run:* MU12 (`stopListener` calls `endServer` in place of `endChild`): L0.4, L0.5 and L0.7 FAIL (and R1, as a tracked file is then modified); MU10 (`executablesOf` without the listeners): R3, R5 and R6 FAIL (and R1); MU8 (R1 asks `trackedChanges !== null`): with the file edited R1 passes (exit 0). *The two hand controls:* one space added to a comment line of `itestSshd.cjs`: R1 FAILs with ` M src_vs_code/scripts/itestSshd.cjs` in its detail, and nothing else fails (so MU8 is found, as the same edit passes under it); MU9 (R1 asks `!trackedChanges`, run on a copy with no git repository) prints R1 FAIL for the real code and ok for the mutant. After each run no `creds-pin` folder, no `creds-<user>-*` socket and no `sshd` or `ssh -F` process of the run was left. The runner's `sshd` (9.6p1 on 2026-11-18): leg 0 and the record there, and R1 on the CI checkout, are the CI run of the head sha, which the lead reads after the push; not measured here. Not measured: that a new counterpart can take the port after the stop (M3 needs it), and the version of a helper (`null`).

**The instruments of E3's rows, probed (E3.S3, batch A seen when it was probed 2026-11-23, commit `1d67a080`; batch B 2026-11-24, commits `cd5088d9` for P3 and `e6cb6532` for P2).** A probe is NOT a row: it passes no row of §10 of the plan and measures no pass criterion; it asks whether an instrument can show what its rows need, through the committed instrument of E3.S2, from a tree with no modified tracked file (R1 empty in each run). The entry is `scripts/itestFeasibility.cjs`; it prints each probe's checks (`ok`, `FAIL`; `info` decides nothing), its record of identities, and one last line `probe <name>: worked` or `probe <name>: FAILED — <its first failed check>`. *The command,* in `src_vs_code`, after `npm run compile`: `CREDS_ITEST_SSHD="$HOME/.cache/creds-itest-sshd/sshd-wrap.sh" CREDS_ITEST_SSHD_CONFIG="$HOME/.cache/creds-itest-sshd/extra.conf" node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs scripts/itestFeasibility.cjs <probe>…`; the probes are `windows-client`, `never-answers`, `forced-zero`, `jump-config`, `install-stand-in` (batch A) and `older-client`, `resolver` (batch B, below, with their own commands). Common identities of every run: `sshd` through `~/.cache/creds-itest-sshd/sshd-wrap.sh` (sha256 `fa2cda0e…ab4f`; `OpenSSH_10.2p1 Ubuntu-2ubuntu3.8, OpenSSL 3.5.5 27 Jan 2026`), its program `…/root/usr/sbin/sshd` (sha256 `e029de38…09cb`), `sshd-session` (`9599ebe4…079d`), `sshd-auth` (`d61881f0…2a5f`), the config file `extra.conf` (`60e2ba89…cea`), the client `/usr/bin/ssh` (`af809334…c15f`, the same version line), Node 26.12.1, Linux (WSL2). Each probe of the five also ran in one run of all five (95 ok lines, exit 0, every last line `worked`). Section 10 after the exports of `itestPinnedRoads.cjs`: 63 ok lines, the whole script 182 ok, as before. Batch B (P3, P2) is below: each ran once from a clean tree; the first run of P2 failed one check of its own set-up (P2.5), and the repaired run is the one quoted.
- **P5 · the Windows client from this machine (M13); `scripts/itestProbeWindows.cjs`, `probeWindowsClient`.** `windows-client`: worked (14 ok, 0 FAIL). The harness starts `/mnt/c/Windows/System32/OpenSSH/ssh.exe` from WSL by interop with `execFile`, the argv as separate arguments. P5.1: `-V` printed `OpenSSH_for_Windows_9.5p2, LibreSSL 3.8.2` and the product's asker answered `knows`. P5.2: with the words of the product for the pin path `C:\Users\<user>\AppData\Roaming\Code\User\globalStorage\probe\keys\<pid>\known_hosts-e1-<uuid>` (a made-up user folder of two words; the script joins the name at run time), `ssh.exe -G -F none …` ended with 0 and printed `stricthostkeychecking true`, `userknownhostsfile <path>` and `globalknownhostsfile <path>` (the path whole), `verifyhostkeydns false`, `loglevel ERROR` and no `knownhostscommand` line. P5.3 (K7's control, a folder `x ~y` below the user): the quoted word ended with 0 and the path whole; the bare word ended with 255 and a `tilde_expand` line. P5.4: identities, R1 and R2 only: `ssh.exe` read through `/mnt/c`, sha256 `98d44773…9dc8`. No login on Windows; no counterpart.
- **P7 · a program that never answers `-V` (M11 d); `scripts/itestProbeNeverAnswers.cjs`, `probeNeverAnswers`.** `never-answers`: worked (8 ok, 0 FAIL). `<dir>/never/ssh` records its start and `exec`s `sleep infinity`; it is first on `process.env.PATH` before `rigOf` and `installShim`, so the shim resolves it as the real client (F15). A cold asker: the shim recorded ONE start with argv `-V`, the program one start with the same pid; the asker answered `{ kind: 'unreadable', exitCode: null }`; the program was gone by its recorded pid and start time. P7.4, recorded against the margin of M11 (d), 10 s to 15 s: `t1 - t0 = 10.01 s` (the asker answered) and `t2 - t0 = 10.04 s` (the program gone) in the single run; `10.00 s` and `10.03 s` in the run of all five. Both are inside the margin. The record: `never/ssh` sha256 differs per run (it holds the owned folder's name); the shape is in the `probe` field. The record's own `-V` of that program ends by the time limit of 10 s too: that start is not a start of the question.
- **P6 · a forced command that writes the line and then runs the command it was asked for, to 0 (M5 h); `scripts/itestProbeForced.cjs`, `probeForcedZero`.** `forced-zero`: worked (24 ok, 0 FAIL, 3 info). K1 with the `extra` line `ForceCommand <dir>/forced/zero.sh` (the line `Host key verification failed.` on stderr, then `exec "$SHELL" -c "$SSH_ORIGINAL_COMMAND"`); K1b (control of M5 g) with `fail.sh` (the same line, then `exit 255`); K1c with no line. P6.1: `probeCommand()` through the shim with the pin words of K1 ended with 0, its stderr held the line exactly, K1 logged one `Accepted publickey`. P6.2: the stdout, read by `interpretProbe`, and the raw stdout equal those against K1c, which wrote no such line. P6.3: K1b ended with 255, the same line, one `Accepted publickey`. P6.4 (recorded, C19's rule): `hostKeyTold(pin, stderr, "box", 0)` answered nothing; with 255, for K1 and for K1b, it answered sentence D (the line without an `Offending` line: the connection ended with "Host key verification failed" and ssh did not name the saved host key). Identities: `zero.sh` and `fail.sh` and the three `ForceCommand` texts by sha256 in the `probe-record` line (they hold the owned folder's name, so they differ per run).
- **P4 · a handed config that the nested client of a jump host reads too (M5 f); `scripts/itestProbeJump.cjs`, `probeJumpConfig`.** `jump-config`: worked (21 ok, 0 FAIL, 1 info). D (K1) and J (a third host key). The handed config (`Host jump` with its own `UserKnownHostsFile <dir>/jump_known_hosts`, `Host 127.0.0.1` with `ProxyJump jump`, then `Host *` with the two `/dev/null` lines) is the shim's `-F`. P4.1: with K2's line for J's address in `jump_known_hosts`, the outer ended with 255; its stderr held `Host key verification failed.` and the line `Offending <type> key in <dir>/jump_known_hosts:<n>`; J logged a connection and no authentication attempt; D logged no connection. P4.2 (control): with J's own line, the outer ended with 0 and J and D each logged one `Accepted publickey`. P4.3 (recorded): the shim recorded 2 starts of ssh over both legs, none with `-W`: the nested client of a `ProxyJump` is NOT started through the shim: OpenSSH starts it by its own absolute path (argv[0]), not by a lookup of `PATH` (`ssh -v -J …` prints `Executing proxy command: exec /usr/bin/ssh … -F … -W …`; read by the Opus review of E3.S3), and the config reached it through the `-F` that OpenSSH hands down; a `ProxyCommand` that names the shim would count it, and no row needs that count, so a row cannot count the nested start with this shim. Two counterparts, one program (R5 held).
- **P1 · the install's stand-in (M1, M2 the switch, M3 the switch, M6, M7, M9, M11 b); `scripts/itestProbeInstall.cjs`, `probeInstallStandIn` (`installStandIn(rig, nonce)`, `listingOf()`).** `install-stand-in`: worked with the FIRST candidate, the `extra` line `SetEnv PATH=<dir>/standin/bin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin` (28 ok, 0 FAIL, 2 info); the second candidate (`ForceCommand`) was not needed. The stand-ins: `bin/curl` (records, fetches nothing), `bin/sh` (records, `exec`s `/bin/sh` on `installer.sh` by its absolute path), `installer.sh` (writes the run's nonce and `env`, prints `creds-install: installed <dir>/standin/bin/creds`). Leg (a), the client alone: the install command through the shim with K1's pin words ended with 0 and printed the line. Leg (b), the road: `openLeg(rig, port, { answer })`, the button read from `confirmationFor` (this machine has a `creds` in `/usr/local/bin`, so the button is the replacing one), the click ended within 200 s. P1.1: K1 logged one `Accepted publickey` for (a) and three in all after (b). P1.2: `env` says `PATH=<dir>/standin/bin:…`, `curl=<dir>/standin/bin/curl`, `sh=<dir>/standin/bin/sh`. P1.3 and P1.4: the stand-in `curl` recorded exactly one start per install run (two) with argv `-fsSL`, `INSTALLER_URL`, `-o`, `/tmp/creds-install.<n>.sh`, the stand-in `sh` one start per run with that script; each run named its own script. P1.5: the marker held the nonce; `interpretInstall` answered `installed` with the stand-in's path; the world showed the product's message; the shim recorded one question `ssh -V` and two connection starts (the probe, the install run). P1.6: the listing of `/usr/local/bin/creds` (8 576 624 bytes, sha256 `15f1dfe7…ed0165`, an existing file of this machine) and of every `/tmp/creds-install.*` (none) was the same before (a) and after (b). P1.7: no new name in `/tmp` was `creds-*` or an owned name; none was new at all. Identities: the three stand-ins by sha256 and the `extra` text in the `probe-record` line. What the probe does NOT show: that `SetEnv` and `ForceCommand` behave the same on CI's `sshd` (10.2p1 only).
- **P3 · a client older than 8.5 that runs here (M11), with the lead's check Q1; `scripts/itestProbeOlderClient.cjs`, `probeOlderClient` (the run parameter `CREDS_ITEST_OLD_SSH_DEBS`).** `older-client`: worked (20 ok, 0 FAIL, 1 info; seen when it was probed, 2026-11-24, commit `cd5088d9`, tree clean). *The command,* in `src_vs_code`, after `npm run compile`: `CREDS_ITEST_OLD_SSH_DEBS="$HOME/.cache/creds-itest-oldssh/deb" CREDS_ITEST_SSHD="$HOME/.cache/creds-itest-sshd/sshd-wrap.sh" CREDS_ITEST_SSHD_CONFIG="$HOME/.cache/creds-itest-sshd/extra.conf" node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs scripts/itestFeasibility.cjs older-client`. *What it runs:* two packages of Debian 11 (bullseye), kept with their `SHA256SUMS` in `~/.cache/creds-itest-oldssh/deb/` (outside any owned folder, like the local `sshd`'s): `openssh-client_8.4p1-5+deb11u3_amd64.deb` (932 024 bytes, sha256 `489e329b…dac3`) and `libssl1.1_1.1.1w-0+deb11u1_amd64.deb` (1 565 664 bytes, sha256 `aadf8b4b…88f7`). Both were taken from `deb.debian.org/debian` and checked against the `Packages.xz` of bullseye, whose own hash is the one `InRelease` lists; the `InRelease` signature was checked with `gpgv` and the keyring file of `debian-archive-keyring` `2025.1ubuntu1` (unpacked, never installed): good signatures of the bullseye archive key, the bookworm archive key and the bullseye release key. *Why not the newest deb11uN:* the signed index of `bullseye-security` lists `openssh-client` `1:8.4p1-5+deb11u7` and `libssl1.1` `1.1.1w-0+deb11u8`, but bullseye is past its security support and those two files answer 404 on `security.debian.org` and `deb.debian.org`; the two versions of the point release (`11.11`) that the `deb.debian.org/debian` pool still serves are the ones above, the versions that the brief names. At each run the probe checks each file against its `SHA256SUMS` line, unpacks both with `dpkg-deb -x` into `<dir>/oldssh/root` and writes `<dir>/oldssh/bin/ssh` (`LD_LIBRARY_PATH=<root>/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu exec <root>/usr/bin/ssh "$@"`); the folder goes with the run's owned folder, nothing is installed. *What it showed:* P3.1, the wrapper `-V` ended with 0 and printed `OpenSSH_8.4p1 Debian-5+deb11u3, OpenSSL 1.1.1w  11 Sep 2023`. P3.2, `installShim` resolved the wrapper as the real client; the product's asker, through the shim, answered `{ kind: 'older', token: 'OpenSSH_8.4' }` and the shim recorded that start as `-V`. P3.3, the product's words for this client are five pairs and no `KnownHostsCommand`; a login through the shim with them and `true` ended with 0 and K1 logged one `Accepted publickey`. *P3.4, the lead's check (Q1), on OpenSSH 8.4p1:* `ssh -G -F /dev/null` with the words of `hostKeyWords(checkedPin(<dir>/data=prod/keys/known_hosts-e1, older))` and `127.0.0.1` ended with 0 and printed exactly `userknownhostsfile <dir>/data=prod/keys/known_hosts-e1` and `globalknownhostsfile <dir>/data=prod/keys/known_hosts-e1`: the quoted path, whole, as the sole value of each option. *P3.5 (recorded):* the same `-G` with the BARE words `UserKnownHostsFile=<path>` and `GlobalKnownHostsFile=<path>` ended with 0 and printed `userknownhostsfile <dir>/data prod/keys/known_hosts-e1` and `globalknownhostsfile <dir>/data prod/keys/known_hosts-e1`: 8.4 splits an unquoted value at `=`, as the docblock of `checkedPin` reads; this is the first time it was run. *Identities (P3.6):* the unpacked `usr/bin/ssh` sha256 `19269e22…7cc4` (`OpenSSH_8.4p1 Debian-5+deb11u3, OpenSSL 1.1.1w`), `libcrypto.so.1.1` `cde898ee…d20a`, `libssl.so.1.1` `f39abf7f…b5be`, the two `.deb` files as above; the wrapper's sha256 differs per run (it holds the owned folder's name); the `sshd` as in the common identities above; commit `cd5088d9`, `trackedChanges` `""`.
- **P2 · a resolver that the run owns, whose answer the client takes as secure (M10); `scripts/itestProbeResolver.cjs` (`probeResolver`: the host part and the part that runs inside), `scripts/itestResolverServer.cjs` (the resolver), `scripts/containers/e3-resolver.Containerfile` and `scripts/containers/e3-resolver-init.sh`.** `resolver`: worked, with the repaired set-up (seen when it was probed, 2026-11-24, commit `e6cb6532`, tree clean; the host part 8 ok and 4 info, the part inside 21 ok and 7 info, 0 FAIL). *The command,* in `src_vs_code`, after `npm run compile` and after the image is built once (`wslc build -f scripts/containers/e3-resolver.Containerfile -t creds-itest-e3-resolver:20261124 scripts/containers`): `node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs scripts/itestFeasibility.cjs resolver`. It needs no `CREDS_ITEST_SSHD`: inside, the image's own `sshd` is the default of `lib/sshdRun.cjs`. *Containers here are `wslc`'s, never Docker's, and the janitor does not read `wslc`:* the container has `--rm`, the owner-form name (`testsToDelete-u<uid>-creds-e3-resolver-<id>`) and the label `testsToDelete.owner=<record>`, and the probe removes it by its exact name in a `finally` and in the scope's `after`. `wslc list -a --no-trunc` before and after the run showed the same three containers that were there before (not of this run); without `--no-trunc` `wslc` cuts a long name with an ellipsis. *The image:* `FROM docker.io/library/node:22-bookworm-slim@sha256:66cb8ef7…ced4a5` (Node v22.23.5), `apt-get install --no-install-recommends openssh-client openssh-server procps`, `/run/sshd` made; id `sha256:2e760f27a6a018f6e995c19473e650e6498b811e742c6e642b943cf91d212c77`, 247 MB; `dpkg-query -W`: `openssh-client 1:9.2p1-2+deb12u10`, `openssh-server 1:9.2p1-2+deb12u10`, `procps 2:4.0.2-3`; `ssh -V` `OpenSSH_9.2p1 Debian-2+deb12u10, OpenSSL 3.0.22`; the image has no `curl`, no `wget` and no `git`. *Step 2.0* (the local `python:3.12-slim` image, nothing of Python run): the image is Debian 13 (trixie); the worktree mounts read-only with `-v <wslpath -w form>:/work:ro` (a write fails with `Read-only file system`); `--dns 127.0.0.1 --dns-option trust-ad --dns-option edns0` wrote `/etc/resolv.conf` as asked (`search localdomain`, `nameserver 127.0.0.1`, `options trust-ad edns0`). *What runs where:* the container's start (`e3-resolver-init.sh`, root) makes the user `probe`, starts the resolver as root on `127.0.0.1:53` (a Node `dgram` server that answers `A` and `SSHFP` for the name `k2.e3-probe.test` from a zone file, `NODATA` for the rest of that name, `NXDOMAIN` for other names, SETS the AD bit on a positive answer, echoes the EDNS0 record and records every question) and runs the harness as `probe`: the counterparts are `startServingSshd` as ever, the client is the image's `ssh`. *What it showed:* P2.1 (host), `wslc inspect` while the container ran answered the image id above, the name and the owner label, and after the probe `wslc list -a --no-trunc` had no container of that name. P2.2, `getent hosts k2.e3-probe.test` answered `127.0.0.1` and the resolver's record holds that `A` question. P2.3 (the control of M10: E1's words minus `VerifyHostKeyDNS=no` and `LogLevel=ERROR`, `-v`, a handed config with `VerifyHostKeyDNS yes`, the pin holding K1's line for `[k2.e3-probe.test]:<port>`, K2 answering): exit 0, K2 logged one `Accepted publickey`, stderr held `found 2 secure fingerprints in DNS` and `matching host key fingerprint found in DNS`, and the resolver was asked `A`, `AAAA` (answered with no data) and `SSHFP`. P2.4 (the opposite, `VerifyHostKeyDNS no`): exit 255 with the line `Host key verification failed.`, K2 logged no authentication attempt, and the resolver was asked `A` and `AAAA` only. P2.5 (the harness of the roads inside): `openLeg` and one click of *Install creds on host*, pinned to K1, K1 answering, reached its question (`confirmationFor`'s message from the probe's answer); the shim recorded one question `ssh -V` and one connection start whose last word is the probe command. *The set-up repair of P2.5:* the first run (commit `d9629402`) passed P2.1 to P2.4 and failed P2.5, because the image has no downloader and `blockerFor` answered `the host has neither curl nor wget`; the install's probe then asks no question. Commit `e6cb6532` gives P2.5's counterpart P1's stand-in (`installStandIn`: `SetEnv PATH=<dir>/standin/bin:…`, its `curl` is the downloader the probe's host answers; the install itself is not run), and lists with `--no-trunc`. *Identities (P2.6):* inside, `/usr/bin/ssh` sha256 `04f2ff5f…64e6`, the image's `sshd` `/usr/sbin/sshd` `OpenSSH_9.2, OpenSSL 3.0.22 25 Aug 2026` sha256 `9f6cdc78…716e` (all three listener programs the same), the resolver `itestResolverServer.cjs` sha256 `758eb63f…1810`, the stand-in `curl` (differs per run: it holds the owned folder's name), the handed configs and `/etc/resolv.conf` inside by sha256 in the `probe-record` line; R1 and R2 are the host's (`identities-host`: commit `e6cb6532b406e8df80a1c8171c049f25cd74ef76`, `trackedChanges` `""`, conventions `c598a11b…`, the pin of the shared rules), because the image has no `git`: inside, those two checks print as `info`. What P2 does NOT show: DNSSEC validation (the resolver SETS the AD bit; nothing validates a signature: the AD bit is what `ssh` reads under `options trust-ad`), and any run of the install to its end on the container's host.

**The first run of P2, as it was seen (commit `d9629402`, 2026-11-24; the record that the brief's §3.1 asks for both runs; added after the Opus review of E3.S3).** Inside the container: P2.0 ok, P2.2 ok, P2.3 ok (exit 0, one `Accepted publickey`, `found 2 secure fingerprints in DNS`, the questions A, AAAA, SSHFP), P2.4 ok (exit 255, `Host key verification failed.`, no authentication attempt; the questions A, AAAA only), "Install creds on host ended within 30 s" ok, the shim's record of `ssh -V` and of the probe ok; two checks FAILED: "the install's probe was answered: exit 0, and no blocker" (the probe answered exit 0 with `get=-`, so the product said "the host has neither curl nor wget, so it cannot fetch the installer.") and "Install creds on host reached its question" (no question). On the host: P2.1 ok; the last line `probe resolver: FAILED — the inside part ran to its end …`. The cause was the probe's own set-up: the image has no downloader, so the product stopped as it must. The repair (`e6cb6532`) hands the counterpart P1's stand-in through its extra config lines (`SetEnv PATH`); the pass line of P2.5 did not change.

**What none of them proves.** These tests show what each run is SENT, and which sentence the roads show for a text that `ssh` printed once, by hand: the texts are those of
OpenSSH 10.2p1 (and one of OpenSSH_for_Windows 9.5p2), printed against public `sshd`s with no login. That a real `ssh` prints the `Offending` line with the pin file of a call
THROUGH these roads is shown by leg 2 of the smoke (section 10 of `scripts/ssh-agent-itest.cjs`, E2.S4's test 27), which E2.S4 ran on 2026-11-19: 48 ok, locally, on OpenSSH
10.2p1; CI is its second run. E3's M5 and M7 add the cases that the smoke leaves out. A hop on the roads, a remote command on the roads, and a login on Windows are E3's or not
measured. The smoke does not prove a quoted path, a quiet config, a hop, a remote text, a client below 8.5, DNS or a `KnownHostsCommand` on the roads: those are E3's M5, M7,
M8, M10 and M11. That a real `ssh` refuses a host with another key through these roads is shown by the smoke of E1.S4 (the same section 10) and by E2; the runner and the
spawner are stand-ins in the unit suites. What a real `sshd` does with the words of `hostKeyWords` was measured by hand (the docblock of the function) and is asked of one
`sshd` on the loopback by the smoke, not by a unit test. Windows: `ssh.exe` of OpenSSH_for_Windows 9.5p2 was measured, once, for the text of a refusal (13 lines, the same
`Offending` line, every backslash of the path as two) and for what `ssh.exe -G` resolves (the quoted word as one file); no login was made there, no path with a drive letter
was run, and the smoke is Linux only. A pin path of about 250 bytes or more outside printable ASCII: `ssh` cuts its own log line at 1024 bytes, and a byte outside printable ASCII is printed as four characters, so a pin path of about 250 such bytes or more loses the `:<n>` that ends its `Offending` line: the line then names no file, and a real key change shows sentence D, not C (a miss only; reported by the review of E2.S3, not run).
A pin file whose write fails part way: the call is refused and the file, if one was made, stays until the purge. The pinned calls ask `ssh` for `VerifyHostKeyDNS=no` and, for OpenSSH 8.5 or newer, `KnownHostsCommand=none` (E2.S2): requests, which `ssh -G` shows `ssh` takes; that a login on these roads then ignores a config's DNS check or known-hosts command is not measured here (E3's M8 and M10). The line of a Connect terminal does not say these words and does not switch them off (C4). The smoke runs one user on the loopback of one Linux machine, with a key named by its path:
not the real resolve, a password entry, a stored key, the install's second run (E2's M1), the system-wide known-hosts file of P1 (E2's M2), a live master (M3), or Windows
and macOS. A run ended by SIGTERM, SIGHUP, SIGINT or an uncaught error in the middle of a leg ends the leg's bridge `ssh`, the listener and what it forked, and removes the
forwarded socket, through the section's scope (measured: SIGTERM sent once the socket appeared in leg 1, afterwards every pid the run had started was gone); a run killed with
SIGKILL still leaves its listener, what it forked, the bridge's `ssh` and one forwarded socket in `/tmp` (the product names it there, outside the owned folder).
No test of the suite ends a real `ssh` with exit code 0 while its text holds the verification line: X9 to X14 answer through the stand-in of the runner. That a real client's run of this kind goes on through the two roads is E3's M5 (h), with (g) of the same run as its control (the same text with exit code 255). The smoke (section 10) has no such leg: its leg 1 ends with 0 and prints no such line, its leg 2 ends with 255.

## What none of them covers

Named rather than implied, because the rule asks for exactly this.

- **The editor's own UI.** Nothing drives VS Code itself — no extension-host test, no click on a
  tree row, no form filled in. Every harness stubs `vscode` or talks to the broker underneath it.
  So a command registered but never wired to a menu, a context value that stops matching, or a
  webview that throws on open, is caught by unit tests over the pure halves and by nothing else.

  **What the viewer's woven-row flow has instead**, named here because the gap above is why it needs
  naming. The Show → reading → Copy path spans a panel that imports `vscode`, two hosts that do not,
  and a page script, and no harness can drive it end to end. It is covered in three places, and the
  split is deliberate: the arithmetic and the row order in `rowFlip.test.ts` and
  `wovenPicture.test.ts`; what each host answers, and that a Copy follows the order the rows were
  shown in — including when a render lands mid-await — in `paymentViewHost.test.ts` and
  `wovenPasswordForm.test.ts`; and the WIRING in `entityViewPanelWiring.test.ts`, which asserts over
  the panel's own source that one store is constructed, handed to both hosts, and cleared at both
  sites. **The limit is exact**: that wiring is read, not executed, so a panel that constructs
  everything correctly and still threw on open would pass it. That is the same limit as the rest of
  this bullet. It exists because this feature once shipped ten modules reachable from nothing but
  their own tests — a test that asserts the CALL is the answer to that — and every scan in it
  carries a companion proving the pattern still matches a known instance.
- **Open Site in Browser (issue #104).** No harness clicks the menu item or the viewer's button, and
  none lets a real VS Code hand the address to a real browser. What stands in: the judge
  (`siteUrl.test.ts`), the PIN-gated command path and the one-door scan (`openSite.test.ts`), the
  REAL viewer panel's message loop under the stub (`viewerOpenSite.test.ts`, which goes red if the
  open branch falls below the copy-only return), and the flag walk (`siteUrlFlag.test.ts`). **The
  limit is exact**: what `vscode.Uri.parse` and `openExternal` finally give the OS is not observed —
  VS Code re-serializes a `Uri`, so a percent-encoded `%26`, `%2F` or `%2B` inside a query value may
  not reach the browser as stored. The judge keeps the query as stored; the editor's last step is
  unverified.
- **The stale WSL MCP install check (2026-10-10, E4.S2 of
  [PLAN_wsl_bridge_outlives_its_client.md](PLAN_wsl_bridge_outlives_its_client.md) §5.8).** No
  harness clicks *Install… → Check the WSL MCP install* or answers its dialog. What stands in: the verdict
  over E2's real `--version` format, the probe's argv and its refusals (`wslMcpStale.test.ts`); when it runs
  — never a stopped distribution, once a day, the dismissal, nothing spawned with nothing recorded, an
  object-property distribution name (`wslMcpCheck.test.ts`); the bounded run's timeout-versus-exit, its
  UTF-16 listing, a failed listing told apart from an empty one, and its output cap (`wslProcess.test.ts`); the panel button being the palette's one id
  (`wslMcpCheckButton.test.ts`); and the window's half — the command, each click, Update's new block and
  record — in-process under the `vscode` stub with `wsl.exe` stood in for (`wslMcpCheckHost.test.ts`). It was run once by hand, read-only, against the real bridge on a Windows
  machine with two running distributions — the argv crossed `wsl.exe` and both pre-0.10.0 binaries read as
  `older`. **The limit is exact**: a `current` verdict from a real 0.10.0 install has not been observed (no
  such release existed yet), and the real editor's dialogs and a real `wsl.exe` under them are unexercised. The automated leg belongs in
  `creds-mcp-wsl-itest.cjs`, which E5.S1 rewrote with run-unique directories on 2026-10-11 — the stale-install leg
  itself is still not in it (open tail).
- **The pinned search (2026-11-18).** The title-bar magnifier, *Clear Filter* gated on
  `credSshManager.searchActive`, and the `TreeView.message` banner are UI-only; no harness drives a
  real workbench, so none shows a title-bar button appearing, the banner line above the tree, or the
  green icon. What stands in: the stub suites `searchPinned.test.ts` (what is sent to the context key
  and the banner, and when) and `searchBox.test.ts` (the box's Escape, Enter, reuse and
  outside-change rules). **The limit is exact**: the rendered look — placement, green, banner text in
  a real window — is an owner hand check. It was done once, on 2026-11-23, in the owner's window on a
  test VSIX built from `main` at 3a6afd22: all five checks passed, the scroll check included
  (`research/PLAN_pinned_green_search.md` §8). Nothing repeats it on a later change.
- **The Marketplace artefact.** `npm run package` runs in CI, and nothing installs the resulting
  `.vsix` into a real editor and opens it. The publish step is verified by reading the release run.
- **The server and the extension end to end.** `server-transport-itest.cjs` would do it, and it is
  the one harness CI does not run. Today the two halves are verified separately: the extension
  against a stubbed transport, the server against `http/`'s requests — 181 of them, 390 checks, over
  46 of 46 registered routes, as of 2026-09-07.
- **The sync merge under real concurrency.** Version-vector merging has thorough unit tests; no
  harness runs two windows against one vault at the same time. That gap has a plan of its own —
  [PLAN_node_writes_are_last_write_wins.md](PLAN_node_writes_are_last_write_wins.md) — and
  it is where the last-write-wins defect lives.
- **Corporate recovery with three people.** Shamir splitting and the escrow wrap are unit-tested;
  the ceremony across three machines has never been run. It is a human task by nature:
  [ЗАДАЧА_проверка_корп_восстановления.md](../todo/ЗАДАЧА_проверка_корп_восстановления.md).
- **macOS and Linux.** Every harness here runs on Windows, and four of them are Windows-only by
  construction (named pipes, WSL). The Linux keychain fallback the README warns about is asserted by
  unit tests and has no scenario coverage at all.

## How to run everything

Locally, every line below runs at most 8 workers at lowered CPU priority — see *Local runs* just after.

```bash
LP=.agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs

# .NET — 1,318 tests on 2026-10-11. The solution carries three of the five test projects, so each of
# the five is built BY NAME before it runs (a project the solution did not build has no executable).
# The executables run ONE AFTER ANOTHER: each gets 8 threads, and two at once on Windows meet F29.
# They need the shared-rules mount (git submodule update --init .agents/conventions) and node 20.19+/22.12+.
node $LP dotnet build src_minimalapi_server/tests/CredVaultServer.Tests.csproj -m:4 --disable-build-servers
node $LP dotnet build src_cli/tests/CredsCli.Tests.csproj -m:4 --disable-build-servers
node $LP dotnet build src_mcp/tests/CredsMcp.Tests.csproj -m:4 --disable-build-servers
node $LP dotnet build src_broker_client/tests/BrokerClient.Tests.csproj -m:4 --disable-build-servers
node $LP dotnet build src_service_defaults/tests/CredsForDevs.ServiceDefaults.Tests.csproj -m:4 --disable-build-servers
node $LP ./src_service_defaults/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredsForDevs.ServiceDefaults.Tests.exe --filter-namespace "CredsForDevs.Tests.Leftovers"
node $LP ./src_minimalapi_server/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredVaultServer.Tests.exe
node $LP ./src_cli/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredsCli.Tests.exe
node $LP ./src_mcp/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredsMcp.Tests.exe
node $LP ./src_broker_client/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredsBroker.Tests.exe
node $LP ./src_service_defaults/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredsForDevs.ServiceDefaults.Tests.exe
# the audit's teeth: this run leaks a folder on purpose and MUST fail with the audit's first line ("creds tests: this run left ...")
CREDS_LEFTOVERS_SELFTEST=1 node $LP ./src_cli/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredsCli.Tests.exe --filter-class "*LeftoversSelfTest"
# Linux and macOS (WSL too): the same lines without the .exe
node $LP ./src_service_defaults/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredsForDevs.ServiceDefaults.Tests --filter-namespace "CredsForDevs.Tests.Leftovers"
node $LP ./src_minimalapi_server/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredVaultServer.Tests
node $LP ./src_cli/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredsCli.Tests
node $LP ./src_mcp/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredsMcp.Tests
node $LP ./src_broker_client/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredsBroker.Tests
node $LP ./src_service_defaults/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredsForDevs.ServiceDefaults.Tests
CREDS_LEFTOVERS_SELFTEST=1 node $LP ./src_cli/tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/CredsCli.Tests --filter-class "*LeftoversSelfTest"

# the extension's unit suite — 5,612 on 2026-10-11; the script lowers itself and caps its pool at 8
cd src_vs_code && npm test

# every scenario harness except the one needing a live server — each script lowers itself
npm run itest:all
# a local run of the real sshd sections on an sshd that is not /usr/sbin/sshd: CREDS_ITEST_SSHD=<sshd or its wrapper> CREDS_ITEST_SSHD_CONFIG=<file of extra lines> npm run itest:ssh-agent
# (the G1 section, "The counterpart of the real runs")

# the HTTP contract suite (see http/README.md for the environment it needs, and its lowered form)
node $LP node .agents/conventions/tools/http-run.mjs --require-env VAULT_LOCAL_SIGNING_KEY --env local --target http://127.0.0.1:5099
```

## Local runs: at most 8 workers, at lowered CPU priority (2026-10-11)

The shared rule [local-cpu-budget.md](../.agents/conventions/common/local-cpu-budget.md) — measured on a
24-thread workstation where one plain `node --test` started 23 test-file processes at normal priority and
every other session lagged. How this repository follows it, entry point by entry point
([PLAN_local_cpu_budget_adoption.md](PLAN_local_cpu_budget_adoption.md)):

| Entry point | Workers | Lowered by |
|---|---|---|
| `npm test` | `scripts/run-tests.mjs` starts `node --test --test-concurrency=min(8, CPUs - 1)`: node's own default capped at 8 (23 here before, 8 now; 3 on a 4-vCPU runner, as before) | `node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs`; the `node --test` child inherits it |
| `npm run itest:*`, `itest:all` | one process, harnesses one after another | the same preload; every binary a harness starts inherits it |
| the five xUnit executables | 8 threads — ONE root `xunit.runner.json`, copied into each test project's output by `Directory.Build.props`, except when `CI=true` | `node $LP <exe>`, one executable at a time |
| `dotnet build` / `publish` named anywhere in these docs, prerequisites included | `-m:4` (`csharp/dotnet-build.md`) | `node $LP dotnet build … -m:4 --disable-build-servers` — see below |
| `deploy/restore-archive-itest.sh` | one bash process | re-runs itself once under `nice` (19; 10 under Git Bash, where 19 is the Windows Idle class the rule forbids) |
| `deploy/nginx/entrypoint.test.sh` | one bash process, `sh entrypoint.sh` once per case | the same self-re-run under `nice` as the harness above |
| the `.http` suite | httpyac, one request at a time | `node $LP node …/http-run.mjs` |
| a one-file run | one worker | `node --import ./scripts/lower-priority.mjs --test out/test/<file>.js` |

- **The preload** `src_vs_code/scripts/lower-priority.mjs` is a verbatim copy of the conventions template
  with only its `MOUNT` line changed to `../../.agents/conventions/`. It calls the shared tool's
  `lowerOwnPriority()`. Where the mount is absent — CI, which checks no submodule out for the extension
  job, or a clone without `git submodule update --init` — it lowers nothing, and outside CI it says so
  once. **No CI job changed.**
- **The xUnit runner reads the file — proved, not assumed.** A file on disk is not enough: a
  `xunit.runner.json` beside the `.csproj` but not copied to the output leaves the runner at its default.
  Each executable's `Starting:` line under `--xunit-diagnostics on`: before, `parallel mode = collections
  [24 threads]`; after, `[8 threads]` for all five; with the root file set to 5 and one project rebuilt,
  `[5 threads]` — the runner follows the root file. `--max-threads N` on the command line overrides it.
- **A lowered build disables the build servers.** Without `--disable-build-servers` a lowered `dotnet
  build` hands its compiling to a compiler server (`VBCSCompiler`) that an earlier, normal-priority build
  started — observed 2026-10-11: one was running at `Normal` while the lowered builds above ran. With the
  flag, the compiler runs inside the lowered tree. The shared rule names this for `nice`; it holds for
  Windows too. `dotnet run` takes the same flag.
- **Measured 2026-10-11, lowered, one after another:** ServiceDefaults 80 (6 s), CredsBroker 133 (3 s),
  CredsCli 142 (101 s), CredsMcp 154 (27 s), CredVaultServer 809 (89 s) — 0 failed; `npm test` 5,612 tests,
  0 failed, 379 s on a machine other sessions were also loading.
- **The pin:** `src/test/localCpuBudget.test.ts` asserts that the `test` script preloads the lowering module
  and starts `scripts/run-tests.mjs` with no exact `--test-concurrency`; the runner's cap (1, 1, 3, 8, 8 for
  1, 2, 4, 9 and 24 threads), its `CAP` equal to the pin's `MAX_WORKERS` (the same bound the xUnit case uses,
  so one budget governs both), and its exit codes (the child's own, 128 + the signal number, 1 when the spawn
  failed); the preload on every `itest:*` script, the preload's one `MOUNT` line and where it resolves, the
  preload against the template (skipped, saying so, where the mount is absent), and the root
  `xunit.runner.json` with the `Directory.Build.props` item that copies it to every test output, skipped
  when `CI=true`. A pin bump that changes the template turns it red until the copy follows.
- **Not lowered, on purpose:** `compile`, `typecheck`, `lint`, `bundle` and the generators are one process
  each and start no pool; the rule's own form `npm run compile && node --import … --test` leaves the
  compile unlowered. `deploy/acme-test/run.sh` does its work in containers the Docker daemon starts, which
  neither `nice` nor the tool reaches.
- **CI parallelism is never raised.** `--test-concurrency` and `maxParallelThreads` are exact values, and the
  first version (a bare 8 for both) raised a 4-vCPU hosted runner from 3 to 8 node workers and from 4 to 8
  xUnit threads. Since the follow-up of 2026-10-11 the runner computes `min(8, CPUs - 1)`, node's own default
  wherever that is at most 8, and the xUnit copy is skipped when `CI=true`, so CI keeps xUnit's default.
- **Open tail:** the two WSL harnesses build inside the distribution with a bare `dotnet build`; a
  Windows-side BelowNormal does not reach the WSL VM. Owed there: `nice -n 19 dotnet build -m:4
  --disable-build-servers`, once the branch rewriting those harnesses lands.

The WSL harnesses skip loudly rather than silently when the distribution, the .NET SDK inside it, or
the Windows binary is missing — each prints the command that would fix it. That is the convention
every harness here follows: a skip says why, and a missing prerequisite is never mistaken for a pass.
